# Evidence pack: Rotational diagrams and LTE column densities (`rotational-diagram-lte`)

Scope: LTE column densities, excitation temperatures, optical depth corrections

Built from 938 non-held-out ALMA papers whose methods cards passed mechanical evidence verification (every quote below is verbatim from the paper). Years 2012-2026.
Matcher (regex over analysis steps): `rotation(?:al)? diagram|population diagram|\blte\b|xclass|madcuba|cassis|weeds|excitation temperature|boltzmann (?:plot|diagram)|lte (?:column|model|fit|analysis|assumption)`
Tagging is by regex, so a few papers may be off-topic: ignore those.

### Validation checks used
- Compare continuum-peak and displaced spectra; continuum-peak lines are weaker.: 1
- Check transitions across the full windows; shorter burn-in did not alter best-fit values.: 1
- Isotope-scaled methanol agrees with high-excitation fit within factors 2–3; most residuals below 3σ; baseline shift inc…: 1
- Models match data below the 3σ level; abundance ratios acknowledged as uncertain.: 1
- Visual model comparison; fitted-line residuals within ±3σ: 1
- Visual comparison favors methanol constraints for strongest species; 18 profiles differ from synthetic profiles.: 1
- Residuals within 3σ except panel c of Figure F.4: 1
- Compare ratios across four positions; most agree within factor 3, while CH2DOH is enhanced at the original pixel.: 1
- at least three lines of main isotopologue for COMs: 1
- compared with single-dish Miettinen et al. 2006: 1
- Criteria of Herbst & van Dishoeck 2009 for line identification: 1
- Two outflow-tracing lines deviate from the trend and may be optically thick, motivating the opacity correction: 1
- Results unchanged with a different random seed and n_prod > 3000; consistent with the opacity-corrected rotation diagra…: 1
- Combined synthetic spectrum compared with observed wide-band spectra: 1
- Reran WEEDSPY_MCMC with an unresolved 0.2'' source size to test dependence on source size: 1
- Brightness temperatures compared with the line Eu/kB; optically thin LTE models (Weeds) compared with 25 GHz lines: 1
- Compared with 24 K from Carney et al. 2017: 1
- Channel maps of data, model and residuals; third-component test: 1
- spectral smearing tested with 0.4 vs 0.8 km/s channel cubes and found negligible: 1
- radially constant 12C/13C checked against the HCN-hyperfine carbon profile: 1
- in the fiducial range the hyperfine Gaussian width is consistent with a radially constant line width: 1
- Dozens of ground-state lines identified at both positions; excited-state lines also detected.: 1
- Only two relatively uncontaminated transitions, at 84306 and 112273 MHz; detection classed as tentative.: 1
- Synthetic upper-limit spectra shown against observations; no DMA evidence at either position.: 1
- Selected region appears uncontaminated; local baseline uncertainty changes the column limit by less than a factor of tw…: 1
- Compare measured HF ratios with theoretical ratios adapted from (cite: Punzi2015): 1
- A posteriori similar component excitation temperatures and modest A/B overlap: 1
- Comparison with previous N(H2) estimates: 1
- Simulated HCO spectra with random noise and channel resampling show weaker lines dilute below 2-3 sigma: 1
- Compared flux integral with earlier papers' 10.0 and 12.3 Jy km/s: 1
- Varied 12CO/13CO between 50 and 200 and HCO+ abundance; similar results: 1
- Line contamination checked with the ALCHEMI line identification and WEEDS (JPL and CDMS): 1
- Second component justified by shifts in FWHM/Vpeak with Eu and by emission becoming more compact with Eu: 1
- Initial fit with all lines gave chi2_r>5 (GMC 6, pSSC 5, pSSC 2) so low-Eu lines were dropped; population diagrams then…: 1
- Corrected diagrams still deviate from a straight line, with T_rot below the LVG minimum gas temperature, so non-LTE eff…: 1
- Parameters consistent within errors among all lines: 1
- Model PV diagrams visually compared to observed ones: 1
- Could not obtain consistent Tex from CO(3-2)/CO(2-1), suggesting non-LTE: 1
- Tested different initial p and q; residuals mostly <=3 sigma: 1
- Visual comparison of model and observed P-V diagrams: 1

### Limitations the authors admit
- 2025A&A...696A..18C: Fit uncertainties are statistical and underestimated
- 2025A&A...696A..18C: Baseline placement changes column densities by factors 1.2–1.8 in L1551 IRS 5 North
- 2025A&A...696A..18C: Haro 5a IRS source size and column density are degenerate
- 2025A&A...696A..18C: L1551 IRS 5 South methanol velocity differs from other COMs, making abundance ratios uncertain
- 2025A&A...696A..18C: V346 Nor profile discrepancies cannot be diagnosed at the available resolution
- 2025A&A...696A..18C: V346 Nor D/H is highly uncertain because of methanol opacity and poor CH2DOH modeling
- 2025A&A...696A..18C: Alternative-pixel abundance tests are infeasible for the other targets
- 2025A&A...696A..18C: Comparison sample too small for firm conclusions
- 2018ApJS..236...45G: Lack of short spacings filters extended emission; modest spectral resolution
- 2023MNRAS.525.6146W: 25 GHz CH3OH maser/thermal result is tentative because of low S/N
- 2023MNRAS.525.6146W: Abundances of species other than CH3OH are only order-of-magnitude estimates
- 2023MNRAS.525.6146W: Disc mass may be underestimated because temperature, opacity and optical depth variations are ignored
- 2023MNRAS.525.6146W: Optical depth effects cannot be excluded as a contributor to the low 12C/13C ratio
- 2023MNRAS.525.6146W: Column density comparisons across the literature depend on beam size, distance and beam dilution
- 2018ApJ...864..170G: o/p is a disk-averaged value; vertical distributions cannot be distinguished by SMA data
- 2025A&A...698A.115R: Hyperfine fit limited by half-covered HCN (4-3), 0.9 km/s resolution and low S/N, giving large errors and no robust ratios inside ~40 au or beyond ~100 au
- 2025A&A...698A.115R: Profiles inside ~25 au are highly uncertain
- 2025A&A...698A.115R: Nitrogen and carbon fractionation cannot be robustly separated, making the interpretation speculative
- 2023MNRAS.523.2887M: ALMA limits do not constrain whether the analogous formation routes operate differently.
- 2023MNRAS.523.2887M: Methylamine toward N2b is only tentatively detected.
- 2023MNRAS.523.2887M: G+0.693 DMA emission falls at a local baseline minimum; its upper limit can change by less than a factor of two.
- 2023MNRAS.523.2887M: DMA chemical production rates are poorly constrained.
- 2023MNRAS.523.2887M: The dominant early formation mechanism depends on uncertain low initial visual extinction.
- 2023MNRAS.523.2887M: The G+0.693 models omit shock chemistry and shock-induced mantle release.
- 2023MNRAS.523.2887M: Neglected internal rotation may matter near 100 GHz for linewidths below 2 km s−1.
- 2021A&A...646A..59R: Characteristic emission heights should not be equated with physical disk scale heights; opacity affects interpretation.
- 2021A&A...646A..59R: Continuum, CS and CN vertical profiles are marginally resolved.
- 2021A&A...646A..59R: CO is contaminated by background clouds near ~1.8 and 6–7 km s^-1.
- 2021A&A...646A..59R: Height uncertainties include only formal Gaussian-fit errors.
- 2021A&A...646A..59R: RADEX does not treat line overlaps; modeling is restricted to resolved HF regions.
- 2021A&A...646A..59R: Temperature-density degeneracy and difficult-to-quantify temperature systematics.
- 2021A&A...646A..59R: East-west thermalization differences may not reflect a real physical asymmetry.
- 2021A&A...646A..59R: Very low inferred temperatures pose a gas-phase depletion problem for CN and CS.
- 2021A&A...646A..59R: Higher-resolution, multiple-transition CN observations are needed to confirm cold temperatures and distinct production layers.
- 2019MNRAS.483..806R: CH3OH and H2CO column densities are lower limits because lines are optically thick
- 2019MNRAS.483..806R: HCO Tex could not be determined since all four lines share Eup of 4 K
- 2017A&A...608A..38O: Separation of outflowing and regular gas is uncertain, so masses are likely lower limits
- 2017A&A...608A..38O: Single-component model not appropriate for mixed gas; Tkin-n degeneracy
- 2025A&A...698A.261B: Spectral resolution (10 km/s) is insufficient to separate blended E/A states and limits identification of gas components
- 2025A&A...698A.261B: Derived T_rot are not true gas temperatures because rotational diagrams deviate from LTE fits
- 2025A&A...698A.261B: Two-component treatment assumes the warm compact component contributes negligibly to the cold extended one
- 2025A&A...698A.261B: Abundance study not performed (column densities only)
- 2018MNRAS.480.4991G: Mass and mass-loss estimates are very uncertain; distance is a single uncertain literature estimate
- 2018MNRAS.480.4991G: Torus model too simple for close reproduction; no detailed fit
- 2014ApJ...793....1Y: Missing flux (~80% of C18O) hinders outer disk radius; p, q, T0, vturb poorly constrained
- 2020MNRAS.496.5308S: Only 3-4 transitions per sub-region limit modelling reliability.
- 2020MNRAS.496.5308S: Rotational diagrams cannot reliably constrain conditions or distinguish two phases from sub-thermal excitation.
- 2020MNRAS.496.5308S: RADEX temperatures have unconstrained upper bounds.
- 2020MNRAS.496.5308S: 2.73 K background may be inappropriate at the AGN.
- 2020MNRAS.496.5308S: Coarse chemical grid makes tabulated values approximate.
- 2020MNRAS.496.5308S: Sulphur depletion not investigated.
- 2020MNRAS.496.5308S: Cause of Boltzmann/rotational column-density discrepancy remains uncertain.
- 2020MNRAS.496.5308S: CS(6-5) cannot cover the SB ring; CS(3-2) SNR drops outside the ring.
- 2015ApJ...798...85P: Gas mass is a crude estimate, dominated by assumed isotopic ratios and temperature
- 2019ApJ...878..113H: Single CO transition cannot break mass-temperature degeneracy
- 2024A&A...681A.117P: The CO abundance in molecular outflows is not established and is a major uncertainty for alpha_CO
- 2024A&A...681A.117P: 13CO-based alpha_CO is more uncertain because it depends on 12CO and 13CO abundances in the outflow
- 2019A&A...623A..68T: Field of view limited by background continuum and finite resolution; ACA combination would help
- 2019A&A...623A..68T: PDF width likely underestimated due to limited field of view
- 2021MNRAS.500.5500B: Large angular distance (~4 deg) to the ALMA TP off position caused spectral ripples that were not removed in post-processing.
- 2021MNRAS.500.5500B: Fainter arcs of the ring are not detected in 13CO J=2-1 due to limited sensitivity.
- 2021MNRAS.500.5500B: ACA is likely missing a fraction of the extended 225 GHz radio continuum flux because its largest angular scale is ~1/3 that of the ATCA 750B configuration at 5.5 GHz.
- 2021MNRAS.500.5500B: Moderate velocity resolution (1.3 km s^-1, sampling the ring across only seven channels) and contamination in the 28.2 and 29.5 km s^-1 channels limit detailed kinematic analysis and cause significan…
- 2021MNRAS.500.5500B: Excitation analysis is restricted to single-dish data (providing only average values affected by beam dilution) due to the lack of interferometric CO J=3-2 observations, and RADEX assumes a homogeneo…
- 2021MNRAS.500.5500B: The molecular ring mass estimate is strongly affected by uncertainties in the X_CO abundance factor, which could be lower in C- and O-deficient LBV nebulae.
- 2021MNRAS.500.5500B: The kinematic duration and average mass-loss rate of the ring are loose limits because the ring width is unresolved (conservatively set to the 7 arcsec ACA beam) and constant expansion velocity with …
- 2024A&A...684A...4U: Gaussian fits to AARPs of centrally-peaked species in IRAS 15194-5115 and IRAS 15082-4808 underestimate the true outer radii because the brightness profiles peak sharply at the centre and fall off gr…
- 2024A&A...684A...4U: Interferometric filtering out of extended emission (negative bowls at large radii) affects SiC2, C4H, HC5N, CN, and CO due to the lack of very short baselines / single-dish total-power combination.
- 2024A&A...684A...4U: Population diagrams for centrally-peaked species (except SiS) yield unrealistically low rotational temperatures (10-20 K) due to limited upper-energy coverage, radially varying excitation conditions,…
- 2024A&A...684A...4U: Abundance calculations assume LTE and a smooth, isotropic, constant mass-loss wind (r^-2 density), neglecting non-LTE radiative transfer effects and the observed arc/shell density enhancements; overa…
- 2024A&A...684A...4U: Lower spatial resolution towards IRAS 07454-7112 prevents resolving inner CSE central components or arc substructures.
- 2014A&A...570A..28V: RADEX assumes all transitions sample the same gas, and several ratios use degraded or low-resolution data
- 2014A&A...570A..28V: Chemical modelling is preliminary and compares time-dependent models only at one time step
- 2014A&A...570A..28V: Infrared pumping not considered
- 2020ApJ...905..122H: H2 density is not independently constrained by the fits.
- 2020ApJ...905..122H: High optical depths can violate the simple LVG approximation; more rigorous radiative transfer including dust opacity is deferred.
- 2020ApJ...905..122H: State-to-state collision coefficients for CO, C and C+ are unavailable, preventing explicit treatment of these collisions.
- 2020ApJ...905..122H: HD 21997 low-density fits poorly constrain temperature and column density, especially for the blue component.
- 2020ApJ...905..122H: Temperature and filling factor are highly correlated.
- 2020ApJ...905..122H: The faint 49 Ceti C18O line has poorer data quality than the other lines.
- 2020ApJ...905..122H: The available isotopologue resolution does not permit direct radial temperature profiles.
- 2020ApJ...905..122H: Herschel resolution differs substantially, limiting assessment of the C+ contribution.
- 2024ApJ...969..102N: Archival calibrator data have poorer angular resolution, frequency resolution and sensitivity than the dedicated CO data
- 2024ApJ...969..102N: Some components heavily contaminated by other lines were excluded for CN, HCN and C2H
- 2024ApJ...969..102N: Narrower line components may exist but SNR prevents identification
- 2024ApJ...969..102N: H2CO only marginally detected because of limited sensitivity
- 2024ApJ...969..102N: Atomic carbon results limited by archive SNR
- 2024ApJ...969..102N: N(H2) from 13CO emission in diffuse gas is highly uncertain and often severely underestimated
- 2025ApJ...989...83B: Bandpass calibration effects on tau_0 and tau_CO are not quantified
- 2025ApJ...989...83B: CO(3-2) and CO(2-1) opacities are less reliable than CO(1-0), so LTE reconciliation is not attempted
- 2025ApJ...989...83B: Xa1 maximum recoverable scale cannot entirely rule out diffuse emission over larger regions
- 2026A&A...705A.201B: Keplerian fit uncertainties likely underestimated
- 2026A&A...705A.201B: Mu and 13CO mass depend on the LTE assumption and radial and vertical model choices
- 2026A&A...705A.201B: Different total CO masses or abundance ratios may fit equally well given high optical depth
- 2026A&A...705A.201B: Azimuthal averaging smooths azimuthal variation in line profile
- 2022A&A...667A.131B: Not all sub-regions have been observed at all spatial resolutions for all molecules; NSB-b lacks consistent resolution observations and NSB-c has only a single high-resolution ratio (with HCN(4-3) an…
- 2022A&A...667A.131B: Gas kinetic temperature, gas density (except in E Knot, W Knot, and NSB-a at 40 pc), and linewidth are poorly constrained by the MCMC RADEX fitting across resolutions.
- 2022A&A...667A.131B: Follow-up studies of other nearby galaxies are required to confirm whether HCN(4-3)/HCO+(4-3) and HCN(4-3)/CS(2-1) hold as reliable diagnostics beyond NGC 1068.
- 2014Sci...345.1584B: Preliminary LTE model not fully optimized for ALMA; abundance uncertainties exclude free-free contamination and dust property uncertainties
- 2021ApJ...921...97J: CO(3-2) from ATCA is spatially unresolved (5-6'') between W and E and had to be distributed by scaling to CO(6-5).
- 2021ApJ...921...97J: Signal-to-noise is insufficient for lensing reconstruction in E and for p-H2O(2_1,1-2_0,2) in W, requiring adoption of fixed magnification factors from Marrone et al. (2018).
- 2021ApJ...921...97J: [CI](2-1) and p-H2O(2_1,1-2_0,2) are not significantly detected in E, and the lack of [CI](1-0) observations prevents direct measurement of the [CI] excitation temperature and confirmation of the [CI…
- 2021ApJ...921...97J: In the LVG models, T_kin/T_dust is assumed constant across all visual extinctions/densities rather than varying with extinction or density.
- 2021ApJ...921...97J: Observations of important neutral gas coolants [OI] (63 um) and [SiII] (35 um), which contribute >50% of total cooling, are unavailable.
- 2017ApJ...837..177C: Elliptical beam and filtering limit interpretation; June 29 Trot profile hard to fit
- 2023A&A...671A..69G: Degrading the simulation by Gaussian beam convolution assumes perfect spatial Fourier space sampling rather than ALMA's true sparse uv coverage.
- 2023A&A...671A..69G: The feature tracking algorithm has a ~60% detection success rate when features overlap and systematically underestimates feature sizes by ~20%, which can affect radial velocity and energy flux estima…
- 2023A&A...671A..69G: ALMA Band 3 and Band 6 observations were not simultaneous and lack direct wave propagation measurements between the two bands.
- 2023A&A...671A..69G: Non-unity filling factors can over- or under-estimate energy flux, and external mass densities cannot be measured from ALMA data so simulation densities had to be used.
- 2023A&A...671A..69G: Standard photospheric cylinder models used to infer sausage wave modes from phase relations assume density and structural uniformity that may not hold in the chromosphere.
- 2025ApJ...987..158F: Source companions are unresolved in AS2COS0001.1 and AS2COS0008.1, so [CI] fluxes are likely overestimated
- 2025ApJ...987..158F: Conversion factor constraints are limited to very active sources and need samples across masses and metallicities
- 2025ApJ...987..158F: Absolute CMB effect on individual lines cannot be determined without well-sampled SLEDs and non-LTE modelling
- 2020MNRAS.499.2493A: Lower flux than in 2012 may be due to emission outside the maximum recoverable scale
- 2020MNRAS.499.2493A: Unclear whether the narrow line in the compact-source spectrum belongs to the compact source
- 2020MNRAS.499.2493A: Blob proper motions depend on identification of Weigelt blobs with ALMA sources
- 2015RAA....15.2213A: Imperfect deconvolution/calibration and crude symmetry and optically thin assumptions
- 2026ApJ...999L..32R: Low signal-to-noise on the longest ACA baselines prevented definitively ruling out CH3OH as purely a parent species.
- 2026ApJ...999L..32R: Low spectral resolution (0.87 km s^-1) on Aug 28, Sept 18 (low-res), Sept 22, and Oct 1 was comparable to the line FWHM, preventing direct retrieval of kinematics or L_p on those dates.
- 2026ApJ...999L..32R: Absence of contemporaneous Q(H2O) measurements required scaling Q(OH) measured at r_H = 1.43 au assuming r_H^-2 insolation-driven dependence, and CH3OH/HCN and CH3OH/H2O ratios relied on interpolatin…

## Paper excerpts (40 of 938, chosen for detail and spread over fields/years)

### 2025A&A...696A..18C: The hot corino-like chemistry of four FUor-like protostars (2025; star_formation, astrochemistry, protoplanetary_disks; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Investigate the chemical composition of four FUor-like Class I YSOs and compare their COM abundances with other YSOs.
- step 2: Extract spectra and identify molecules
  - method: Search individual pixels for strongest line emission in both windows; extract off-center spectra; identify lines with CASSIS and molecular databases. [Spectra: "We chose these positions by extracting spectra using a pixel-size aperture placed at multiple positions throughout the disks, and finding the pixels where the line emission was stronger in both spectral windows."] [Spectra: "For the line identification we used the CASSIS[^1] software"]
  - software: CASSIS, CDMS, JPL molecular database, Lille Spectroscopic Database
  - parameter: Spectral extraction coordinates: N 04:31:34.1575 +18:08:04.793; S 04:31:34.1669 +18:08:04.253; Haro 05:35:26.5616 -05:03:55.064; V346 16:32:32.1951 -44:55:30.769; OO 18:29:49.1535 +01:16:19.646. [Spectra: "04:31:34.1575 $+$18:08:04.793"] [Spectra: "04:31:34.1669 $+$18:08:04.253"]
  - parameter: CDMS for most species; JPL for CHD2OH, CH3COCH3 and CH3OCHO as written; LSD for CH3CHO. [Spectra: "We used CDMS for most of the species."] [Spectra: "For CHD$_2$OH, CH$_3$COCH$_3$, and CH$_3$OCHO, we utilized the JPL database, and for CH$_3$CHO we used LSD."]
  - validation: Compare continuum-peak and displaced spectra; continuum-peak lines are weaker. [Spectra: "We found weaker line emission in the spectra extracted on the pixel closest to the protostar."]
- step 4: Fit LTE synthetic spectra
  - method: CASSIS MCMC synthetic spectra fit N, Tex, vLSR, FWHM and source size using selected unblended lines with sufficient SNR. [Analysis: "We used the Markov chain Monte Carlo (MCMC) approach provided in CASSIS for our analysis."] [Analysis: "Each synthetic spectrum is created using a combination of column density ($N$), excitation temperature ($T_\mathrm{ex}$), systemic velocity ($v_\mathrm{LSR}$), FWHM of the line, and source size ($s$)."]
  - software: CASSIS
  - parameter: N: 10^12–10^19 cm−2; Tex: 10–300 K; FWHM: 1–10 km s−1; s: 0.01–1.0 arcsec [L1551 IRS 5 North: "10$^{12}$ cm$^{-2}\leq N \leq 10^{19}$ cm$^{-2}$, 10 K $\leq T_\mathrm{ex} \leq$ 300 K"] [L1551 IRS 5 North: "1 km s$^{-1}$$\leq \mathrm{FWHM} \leq$ 10 km s$^{-1}$, and 0.01$''$$\leq s \leq$ 1.0$''$."]
  - parameter: N, Tex and s logarithmic; vLSR and FWHM linear [L1551 IRS 5 North: "$N$, $T_\mathrm{ex}$ and $s$ were explored in logarithmic scale, while linear scales were used for $v_\mathrm{LSR}$ and $\mathrm{FWHM}$."]
  - parameter: 50 000 iterations; first 45 000 discarded; uncertainties from 0.16, 0.50 and 0.84 percentiles as written [L1551 IRS 5 North: "We ran the MCMC for 50 000 iterations and dropped the first 45 000 as a burn-in phase."] [L1551 IRS 5 North: "Afterwards we determined the best-fit parameters and their uncertainties using the 0.16, 0.50, and 0.84 percentiles."]
  - assumption: LTE; other targets have densities comparable to L1551 IRS 5 [Analysis: "Thus, we assume that the other three targets have densities comparable to L1551 IRS 5 so that the LTE approximation is also valid for them."]
  - fitting: Minimum χ²; errors include spectral-window rms and nominal ALMA flux calibration uncertainty; explicit priors not stated. [Analysis: "This consists in generating synthetic spectra then comparing them to the observed data, and finding the set of parameters which results in the minimum $\chi^2$."] [Analysis: "The uncertainties we consider for this $\chi^2$ are the rms of each spectral window (see [1](#tab:observations)) and the nominal flux calibration uncertainty of ALMA (cite: ALMA_Cycle4)."]
  - validation: Check transitions across the full windows; shorter burn-in did not alter best-fit values. [Analysis: "Using this methodology instead of a rotational diagram approach allows us to verify that all the transitions in the full spectral window (not just those used in the fit) match the observational data."] [L1551 IRS 5 North: "We found that shortening the burn-in phase did not cause changes in the values of the best-fit parameters."]
- step 5: Resolve opacity and baseline effects in L1551 IRS 5 North
  - method: Fit all species freely, check blending with a combined reference spectrum, refit methanol with high-excitation lines and CH2DOH with low-opacity lines, then refit N with other parameters fixed to species medians. [L1551 IRS 5 North: "After obtaining a first version of the best-fit spectrum for all species, we incorporated it into a “reference” spectrum, which was used to double-check that the lines used in the fitting process were not blended."] [L1551 IRS 5 North: "we ran our fitting again using only lines with $E_\mathrm{up}$ $\gtrsim$ 657 K."]
  - software: CASSIS
  - parameter: Initial vLSR range: 7.0–12.5 km s−1 [L1551 IRS 5 North: "7.0 km s$^{-1}$$\leq v_\mathrm{LSR} \leq$ 12.5 km s$^{-1}$"]
  - parameter: CH2DOH: upper Ka < 10, present in JPL and Oyama list, J < 26; retain lowest-opacity transitions [L1551 IRS 5 North: "only included the lines with $K_a$ $<$ 10 in the upper transition."] [L1551 IRS 5 North: "we only kept the CH$_2$DOH transitions that were present in the JPL database and in their list, and those with $J$ $<$ 26."]
  - parameter: Fixed-parameter isotope test: 10 000 iterations, first 5 000 discarded; exclude optically thick CH3OH and CH2DOH fits from medians. [L1551 IRS 5 North: "When determining the medians, we excluded the results of CH$_3$OH (and CH$_2$DOH) that were obtained using optically thick lines."] [L1551 IRS 5 North: "Here we ran the MCMC for 10 000 iterations and dropped the first 5 000 as a burn-in phase."]
  - assumption: Methanol isotopologues and other COMs arise from the same region [L1551 IRS 5 North: "assuming all the CH$_3$OH isotopologues and the COMs come from the same region"]
  - assumption: Isotopic ratios: 12C/13C = 68; 16O/18O = 560 [L1551 IRS 5 North: "[$^{12}$C]/[$^{13}$C] = 68 (cite: Milam2005_ApJ6341126M)"] [L1551 IRS 5 North: "[$^{16}$O]/[$^{18}$O] = 560 (cite: WilsonRood1994_ARA&A32191W)."]
  - fitting: N alone free in median-constrained fits; baseline test adds 1 rms and refits N and FWHM with s, vLSR and Tex fixed. [L1551 IRS 5 North: "which left the column density as the only free parameter."] [L1551 IRS 5 North: "We assumed that our continuum level was overestimated by 1 *rms* so we added 1 *rms* to the spectrum and performed a new set of fits."]
  - validation: Isotope-scaled methanol agrees with high-excitation fit within factors 2–3; most residuals below 3σ; baseline shift increases N by factors 1.2–1.8. [L1551 IRS 5 North: "These are in reasonable agreement with the fit of the high-excitation lines ($1.60 \times 10^{19}$ cm$^{-2}$) by factors of 2–3"] [L1551 IRS 5 North: "For the majority of the lines, the differences between the data (black line) and the total model (green line) are below the 3$\sigma$ level (shaded area)."]
- step 6: Fit L1551 IRS 5 South
  - method: Fix source size; first fit N, Tex, vLSR and FWHM; refit non-methanol COMs using median Tex, vLSR and FWHM. [L1551 IRS 5 South: "The first set of MCMC runs were with $N$, $T_\mathrm{ex}$, $v_\mathrm{LSR}$ and FWHM left as free parameters."] [L1551 IRS 5 South: "We carried out a second set of MCMC runs for CH$_3$OCH$_3$, CH$_3$OCHO and $^{13}$CH$_3$CN by fixing $T_\mathrm{ex}$, $v_\mathrm{LSR}$ and FWHM to the medians found from the first run."]
  - software: CASSIS
  - parameter: s = 0.08 arcsec; vLSR range 2–5 km s−1 [L1551 IRS 5 South: "Due to a lack of constraints, we assumed for these MCMC runs the same source size as the Northern disk (0.08$''$)."] [L1551 IRS 5 South: "except for $v_\mathrm{LSR}$ which we limited to within 2 and 5 km s$^{-1}$."]
  - parameter: Median Tex = 193.90 K, FWHM = 2.23 km s−1, vLSR = 3.47 km s−1; exclude CH3OCH3 from median computation. [L1551 IRS 5 South: "We computed the medians of this first set of MCMC runs and found $T_\mathrm{ex}$ = 193.90 K, FWHM = 2.23 km s$^{-1}$ and $v_\mathrm{LSR}$ = 3.47 km s$^{-1}$."] [L1551 IRS 5 South: "we excluded the results for CH$_3$OCH$_3$ because its best-fit $T_\mathrm{ex}$ value cannot be constrained"]
  - assumption: Same source size as Northern disk [L1551 IRS 5 South: "we assumed for these MCMC runs the same source size as the Northern disk (0.08$''$)."]
  - fitting: Use second-run column densities for non-methanol species despite different methanol vLSR [L1551 IRS 5 South: "This second set of column densities will be the ones used to compute the abundances w.r.t. methanol (see below) even though CH$_3$OH has a different $v_\mathrm{LSR}$ than the other three COMs."]
  - validation: Models match data below the 3σ level; abundance ratios acknowledged as uncertain. [L1551 IRS 5 South: "match the data, with the uncertainties being less than the 3$\sigma$ level."] [L1551 IRS 5 South: "These abundance ratios could then be quite uncertain."]
- step 7: Fit Haro 5a IRS
  - method: Fix size and linewidth; fit N, Tex and velocity; compare median-constrained and methanol-constrained refits; retain methanol-constrained models. [Haro 5a IRS: "we decided to set the source size to 0.1$''$"] [Haro 5a IRS: "We also had to fix the FWHM at 2.5 km s$^{-1}$ in order to decrease the number of free parameters and better fit the low SNR lines."]
  - software: CASSIS
  - parameter: s = 0.1 arcsec; FWHM = 2.5 km s−1; vLSR range 13–15 km s−1 [Haro 5a IRS: "we decided to set the source size to 0.1$''$"] [Haro 5a IRS: "fix the FWHM at 2.5 km s$^{-1}$"]
  - assumption: Fixed size comparable to moment-map emission [Haro 5a IRS: "which is comparable to the size of the emission seen in the moment maps"]
  - fitting: Gaussian line fits establish fixed FWHM; final MCMC fixes Tex and vLSR to methanol fit. [Haro 5a IRS: "This FWHM value was measured with CASSIS by fitting a Gaussian profile on the lines of the different COMs separately."] [Haro 5a IRS: "We ran a third set of MCMC fits using the $T_\mathrm{ex}$ and $v_\mathrm{LSR}$ from the CH$_3$OH fit"]
  - validation: Visual model comparison; fitted-line residuals within ±3σ [Haro 5a IRS: "We examined the residuals between the data and the best-fit model and found that the residual of all lines used for the fit fall within $\pm$3$\sigma$."]
- step 8: Fit V346 Nor
  - method: Fix size from methanol integrated emission; fit remaining parameters; compare median-constrained and methanol-constrained refits; retain methanol constraints. [V346 Nor: "So we assumed the size determined from the integrated emission map of CH$_3$OH, 0.177$''$"] [V346 Nor: "We first ran the MCMC with $N$, $T_\mathrm{ex}$, $v_\mathrm{LSR}$ and FWHM as free parameters."]
  - software: CASSIS
  - parameter: s = 0.177 arcsec; vLSR range −5.0 to −2.0 km s−1 [V346 Nor: "So we assumed the size determined from the integrated emission map of CH$_3$OH, 0.177$''$"] [V346 Nor: "The parameter space for $v_\mathrm{LSR}$ was from -5.0 km s$^{-1}$ to -2.0 km s$^{-1}$"]
  - assumption: Use methanol-derived parameters for final COM column densities [V346 Nor: "we decided to keep the column densities obtained using the CH$_3$OH parameters for the remaining of this paper."]
  - fitting: Third set of fits uses CH3OH fit parameters after unsatisfactory median-constrained fits [V346 Nor: "the models obtained using the median values was not satisfactory, and we ran a third set of fits using the results of the CH$_3$OH fit."]
  - validation: Visual comparison favors methanol constraints for strongest species; 18 profiles differ from synthetic profiles. [V346 Nor: "We carried out a visual comparison between the models obtained using the medians and the models obtained with the values found for CH$_3$OH."] [V346 Nor: "We noted that 18 lines in this source have line profiles different from the synthetic ones"]
- step 9: Fit the dominant OO Ser component
  - method: Abandon inconsistent two-component fits; fit dominant component with N and Tex free; then fix Tex to the median and fit N. [OO Ser: "we could not find consistent results during the runs. To try to circumvent this issue, we focused on the strongest component."] [OO Ser: "Therefore, $N$ and $T_\mathrm{ex}$ were the only free parameters"]
  - software: CASSIS
  - parameter: s = 0.155 arcsec; FWHM = 2.0 km s−1; vLSR = 9.25 km s−1 [OO Ser: "we assumed the one obtained from the Moment 0 maps, 0.155$''$."] [OO Ser: "We also fixed the values of FWHM and $v_\mathrm{LSR}$ to 2.0 km s$^{-1}$ and 9.25 km s$^{-1}$, respectively."]
  - assumption: One component represents the dominant peaks [OO Ser: "The dominant peaks in the line profiles of OO Ser appear to be well described with one component."]
  - fitting: N and Tex first free; N alone free in the second fit [OO Ser: "Therefore, $N$ and $T_\mathrm{ex}$ were the only free parameters"] [OO Ser: "with $N$ being the sole free parameter and $T_\mathrm{ex}$ fixed to the median"]
  - validation: Residuals within 3σ except panel c of Figure F.4 [OO Ser: "With the exception of panel $c$ in [Figure F.4](https://zenodo.org/records/14967612), the residuals between the data and the best-fit models are all within the 3$\sigma$ level."]
- step 10: Bracket small-molecule column densities
  - method: First fix size and Tex to COM values and fit N, vLSR and FWHM; then use a 2 arcsec source and Tex = 50 and 300 K to bracket N. [Small molecules: "First, for each target, we fixed the source size and $T_\mathrm{ex}$ to the values obtained for the COMs, and we let $N$, $v_\mathrm{LSR}$ and FWHM be free parameters."] [Small molecules: "Second, we assumed a source size of 2$''$, so that there is no beam dilution, and considered two $T_\mathrm{ex}$ values (50 K and 300 K) to provide a range of possible column densities for these molecules."]
  - software: CASSIS
  - parameter: Second approach fixes vLSR and FWHM from the first approach [Small molecules: "Here we fixed the $v_\mathrm{LSR}$ and FWHM to the values obtained in the first approach, and computed another set of MCMC runs."]
  - assumption: Only one transition per molecule; source size and Tex must be assumed [Small molecules: "For the small molecules, we only have one transition of each. To derive column densities, we consequently need to assume a source size and $T_\mathrm{ex}$."]
  - fitting: MCMC in both approaches [Small molecules: "We then ran an MCMC as for the previous fits."] [Small molecules: "computed another set of MCMC runs."]
- step 12: Test sensitivity to extraction position
  - method: Repeat line fitting at NE, SE and SW pixels in L1551 IRS 5 North; use CH3(18)OH to estimate methanol and compare abundance ratios with the original NW position. [Impact of the chosen pixels: "We examined three additional positions in this FUor, at the northeast (NE), southeast (SE), and southwest (SW) sides of the disk, with our original pixel located on the northwest (NW)."] [Impact of the chosen pixels: "We used the lines of CH$_3$$^{18}$OH to estimate the column density of methanol."]
  - software: CASSIS
  - parameter: Fix source size to 0.08 arcsec at all additional positions [Impact of the chosen pixels: "we conducted line fitting by fixing the source size to the same size as for the NW position (0.08$''$"]
  - assumption: Same source size as original NW position [Impact of the chosen pixels: "fixing the source size to the same size as for the NW position"]
  - fitting: Same procedure as the main analysis [Impact of the chosen pixels: "We followed the same procedure as in Sect. [4](#sec:analysis) to obtain the column densities at these alternative positions."]
  - validation: Compare ratios across four positions; most agree within factor 3, while CH2DOH is enhanced at the original pixel. [Impact of the chosen pixels: "We found that, aside from CH$_2$DOH, relative abundances to methanol were consistent across the four positions, within a factor of 3."] [Impact of the chosen pixels: "For CH$_2$DOH, the original position showed higher abundances compared to the three other positions by factors of 3–5"]
