- result: L1551 IRS 5 N statistical D/H at three alternative positions = 0.3–0.5 ± not stated % [Impact of the chosen pixels: "the D/H statistical ratio at these three alternative positions (0.3–0.5%)"]
- limitation (authors): Fit uncertainties are statistical and underestimated [L1551 IRS 5 North: "We emphasize that these uncertainties are purely statistical and should be considered as underestimated."]
- limitation (authors): Baseline placement changes column densities by factors 1.2–1.8 in L1551 IRS 5 North [L1551 IRS 5 North: "found that the values of $N$ were higher by a factor between 1.2 to 1.8, depending on the COM."]
- limitation (authors): Haro 5a IRS source size and column density are degenerate [Haro 5a IRS: "There is a degeneracy between the column densities and the source size."]
- not stated (card): Calibration scripts, pipeline/manual status, calibrators, flagging and flux-scale transfer.; Self-calibration model, solution intervals, iterations, reference antenna and solution acceptance criteria.; Continuum-subtraction task, fit order and exact line-free channels.; Numerical image and pixel sizes, deconvolver, masks, stopping threshold and primary-beam treatment.; Exact velocity integration …

### 2018ApJS..236...45G: Chemistry of the High-mass Protostellar Molecular Clump IRAS 16562-3959 (2018; star_formation, astrochemistry; observational_alma_primary)
- question: What is the chemistry, morphology and kinematics of molecular emission in the high-mass protostellar clump IRAS 16562-3959 at 0.014 pc resolution, including hot cores, extended gas and cold starless gas?
- step 2: Identify lines
  - method: CASSIS with JPL and CDMS; LTE model comparison; anti-coincidence rejects [3.1: "To identify different molecular species we used CASSIS together with the Jet Propulsion Laboratory"]
  - software: CASSIS
  - parameter: VLSR between -6 and -17 km/s [3.1: "have $V_{\rm LSR}$ between $-6$ and $-17$ km s$^{-1}$"]
  - validation: at least three lines of main isotopologue for COMs [3.1: "For the COMs, we were able to identify at least three lines of the main isotopologue per species."]
- step 4: Propyne temperature and column density per pixel
  - method: Gaussian fits to K components, LTE optically thin rotation diagram, pixels >5 sigma [4.1: "We calculate the temperature and column density of CH$_3$CCH by fitting Gaussians to the four $K$ components and modeling the rotation diagrams assuming LTE and optically thin conditions."]
  - software: Minuit, Perl Data Language
  - parameter: sigma=1.2 mJy/beam, 5 sigma threshold [4.1: "where $\sigma=1.2$ mJy beam$^{-1}$ is the rms noise"]
  - assumption: LTE since density above critical density 1.5e4 cm^-3 [4.1: "we conclude that the critical density for the transitions is $\approx1.5\times10^4$ cm$^{-3}$."]
  - fitting: chi-square weighted by inverse variance; uncertainties per Lampton 1976 [4.1: "We obtain best fitting parameters (column density, temperature, central velocity and FWHM of the line) by minimizing the squared differences weighted by the inverse variances."]
  - validation: compared with single-dish Miettinen et al. 2006 [4.1: "Both the excitation temperature and column densities are consistent with previous single dish observations"]
- step 5: Column densities and Tex for sources
  - method: single excitation temperature models for all molecules except methanol; Radex non-LTE for CH3OH; Gaussian line profiles; filling factor 1 [4.2: "We model the emission of all molecules except CH$_3$OH using a single excitation temperature (SET) model"]
  - software: Radex, Minuit
  - parameter: Tex assigned from SO, CH3OH or CH3CCH based on critical density and VLSR group [4.2: "we assign the same temperature as SO to all molecules whose transitions have critical densities above or equal to that of SO"]
  - assumption: beam filling factor 1; equal E and A methanol abundances [4.2: "We assume, unless explicitly stated, that the beam filling factor of the emission is 1."]
  - fitting: chi-square weighted by sigma^-2 on primary-beam-corrected spectra; Lampton prescription [4.2: "We find optimal parameters by minimizing the squared difference between the data and the model, weighted by $\sigma^{-2}$"]
- result: CH3OH Tkin, CC core = 107 ± 10 K [Table 4: "CC core | $107\pm 10$ | $16.57\pm 0.06$"]
- result: CH3OH log N, CC core = 16.57 ± 0.06 log cm^-2 [Table 4: "CC core | $107\pm 10$ | $16.57\pm 0.06$"]
- result: CH3CCH radial temperature profile = 80 K (r/0.01 pc)^-0.3 ± not stated K [4.1: "decaying power law given by $80~\text{K}(r/0.01\text{ pc})^{-0.3}$"]
- result: [CH3OCH3]/[CH3OH] CC core = 1.7e-1 ± 0.03 dimensionless [6.2.1: "Methoxymethane to methanol ratios of $1.7\pm0.03\times10^{-1}$ and $4\pm2\times10^{-2}$ are measured toward the CC core and C8"]
- limitation (authors): Lack of short spacings filters extended emission; modest spectral resolution [2 Observations: "The lack of shorter baselines or single dish complementary data prevents the adequate recovery of structures larger than $\sim19\ensuremath{''}$."]
- not stated (card): Calibrators, flux scale and calibration pipeline version (deferred to earlier paper); Robust/weighting, cell and image size, line-free channels for continuum subtraction; Exact spectral ranges for moment masking; Initial parameters/priors for Minuit fits; Which lines/regions used for each assumed Tex

### 2023MNRAS.525.6146W: ALMA observations of the Extended Green Object G19.01-0.03 - II. A massive protostar with typical chemical abundances surrounded by four low-mass pre-stellar c… (2023; star_formation, astrochemistry; observational_alma_primary)
- question: What are the physical and chemical properties of the massive protostar G19.01-0.03 MM1 (hot core chemistry, CH3OH temperature structure) and its protocluster environment (four neighbouring millimetre sources), given ALMA 1.05 mm and VLA 1.…
- step 2: Identify molecular lines in the wide spws
  - method: Match emission peaks to JPL/CDMS rest frequencies at the systemic velocity and confirm with LTE synthetic spectra [3.2.1 COMs towards MM1 with ALMA: "a molecular transition was positively identified when all lines for that species predicted in the synthetic spectrum were present in the observed spectrum"]
  - software: CLASS/WEEDS, Splatalogue
  - parameter: typical hot core model parameters, T > 100 K, used for the identification spectra [3.2.1 COMs towards MM1 with ALMA: "for typical model parameters expected of a hot core (e.g. $T>100$ K)"]
  - assumption: Lines at the source systemic velocity of MM1 [3.2.1 COMs towards MM1 with ALMA: "at the source systemic velocity (cite: cyganowski11a)"]
  - validation: Criteria of Herbst & van Dishoeck 2009 for line identification [3.2.1 COMs towards MM1 with ALMA: "We follow the criteria presented by (cite: herbst09) in identifying molecular lines"]
- step 3: Rotation diagram of CH3OH at the MM1 continuum peak
  - method: LTE rotation diagram, with and without opacity correction, of eleven CH3OH transitions [4.1.1 Rotation diagram analysis of CH3OH: "We produce a rotation diagram towards the ALMA 1.05 mm continuum peak of MM1"]
  - software: CASA, Python
  - parameter: eleven CH3OH transitions (blended 15(1,0)-14(2,0) and 6(1,2)-5(1,2) excluded) [4.1.1 Rotation diagram analysis of CH3OH: "using eleven CH$_{3}$OH transitions"]
  - parameter: A and E transitions fit together [4.1.1 Rotation diagram analysis of CH3OH: "We include both A and E transitions in this analysis since we do not detect enough transitions to to fit them separately."]
  - assumption: LTE and optically thin emission, single temperature [4.1.1 Rotation diagram analysis of CH3OH: "Assuming local thermodynamic equilibrium and optically thin emission, a single temperature may describe the emission of all observed lines."]
  - fitting: Weighted least-squares with Gaussian-fit errors propagated; opacity-corrected version iteratively solves tau_i and T_rot by minimising chi2 [4.1.1 Rotation diagram analysis of CH3OH: "Errors on the Gaussian fits are propagated through to the rotation diagram, and the best fit is found following a weighted least-squares fit."] [4.1.1 Rotation diagram analysis of CH3OH: "by iteratively solving for the $\tau_{i}$ and $T_{\mathrm{rot}}$ values that produce the best fit, minimising the $\chi^{2}$ value"]
  - validation: Two outflow-tracing lines deviate from the trend and may be optically thick, motivating the opacity correction [4.1.1 Rotation diagram analysis of CH3OH: "deviate from the general trend of the other nine lines"]
- step 4: Bayesian LTE fit of CH3OH at the continuum peak and pixel by pixel across MM1
  - method: emcee MCMC over WEEDS/CLASS LTE synthetic spectra of six CH3OH lines in the ~278 GHz band, repeated per pixel within the MM1 continuum source size [4.1.3 Pixel-by-pixel analysis of CH3OH …: "we conduct our MCMC analysis on the six remaining CH$_{3}$OH lines in the"] [4.1.3 Pixel-by-pixel analysis of CH3OH …: "we find the highest likelihood model on a pixel-by-pixel basis across MM1"]
  - software: emcee, CLASS/WEEDS, WeedsPy_MCMC, toddTools, analysisUtils
  - parameter: 60 walkers, n_burn = 200, n_prod = 500 [4.1.3 Pixel-by-pixel analysis of CH3OH …: "We initialise 60 walkers, and allow them to run for $n_{\mathrm{burn}}=200$ and $n_{\mathrm{prod}}=500$ iterations"]
  - parameter: Uniform priors: N 0.05-3.0e18 cm-2, T 80-300 K, v_cen 56.0-64.0 km/s, dV 2.0-8.0 km/s [4.1.3 Pixel-by-pixel analysis of CH3OH …: "are set to be $(0.05-3.0)\times10^{18}$ cm$^{-2}$ for the column density, $80-300$ K for the temperature, $56.0 - 64.0$ km s$^{-1}$ for the centroid velocity, and $2.0 - 8.0$ km s$^{-1}$ for the velocity width"]
  - parameter: source size fixed to the geometric mean of the ~278 GHz synthesised beam (~0.5''); ALMA projected diameter 558 m [4.1.3 Pixel-by-pixel analysis of CH3OH …: "we fix the source size to the geometric mean of the"] [4.1.3 Pixel-by-pixel analysis of CH3OH …: "The projected diameter of the ALMA array was calculated to be 558 metres"]
  - assumption: LTE with background continuum included; all modelled transitions arise in the same emitting region (outflow-tracing 9(-1,9)-8(0,8) and 6(1,5)-5(1,4), blended lines and the vt=1 line excluded) [4.1.2 Synthetic CH3OH spectra: "WEEDS solves the radiative transfer equation assuming a state of local thermodynamic equilibrium (LTE), and takes into account the background continuum emission."] [4.1.3 Pixel-by-pixel analysis of CH3OH …: "since an assumption made here is that the emitting region is the same for all modelled transitions"]
  - assumption: Background continuum from the ALMA 1.05 mm continuum converted to brightness temperature [4.1.3 Pixel-by-pixel analysis of CH3OH …: "with the ALMA 1.05 mm continuum converted to brightness temperature"]
  - fitting: emcee affine-invariant sampler; Gaussian log-likelihood with observed spectrum rms; uninformative uniform priors; uncertainties from 0.16 and 0.84 quantiles of the posterior [4.1.2 Synthetic CH3OH spectra: "using the Python package EMCEE (cite: emcee), an affine-invariant Monte Carlo Markov Chain (MCMC) sampler"] [4.1.2 Synthetic CH3OH spectra: "The statistical uncertainty on each model parameter is evaluated from the 0.16 and 0.84 quantiles of the posterior"]
  - validation: Results unchanged with a different random seed and n_prod > 3000; consistent with the opacity-corrected rotation diagram; model reproduces the observed spectrum [4.1.3 Pixel-by-pixel analysis of CH3OH …: "These remain unchanged (within the errors) when walkers are initiated with a different random number seed"] [4.1.3 Pixel-by-pixel analysis of CH3OH …: "These column density and temperature values are remarkably consistent with those derived from the opacity-corrected rotation diagram"]
- step 5: Column densities and fractional abundances of other species
  - method: Order-of-magnitude LTE fits with only column density free, per species, then ratios to CH3OH and H2 column densities [4.3.1 Estimation of likely column densi…: "by keeping only the column density as a free parameter (cite: csengeri19) in the emcee sampler with weedspy_mcmc"] [4.3.1 Estimation of likely column densi…: "Fractional abundances of each species with respect to CH$_{3}$OH and H$_{2}$ are evaluated from the ratio of their respective column densities."]
  - software: emcee, WeedsPy_MCMC, CLASS/WEEDS
  - parameter: T = 162.7 K, v = 59.7 km/s, dV = 6.4 km/s fixed from the CH3OH fit; source size ~0.5'' [4.3.1 Estimation of likely column densi…: "The temperature, centroid velocity and velocity width are set to the highest likelihood values evaluated at the ALMA 1.05 mm continuum peak from the CH$_{3}$OH spectra"]
  - assumption: Line emission of MM1 species has the same extent as the continuum [4.3.1 Estimation of likely column densi…: "the extent of the line emission is generally consistent across species and with the extent of the 1.05 mm continuum emission"]
  - fitting: emcee single-parameter (column density) highest-likelihood fit [4.3.1 Estimation of likely column densi…: "The highest likelihood column density of each species is listed in Table [2](#tab:chemistry)"]
  - validation: Combined synthetic spectrum compared with observed wide-band spectra [4.3.1 Estimation of likely column densi…: "On the whole the combined model reproduces the observed emission well."]
- step 6: Derive the 12C/13C ratio from CH3OH isotopologues
  - method: Ratio of 12CH3OH to 13CH3OH column densities at the MM1 peak [4.3.3 12C/13C isotope ratio: "we can estimate the $^{12}\mathrm{C}/^{13}\mathrm{C}$ isotope ratio from the ratio of their column densities"]
  - software: emcee, WeedsPy_MCMC
  - assumption: Optically thin lines, same rotational temperature, similar spatial extent [4.3.3 12C/13C isotope ratio: "This is valid if the molecular transitions are optically thin, described by the same rotational temperature, and exhibit similar spatial emission extents"]
  - validation: Reran WEEDSPY_MCMC with an unresolved 0.2'' source size to test dependence on source size [4.3.3 12C/13C isotope ratio: "To test for dependence on the assumed source size, we reran our WEEDSPY_MCMC analysis at the 1.05 mm continuum peak for the case of unresolved emission"]
- step 10: Characterise NH3(3,3) and 25 GHz CH3OH maser candidates in the VLA data
  - method: 2D Gaussian (point-source, beam-sized) fits to >=5 sigma emission spots; compare angular separations of NH3(3,3) and 44 GHz CH3OH masers with K-S and A-D tests [3.2.3 Ammonia emission with the VLA: "we fit the observed emission with a 2D Gaussian using the casa imfit task"] [3.2.3 Ammonia emission with the VLA: "Running both the K-S and A-D tests on the full range of angular separations"]
  - software: CASA, CLASS/WEEDS
  - parameter: >=5 sigma emission in >=2 consecutive channels, spatially contiguous within a beam; sigma measured per channel [3.2.3 Ammonia emission with the VLA: "is spatially contiguous within a VLA beam"]
  - parameter: 25 GHz CH3OH: 4 sigma criterion; thermal if >=5 channels, maser candidate if <=4 channels [3.2.4 25 GHz methanol emission with the…: "As (cite: towner17) adopt a 4$\sigma$ detection criterion"]
  - assumption: Emission expected to be unresolved, fitted size fixed to the synthesised beam [3.2.3 Ammonia emission with the VLA: "we fit each emission spot as a point source by fixing the size to that of the synthesised beam"]
  - fitting: casa imfit 2D Gaussian; p > 0.05 criterion for same distribution [3.2.3 Ammonia emission with the VLA: "The null hypothesis in this case states that the two distributions may be drawn from the same underlying distribution if the tests return $p$-values larger than 0.05."]
  - validation: Brightness temperatures compared with the line Eu/kB; optically thin LTE models (Weeds) compared with 25 GHz lines [3.2.4 25 GHz methanol emission with the…: "Line models under LTE and optically thin conditions (produced using the Weeds package of the class software) reasonably describe the broad emission components of all four lines towards MM1."]
- result: CH3OH column density at MM1 continuum peak (MCMC LTE) = (2.22 +/- 0.01)e18 ± 0.01e18 cm-2 [Conclusions (ii): "returns highest likelihood column density and temperature of $(2.22\pm0.01)\times10^{18}$ cm$^{-2}$ and $162.7\substack{+0.3 \\ -0.5}$ K respectively"]
- result: CH3OH excitation temperature at MM1 continuum peak (MCMC LTE) = 162.7 ± +0.3/-0.5 K [Conclusions (ii): "returns highest likelihood column density and temperature of $(2.22\pm0.01)\times10^{18}$ cm$^{-2}$ and $162.7\substack{+0.3 \\ -0.5}$ K respectively"]
- result: CH3OH centroid velocity and FWHM width at MM1 peak = V_cen = 59.73; dV = 6.38 ± V_cen +/- 0.01; dV +0.01/-0.03 km/s [4.1.3 Pixel-by-pixel analysis of CH3OH …: "V$_{\mathrm{cen}}=59.73\pm0.01$ km s$^{-1}$ and $\Delta V = 6.38\substack{+0.01 \\ -0.03}$ km s$^{-1}$"]
- result: Opacity-corrected rotation diagram CH3OH column density and T_rot = N = (2.0 +/- 0.4)e18 cm-2; T_rot = 166 +/- 9 K ± 0.4e18 cm-2; 9 K cm-2; K [Conclusions (ii): "our opacity-corrected rotation diagram analysis of $(2.0\pm0.4)\times10^{18}$ cm$^{-2}$ and $166\pm9$ K respectively"]
- result: Rotational temperature without opacity correction = 186 ± 8 K [4.1.1 Rotation diagram analysis of CH3OH: "The derived rotational temperature is $186\pm8$ K."]
- result: Range of line optical depths in the opacity-corrected rotation diagram = ~0.4-11.7 ± not stated dimensionless [4.1.1 Rotation diagram analysis of CH3OH: "The $\tau_{i}$ values for this best fit range between $\sim0.4-11.7$"]
- result: Peak CH3OH temperature, offset from the continuum peak by 0.22'' (~880 au) = 165.5 ± 0.6 K [Conclusions (iii): "The peak CH$_{3}$OH temperature ($165.5\pm0.6$ K) is offset from the MM1 dust peak by 0.22$\ensuremath{''}\sim880$ au."]
- result: Temperature difference between offset peak and the column-density peak = 2.8 ± 0.7 K [4.1.4 Images of CH3OH physical paramete…: "is significantly greater than its propagated statistical error (i.e. $2.8\pm0.7$ K)"]
- result: Number of line transitions and species identified in the wide ALMA spws = 43 transitions from 11 species ± not stated dimensionless [3.2.1 COMs towards MM1 with ALMA: "we identify 43 line transitions from 11 different species in the wide ALMA spws"]
- result: CH3OCH3 column density at MM1 peak = 4.3e17 ± order of magnitude estimate cm-2 [Table 2: "$4.3\times10^{17}$"]
- result: 13CH3OH column density at MM1 peak = 3.9e17 ± order of magnitude estimate cm-2 [Table 2: "$3.9\times10^{17}$"]
- result: CH3OCHO column density at MM1 peak = 1.3e17 ± order of magnitude estimate cm-2 [Table 2: "$1.3\times10^{17}$"]
- result: CH3CH2OH column density at MM1 peak = 6.0e16 ± order of magnitude estimate cm-2 [Table 2: "$6.0\times10^{16}$"]
- result: NH2CHO column density at MM1 peak = 5.0e15 ± order of magnitude estimate cm-2 [Table 2: "$5.0\times10^{15}$"]
- result: Fractional abundance ranges of identified species = 1e-1 to 1e-4 relative to CH3OH; 1e-6 to 1e-9 relative to H2 ± not stated dimensionless [4.3.1 Estimation of likely column densi…: "we report fractional abundances of the identified species ranging from $10^{-1}-10^{-4}$ with respect to CH$_{3}$OH, and $10^{-6}-10^{-9}$ with respect to H$_{2}$."]
- result: 12C/13C ratio from CH3OH isotopologues at MM1 peak = ~6 ± not stated dimensionless [4.3.3 12C/13C isotope ratio: "we estimate that $^{12}\mathrm{C}/^{13}\mathrm{C}\simeq6$"]
- result: 12C/13C ratio with unresolved 0.2'' source size = ~7.5 ± not stated dimensionless [4.3.3 12C/13C isotope ratio: "we derive a $^{12}\mathrm{C}/^{13}\mathrm{C}$ ratio of $\sim$7.5"]
- result: Number of NH3(3,3) emission spots and groups above 5 sigma = 50 spots in 8 groups (mean sigma 1.14 mJy/beam) ± not stated dimensionless [3.2.3 Ammonia emission with the VLA: "we identify 50 emission spots at the $\geq5\sigma$ level (where the mean $\sigma$ across all channels with emission is $\sigma=1.14$ mJy beam$^{-1}$) that reside in 8 emission groups"]
- result: Peak intensity of brightest NH3(3,3) group (ID 4) = 216.0 (T_B ~1550 K) ± 1.3 mJy/beam [3.2.3 Ammonia emission with the VLA: "has a fitted peak intensity ($216.0\pm1.3$ mJy beam$^{-1}$) corresponding to a brightness temperature $\sim$1550 K"]
- limitation (authors): 25 GHz CH3OH maser/thermal result is tentative because of low S/N [3.2.4 25 GHz methanol emission with the…: "we emphasise that this is a tentative result due to the low S/N of the 25 GHz CH$_3$OH emission towards MM1"]
- limitation (authors): Abundances of species other than CH3OH are only order-of-magnitude estimates [4.3.1 Estimation of likely column densi…: "We instead evaluate order of magnitude estimates for the column densities of these species"]
- limitation (authors): Disc mass may be underestimated because temperature, opacity and optical depth variations are ignored [4.2.1 Temperature, mass and stability o…: "This approach does however risk underestimating the disc mass"]
- not stated (card): Self-calibration details (number of iterations, solution intervals, solution types, reference antenna) for the ALMA continuum are not given; Explicit line-free channel ranges used for the pseudo-continuum and continuum subtraction are not listed; tclean-level parameters (cell size, image size, niter, threshold, masks, deconvolver, gridder for the mosaic) are not given; Flagging choices, weights a…

### 2018ApJ...864..170G: H<SUB>2</SUB>CO Ortho-to-para Ratio in the Protoplanetary Disk HD 163296 (2018; protoplanetary_disks, astrochemistry; observational_alma_primary)
- question: What is the disk-averaged H2CO ortho-to-para ratio in the HD 163296 disk, and what does it say about H2CO formation (gas-phase vs grain surface)?
- step 2: Measure disk-integrated fluxes and rotational temperature
  - method: Circular 6 arcsec mask fluxes; rotational diagram of three ortho lines [3.2: "Using the three ortho lines we constructed a rotational diagram"]
  - parameter: Flux error: std of 500 simulated flux measurements at random signal-free positions [3.2: "The uncertainty in the flux was estimated by taking the standard deviation of 500 simulated flux measurements from regions free of signal"]
  - assumption: Emission thermalized (LTE) given densities >1e6 cm-3 [3.2: "we expect the bulk of the emission to be thermalized"]
  - validation: Compared with 24 K from Carney et al. 2017 [3.2: "This temperature is consistent with the temperature of 24 K derived by (cite: carney2017)"]
- step 3: Fit H2CO abundance structure to ALMA visibilities
  - method: Two-component parametric abundance (X1, X2, alpha free), RADMC-3D LTE models, vis-sample, MCMC, for three (z/r)_l [4.1: "We use a Bayesian approach to find the best fit of the model to the ALMA visibilities."]
  - software: RADMC-3D, vis-sample, emcee
  - parameter: Fixed R1=1 au, R2=80 au, Rout=600 au, (z/r)_u=0.5; (z/r)_l=0.15, 0.20, 0.25 [Table 3: "$R_1$ | 1 au"]
  - assumption: LTE; uniform o/p of 3 for total abundance [4.1: "are computed assuming a uniform o/p ratio of 3"]
  - fitting: emcee MCMC; likelihood from weighted real and imaginary visibility differences [4.1: "We then compute the weighted difference between model and observations, for the real and imaginary parts of the complex visibility, which gives the likelihood of the model."]
  - validation: Channel maps of data, model and residuals; third-component test [4.1: "Fig. [5] shows channel maps of the $\mathrm{H_2CO}$ line observed with ALMA (top row), together with the best fit model (middle row), and the residuals (bottom row)."]
- result: Rotational temperature from three ortho lines = 24 ± ±14 K [3.2: "We obtain a rotational temperature of $24\pm14$ K."]
- result: ALMA 290 GHz p-H2CO flux = 1.22 ± 0.05 Jy km/s [Table 2: "$1.22\pm 0.05$"]
- limitation (authors): o/p is a disk-averaged value; vertical distributions cannot be distinguished by SMA data [4.2: "However, we cannot rule out any of the vertical distribution choices based on the SMA data."]
- not stated (card): Line-free channels and continuum subtraction details; Self-calibration solution intervals and reference; Cleaning threshold, image size and cell size; MCMC priors, walkers and steps; Source coordinates; Gas-to-dust ratio, collisional rates and line broadening used in RADMC-3D; SMA calibration flux values and MIR steps; Dust/gas structure parameters from Qi et al. 2011 in detail

### 2025A&A...698A.115R: Radial variations in the nitrogen, carbon, and hydrogen fractionation in the PDS 70 planet-hosting disk (2025; protoplanetary_disks, astrochemistry; observational_alma_primary)
- question: What are the radial profiles of nitrogen, carbon and hydrogen fractionation of HCN in the planet-hosting PDS 70 disk, and what fractionation processes do they point to?
- step 6: radial rotational diagram for H13CN and HC15N
  - method: radially resolved rotational diagram with optical-depth correction C_tau, on cubes with equal beams; single Tex fitted jointly for both isotopologues [Radial rotational diagram: "we could apply the rotational diagram (cite: goldsmith1999population) analysis to extract their molecular column density $N_\mathrm{t}$ and excitation temperature $T_\mathrm{ex}$"] [Radial rotational diagram: "We computed the rotational diagram correcting for the optical depth factor $C_\tau$."]
  - software: emcee
  - parameter: optical depth from peak brightness temperature and Tex, tau = -ln(1 - Tb/Tex), Tb the azimuthal average of peak Tb in each annulus [Radial rotational diagram: "where $T_\mathrm{b}(R)$ is the radial profile of the brightness temperature, taken as the azimuthal average of the peak brightness temperature over each annulus in which we applied the rotational diagram"]
  - parameter: cubes with smallest channel width allowed by the spectral resolution [Radial rotational diagram: "We therefore used cubes imaged with the smallest width of the velocity channel allowed by the spectral resolution of the observations."]
  - parameter: Band 6-optimised cubes (~0.20'') in the main result; Band 7-optimised (~0.15'') as a consistent alternative [Radial rotational diagram: "The two results are consistent, and were both used to extract the radial profiles of N, C, and H fractionation of HCN"]
  - assumption: optically thin N_u relation, corrected by C_tau [Radial rotational diagram: "In the optically thin assumption, $N_\mathrm{u}$ depends on the integrated flux density"]
  - assumption: excitation temperature equals kinetic temperature (LTE) [HCN emitting layer from the excitation …: "The rotational diagram assumes that the excitation temperature $T_\mathrm{ex}(r)$ is equal to the kinetic temperature $T(r,z)$ (LTE)."]
  - fitting: emcee, 128 walkers, 3000 burn-in, 500 steps; uniform priors Tex 10-100 K, Nt 1e10-1e16 cm-2; likelihood includes propagated uncertainties and 10% flux calibration in quadrature [Radial rotational diagram: "through a Markov-Chain Monte Carlo (MCMC) method with the `emcee` package (cite: foreman2013emcee), with 128 walkers, 3000 burn-in steps, and 500 steps"] [Radial rotational diagram: "We considered a uniform prior with $T_\mathrm{ex}\in (10,100)$ K and $N_\mathrm{t} \in (10^{10}, 10^{16})$ cm$^{-2}$."]
  - validation: spectral smearing tested with 0.4 vs 0.8 km/s channel cubes and found negligible [Spectral smearing effect on brightness …: "This effect is thus negligible in the rotational diagram analysis we performed in this work."]
- step 7: N and H fractionation with fixed 12C/13C
  - method: build posteriors of 69 x N(H13CN)/N(HC15N) and N(DCN)/[69 x N(H13CN)] from column-density posteriors; DCN column density from the rotational-diagram equation with randomly sampled Tex [Isotopologue ratios: "ratios were built from the posterior distributions of the column density of , , and DCN."] [Isotopologue ratios: "by randomly sampling the excitation temperature from the rotational diagram result, assuming DCN has the same excitation temperature as and"]
  - parameter: 12C/13C = 69 (radially constant) [Isotopologue ratios: "assuming a radially constant $\ce{^12C}/\ce{^13C}=69$ ratio (cite: wilson1999isotopes)"]
  - parameter: DCN flux sampled from a normal distribution with its statistical uncertainty [Isotopologue ratios: "by randomly sampling the flux of the DCN (3-2) line from a normal distribution centered around the median value, with a standard deviation given by its statistical uncertainty"]
  - assumption: DCN has the same excitation temperature as H13CN and HC15N [Isotopologue ratios: "assuming DCN has the same excitation temperature as and"]
  - validation: radially constant 12C/13C checked against the HCN-hyperfine carbon profile [Isotopologue ratios using HCN column de…: "the carbon fractionation profile is consistent with the ISM value between $\sim50$ and 100 au, within the errorbars"]
- step 8: HCN column density from resolved hyperfine component
  - method: double-Gaussian fit to deprojected spectra in radial bins (hyperfine component at 3.8 km/s and main component at 5.5 km/s), flux posterior, optically thin N(HCN) with Tex from the rotational diagram [Radial profile of HCN column density: "To extract the radial profile of the flux of the hyperfine component, we applied a double-Gaussian fit to the spectrum in multiple radial bins."] [12C/13C ratio from the HCN hyperfine co…: "we extracted the radial profile of the column density of HCN, assuming that the hyperfine component is optically thin, and the same excitation temperature inferred from the rotational diagram analysis of and"]
  - software: GoFish, emcee
  - parameter: centers fixed at 3.8 and 5.5 km/s; widths and peaks free; spectra shifted for Keplerian rotation with GoFish [12C/13C ratio from the HCN hyperfine co…: "We fixed the center of the two Gaussian components to 3.8 km s$^{-1}$ for the hyperfine and to 5.5 km s$^{-1}$ for the main component respectively, and fit for the two widths and peaks."]
  - parameter: fit stopped at 5.5 km/s (only half of the main component covered by LB) [12C/13C ratio from the HCN hyperfine co…: "We extracted the flux associated with the hyperfine component by fitting a double Gaussian to the spectrum, stopping at 5.5 km s$^{-1}$."]
  - parameter: Gaussians spectrally over-sampled and averaged in each spectral bin [12C/13C ratio from the HCN hyperfine co…: "we spectrally over-sampled the Gaussian profiles and took the average value inside a spectral bin, to fit the observed spectrum"]
  - parameter: fiducial radial range ~50-120 au [12C/13C ratio from the HCN hyperfine co…: "The fiducial radial range is between $\sim50$ and 120 au, because for small radii the two components are blended, and the fit is poorly constrained"]
  - assumption: hyperfine component optically thin (tau <~ 0.02) [Radial profile of HCN column density: "by assuming it is optically thin, which is valid since $\tau\lesssim0.02$"]
  - assumption: same Tex as H13CN/HC15N from the Band 7 resolution rotational diagram [Radial profile of HCN column density: "We therefore computed the radial profile of the HCN column density, assuming the same excitation temperature obtained from the rotational diagram of and"]
  - fitting: emcee per annulus, 128 walkers, 500 burn-in, 500 steps; uniform priors: widths (0,5) km/s, hyperfine peak (0,10) mJy/beam, main peak (0,30) mJy/beam; flux from 50th/16th/84th percentiles [12C/13C ratio from the HCN hyperfine co…: "We applied the MCMC sampling to each annulus in which we divided the disk, with 128 walkers, 500 burn-in steps, and 500 steps"] [12C/13C ratio from the HCN hyperfine co…: "assuming a uniform prior of (0,5) km s$^{-1}$ for the two widths, of (0,10) mJy beam$^{-1}$ for the peak of the hyperfine component, and of (0,30) mJy beam$^{-1}$ for the peak of the main component"]
  - validation: in the fiducial range the hyperfine Gaussian width is consistent with a radially constant line width [12C/13C ratio from the HCN hyperfine co…: "in the fiducial range the Gaussian fit to the hyperfine component is consistent with a radially constant line width"]
- result: Optical depth of HCN hyperfine component = <~0.02 ± not stated dimensionless [Radial profile of HCN column density: "by assuming it is optically thin, which is valid since $\tau\lesssim0.02$"]
- result: Fiducial radial range of the hyperfine-based HCN column density = ~50-120 ± not stated au [12C/13C ratio from the HCN hyperfine co…: "The fiducial radial range is between $\sim50$ and 120 au"]
- limitation (authors): Hyperfine fit limited by half-covered HCN (4-3), 0.9 km/s resolution and low S/N, giving large errors and no robust ratios inside ~40 au or beyond ~100 au [Isotopologue ratios using HCN column de…: "is strongly limited by the fact that only half of the HCN (4-3) line is covered by LB observations, by the low spectral resolution of $\sim0.9$ km s$^{-1}$, and the lower signal to noise ratio"]
- limitation (authors): Profiles inside ~25 au are highly uncertain [Nitrogen: "highly uncertain for radii $\lesssim 25$ au, and therefore need to be interpreted with caution"]
- limitation (authors): Nitrogen and carbon fractionation cannot be robustly separated, making the interpretation speculative [Carbon: "which makes the above interpretation only speculative, as the role of nitrogen and carbon fractionation in determining the decreasing trend cannot be robustly distinguished with current observations"]
- not stated (card): The exoALMA self-calibration pipeline (Loomis et al., subm.) is cited as submitted, so its exact scripts, SNR stop criteria and parameters are not available from this paper.; ALMA pipeline version, flux/bandpass/phase calibrators and observation dates for the Band 7 and Band 6 EBs are not given.; Band 6 calibration, self-calibration and imaging details are deferred to Rampinelli et al. 2024 and L…

### 2023MNRAS.523.2887M: Rotation-tunnelling spectrum and astrochemical modelling of dimethylamine, CH<SUB>3</SUB>NHCH<SUB>3</SUB>, and searches for it in space (2023; astrochemistry, star_formation, milky_way_ism; archival_reuse)
- question: Improve DMA spectroscopic predictions, search for DMA toward Sgr B2(N) and G+0.693–0.027, and compare abundance limits with astrochemical models.
- step 4: Fit dimethyl ether as the DMA template
  - method: Fit population diagrams for rotational temperature; adjust synthetic LTE spectra for column density. [Search for DMA toward Sgr B2(N1S) and S…: "A fit to these population diagrams yields rotational temperatures of $\sim$170 K and $\sim$130 K, respectively"] [Search for DMA toward Sgr B2(N1S) and S…: "Assuming these temperatures, we adjusted synthetic LTE spectra to the observed spectra"]
  - software: Weeds
  - parameter: Five model parameters: source size, column density, rotational temperature, linewidth and velocity offset [Search for dimethylamine toward Sgr B2(…: "Each species was modeled with a set of five parameters: size of the emitting region ($\theta_{\rm s}$), column density ($N$), temperature ($T_{\rm rot}$), linewidth ($\Delta V$), and velocity offset ($V_{\rm off}$)"]
  - parameter: Sgr B2(N1S): emission size 2″; Sgr B2(N2b): ∼0.8″ from uncontaminated integrated-intensity maps [Search for DMA toward Sgr B2(N1S) and S…: "we assumed an emission size of 2$\ensuremath{''}$ to model the emission of dimethyl ether toward Sgr B2(N1S)."] [Search for DMA toward Sgr B2(N1S) and S…: "In the case of Sgr B2(N2b), integrated intensity maps of lines of dimethyl ether that are free of contamination suggest an emission size on the order of 0.8$\ensuremath{''}$."]
  - assumption: LTE; finite angular resolution and transition optical depth included [Search for dimethylamine toward Sgr B2(…: "Like in our previous ReMoCA studies (cite: Belloche19; Belloche22), we compared the observed spectra to synthetic spectra computed under the assumption of LTE with the astronomical software Weeds (cite: Maret11)."] [Search for dimethylamine toward Sgr B2(…: "The calculations take into account the finite angular resolution of the observations and the optical depth of the rotational transitions."]
  - fitting: By-eye best-fit synthetic spectra for each molecule; sum contributions of identified molecules. [Search for dimethylamine toward Sgr B2(…: "For each position, we derived by eye a best-fit synthetic spectrum for each molecule separately, and then added together the contributions of all identified molecules."]
  - validation: Dozens of ground-state lines identified at both positions; excited-state lines also detected. [Search for DMA toward Sgr B2(N1S) and S…: "Dimethyl ether is well detected toward both Sgr B2(N1S) and Sgr B2(N2b), with dozens of lines in its vibrational ground state easily identified"]
- step 5: Estimate methylamine toward Sgr B2(N2b)
  - method: Fit a synthetic LTE spectrum using methanol’s rotational temperature and emission size. [Search for DMA toward Sgr B2(N1S) and S…: "Methylamine has a similar rotational temperature as methanol toward Sgr B2(N1S), which led us to assume the same rotational temperature and emission size as methanol toward Sgr B2(N2b)."]
  - software: Weeds
  - parameter: Size 0.5″; Trot 140 K; FWHM 3.5 km s−1; velocity offset 0.0 km s−1 [Table 9: "| CH$_3$NH$_2$, $\upsilon=0$ | t | 2 | 0.5 | 140 | 2.7 (17) | 1.07 | 3.5 | 0.0 | 299 |"]
  - assumption: Methylamine shares methanol’s rotational temperature and emission size at N2b. [Search for DMA toward Sgr B2(N1S) and S…: "assume the same rotational temperature and emission size as methanol toward Sgr B2(N2b)."]
  - fitting: By-eye synthetic LTE fit [Search for dimethylamine toward Sgr B2(…: "For each position, we derived by eye a best-fit synthetic spectrum for each molecule separately"]
  - validation: Only two relatively uncontaminated transitions, at 84306 and 112273 MHz; detection classed as tentative. [Search for DMA toward Sgr B2(N1S) and S…: "Only two transitions are relatively free of contamination (at 84 306 and 112 273 MHz)."] [Search for DMA toward Sgr B2(N1S) and S…: "As a result, we only claim a tentative detection of methylamine toward Sgr B2(N2b)."]
- step 6: Set ALMA DMA column-density limits
  - method: Compute synthetic LTE spectra with the dimethyl ether size and temperature; vary DMA column density by hand and compare by eye. [Search for DMA toward Sgr B2(N1S) and S…: "we computed synthetic LTE spectra assuming the same emission size and rotational temperature as those derived for dimethyl ether."] [Search for DMA toward Sgr B2(N1S) and S…: "by varying the column density by hand and comparing the synthetic and observed spectra by eye"]
  - software: Weeds
  - parameter: N1S: size 2.0″, Trot 170 K, FWHM 5.0 km s−1, Voff 0.0 km s−1, Fvib 1.40 [Table 8: "| CH$_3$NHCH$_3$, $\upsilon=0$ | n | 0 | 2.0 | 170 | $<$ 9.8 (16) | 1.40 | 5.0 | 0.0 | $>$ 204 |"]
  - parameter: N2b: size 0.8″, Trot 130 K, FWHM 3.5 km s−1, Voff 0.0 km s−1, Fvib 1.18 [Table 9: "| CH$_3$NHCH$_3$, $\upsilon=0$ | n | 0 | 0.8 | 130 | $<$ 5.9 (16) | 1.18 | 3.5 | 0.0 | $>$ 1360 |"]
  - parameter: Systemic velocities: N1S 62.0 km s−1; N2b 74.2 km s−1 [Search for dimethylamine toward Sgr B2(…: "$V_{\rm sys}=62.0$ km s$^{-1}$ for Sgr B2(N1S) and $V_{\rm sys}=74.2$ km s$^{-1}$ for Sgr B2(N2b)."]
  - assumption: DMA shares dimethyl ether’s emission size and rotational temperature. [Search for DMA toward Sgr B2(N1S) and S…: "we computed synthetic LTE spectra assuming the same emission size and rotational temperature as those derived for dimethyl ether."]
  - fitting: 3σ level used as a guide, accounting for blends and baseline uncertainties; no sampler or likelihood stated [Search for DMA toward Sgr B2(N1S) and S…: "using the 3 $\sigma$ level (dashed lines in Figs. [9](#f:remoca_ch3nhch3_n1s) and [10](#f:remoca_ch3nhch3_n2b)) as a guide, but also accounting for the blends with other species and the uncertainties on the baseline level."]
  - validation: Synthetic upper-limit spectra shown against observations; no DMA evidence at either position. [Search for DMA toward Sgr B2(N1S) and S…: "We did not find any evidence for dimethylamine in either source."] [Search for DMA toward Sgr B2(N1S) and S…: "The synthetic spectra used to estimate the upper limit to its column density are shown in Figs. [9](#f:remoca_ch3nhch3_n1s) and [10](#f:remoca_ch3nhch3_n2b)"]
- step 7: Set the G+0.693 DMA limit
  - method: Generate an LTE spectrum with MADCUBA/SLIM; use the brightest unblended emission, an unresolved Q-band HFS group, for an integrated-area upper limit. [Nondetection of dimethylamine toward G+…: "Using the SLIM (Spectral Line Identification and Modeling) tool of MADCUBA, we generated a synthetic spectrum of DMA under the assumption of local thermodynamic equilibrium (LTE) and compared it with the observed spectra."] [Nondetection of dimethylamine toward G+…: "To derive the upper limit to the column density, we have used the brightest emission according to the simulated LTE model that appears unblended"]
  - software: MADCUBA, SLIM
  - parameter: Tex 16 K; VLSR 67 km s−1; FWHM 18 km s−1 [Nondetection of dimethylamine toward G+…: "For the LTE simulated spectrum, we have used the excitation temperature derived for by (cite: zeng2018), 16 K"] [Nondetection of dimethylamine toward G+…: "We also used the velocity and linewidth found for the latter species: $V_{\rm LSR}=67$ km s$^{-1}$ and $FWHM=18$ km s$^{-1}$"]
  - parameter: 44.9 GHz beam 39″; rms 1.6 mK in a 1.5 km s−1 channel [Nondetection of dimethylamine toward G+…: "the beam size at 44.9 GHz is 39”."] [Nondetection of dimethylamine toward G+…: "The rms noise level in the Yebes spectrum near 44.9 GHz is 1.6 mK in a 1.5 km s$^{-1}$ channel."]
  - assumption: LTE; H2 column density 1.35 × 10^23 cm−2 for fractional abundance [Nondetection of dimethylamine toward G+…: "under the assumption of local thermodynamic equilibrium (LTE)"] [Nondetection of dimethylamine toward G+…: "using $N$(H$_{2}$)=1.35$\times$10$^{23}$ cm$^{-2}$ ((cite: martin_tracing_2008))."]
  - fitting: 3σ integrated-area upper limit [Nondetection of dimethylamine toward G+…: "Considering the noise of the data, we obtained a 3$\sigma$ upper limit in integrated area"]
  - validation: Selected region appears uncontaminated; local baseline uncertainty changes the column limit by less than a factor of two. [Nondetection of dimethylamine toward G+…: "The upper limit to the column density of DMA could be affected by the local baseline by less than a factor of two when the local baseline uncertainty is considered."] [Nondetection of dimethylamine toward G+…: "Fortunately, the spectral region of these transitions seems to be clean from contamination"]
- result: Dimethyl ether population-diagram temperature, Sgr B2(N1S) = 174 ± 7; statistical standard deviation, possibly underestimated K [Table 6: "| CH$_3$OCH$_3$ | $\upsilon=0$, $\upsilon_{11}=1$, $\upsilon_{15}=1$ | 174 ( 7) |"]
- result: Dimethyl ether population-diagram temperature, Sgr B2(N2b) = 127.2 ± 1.1; statistical standard deviation, possibly underestimated K [Table 7: "| CH$_3$OCH$_3$ | $\upsilon=0$, $\upsilon_{11}=1$, $\upsilon_{15}=1$ | 127.2 (1.1) |"]
- result: Dimethyl ether column density, Sgr B2(N1S) = 1.4 (18) ± not stated cm−2; x (y) means x × 10^y [Table 8: "| CH$_3$OCH$_3$, $\upsilon=0$ | d | 32 | 2.0 | 170 | 1.4 (18) | 1.02 | 5.0 | 0.0 | 14 |"]
- result: Dimethyl ether column density, Sgr B2(N2b) = 3.1 (18) ± not stated cm−2; x (y) means x × 10^y [Table 9: "| CH$_3$OCH$_3$, $\upsilon=0$ | d | 93 | 0.8 | 130 | 3.1 (18) | 1.00 | 3.5 | -0.5 | 26 |"]
- result: Tentative methylamine column density, Sgr B2(N2b) = 2.7 (17) ± not stated cm−2; x (y) means x × 10^y [Table 9: "| CH$_3$NH$_2$, $\upsilon=0$ | t | 2 | 0.5 | 140 | 2.7 (17) | 1.07 | 3.5 | 0.0 | 299 |"]
- result: DMA column-density upper limit, Sgr B2(N1S) = < 9.8 (16) ± not stated cm−2; x (y) means x × 10^y [Table 8: "| CH$_3$NHCH$_3$, $\upsilon=0$ | n | 0 | 2.0 | 170 | $<$ 9.8 (16) | 1.40 | 5.0 | 0.0 | $>$ 204 |"]
- result: DMA column-density upper limit, Sgr B2(N2b) = < 5.9 (16) ± not stated cm−2; x (y) means x × 10^y [Table 9: "| CH$_3$NHCH$_3$, $\upsilon=0$ | n | 0 | 0.8 | 130 | $<$ 5.9 (16) | 1.18 | 3.5 | 0.0 | $>$ 1360 |"]
- result: Methylamine/DMA abundance lower limit, Sgr B2(N1S) = at least 14 ± not stated dimensionless [Sgr B2(N): "dimethylamine is at least 14 times less abundant than methylamine toward this position."]
- result: Methylamine/DMA abundance lower limit, Sgr B2(N2b) = at least 4.5 ± not stated dimensionless [Sgr B2(N): "we can only say that dimethylamine is at least 4.5 times less abundant than methylamine toward this position."]
- result: Methanol/dimethyl ether abundance ratio, Sgr B2(N1S) = 14 ± not stated dimensionless [Sgr B2(N): "dimethyl ether is 14 times less abundant than methanol in Sgr B2(N1S)"]
- result: Methanol/dimethyl ether abundance ratio, Sgr B2(N2b) = 26 ± not stated dimensionless [Sgr B2(N): "Toward Sgr B2(N2b), dimethyl ether is 26 times less abundant than methanol"]
- result: DMA column-density upper limit, G+0.693 = < 7.6 × 10^13 ± 3σ upper limit in integrated area cm−2 [Nondetection of dimethylamine toward G+…: "Considering the noise of the data, we obtained a 3$\sigma$ upper limit in integrated area of $N<$ 7.6$\times$10$^{13}$ cm$^{-2}$"]
- result: DMA/H2 abundance upper limit, G+0.693 = < 5.6 × 10−10 ± not stated dimensionless [Nondetection of dimethylamine toward G+…: "which corresponds to an abundance compared to H$_2$ of $<$ 5.6$\times$10$^{-10}$"]
- result: Methylamine/DMA abundance lower limit, G+0.693 = >39 ± not stated dimensionless [Abstract: "dimethylamine is at least 14, 4.5 and 39 times less abundant than methylamine toward these sources, respectively."]
- limitation (authors): ALMA limits do not constrain whether the analogous formation routes operate differently. [Sgr B2(N): "This limit is not constraining enough to infer whether or not the formation routes that possibly relate methanol to dimethyl ether and methylamine to dimethylamine operate differently."]
- limitation (authors): Methylamine toward N2b is only tentatively detected. [Search for DMA toward Sgr B2(N1S) and S…: "As a result, we only claim a tentative detection of methylamine toward Sgr B2(N2b)."]
- limitation (authors): G+0.693 DMA emission falls at a local baseline minimum; its upper limit can change by less than a factor of two. [Nondetection of dimethylamine toward G+…: "Unfortunately, the predicted emission just appears in a spectral region which shows a local minimum in the data with respect to the adjusted broad band baseline."]
