### 2021A&A...646A..59R: Tracing molecular stratification within an edge-on protoplanetary disk (2021; protoplanetary_disks, astrochemistry; archival_reuse)
- question: Constrain CN formation pathways through its vertical distribution and physical conditions in the Flying Saucer disk.
- step 5: Measure CN hyperfine profiles and opacity indicators
  - method: Extract ellipse-aperture spectra where HF components are resolved; fit A–E with Gaussians; compare ratios to optically thin LTE predictions. [CN Line Profiles and Parameters: "presents the spectra extracted from ellipse-shaped apertures"] [CN Line Profiles and Parameters: "We used Gaussian fits to these line profiles to determine line widths and relative integrated intensities of the five HF components A–E."]
  - parameter: Radii >210 au on both east and west sides; ratios A/B, C/B, D/B and E/B [CN Line Profiles and Parameters: "The CN HF transitions are well resolved at radii larger than 210 au, on both the east and west sides of the disk."] [CN Line Profiles and Parameters: "the ratio of the intensity of the HF components relative to the brightest component (A/B, C/B, D/B, and E/B) would range between 0.1 and 0.7"]
  - assumption: Optically thin LTE ratios as reference; uniform excitation temperature for thick-line saturation [CN Line Profiles and Parameters: "Under optically thin local thermodynamic equilibrium (LTE) conditions"] [CN Line Profiles and Parameters: "Under optically thick conditions and for a uniform excitation temperature, the lines would saturate, reaching equal intensities in the limit of very large optical depth."]
  - fitting: Gaussian fits for line widths and relative integrated intensities [CN Line Profiles and Parameters: "We used Gaussian fits to these line profiles to determine line widths and relative integrated intensities of the five HF components A–E."]
  - validation: Compare measured HF ratios with theoretical ratios adapted from (cite: Punzi2015) [CN Line Profiles and Parameters: "as determined by the measured HF transition ratios shown in Table"] [CN Line Profiles and Parameters: "The relative intensities of HF transitions of CN are adapted from Table 2 of (cite: Punzi2015)."]
- step 6: Generate non-LTE CN spectral models
  - method: Build and interpolate a 3-D RADEX LVG grid; use predicted Tex and tau to synthesize and sum A, B and C profiles. [CN Column Densities: "we have modeled the CN HF line structure using a grid-based parameter exploration approach that exploits the non-LTE radiative transfer code RADEX under the so-called Large Velocity Gradient (LVG) framework (cite: vanderTak2007)."] [CN Column Densities: "we upsampled these RADEX 3D-grid models by interpolating between grid points to increase the resolution of the parameter space explored."]
  - software: RADEX, PySpecKit
  - parameter: 21 H2 densities: 10^4–10^9 cm^-3 [CN Column Densities: "21 values of H$_{2}$ density in the range $\rm n_{H_{2}}$ = 10$^{4}$ - 10$^{9}$ cm$^{-3}$"]
  - parameter: 21 CN columns: 10^11–10^17 cm^-2 [CN Column Densities: "21 values of CN column density in the range $\rm N_{CN}$ = 10$^{11}$ - 10$^{17}$ cm$^{-2}$"]
  - parameter: 51 kinetic temperatures: 5–60 K [CN Column Densities: "51 values of $\rm T_{kin}$ from 5 to 60 K."]
  - assumption: Fixed FWHM 0.4 km s^-1 for all three HF components [CN Column Densities: "The FWHM line width of all three HF components was held fixed at 0.4 km s$^{-1}$, which is the average of the individual line-widths derived"]
  - assumption: Background temperature 2.73 K; collision rates from (cite: HilyBlant2013) [CN Column Densities: "For the background temperature, we adopt 2.73 K."] [CN Column Densities: "using collision rates from (cite: HilyBlant2013)."]
  - assumption: Restrict modeling to spectrally resolved regions because RADEX does not treat line overlaps [CN Column Densities: "Since RADEX does not treat line overlaps, we restricted the spectral modeling to disk regions where these CN HF structures are well resolved"]
  - validation: A posteriori similar component excitation temperatures and modest A/B overlap [CN Column Densities: "This procedure is validated a posteriori because the excitation temperatures of each component are very similar and line overlap for the A and B components is modest"]
- limitation (authors): Characteristic emission heights should not be equated with physical disk scale heights; opacity affects interpretation. [Integrated Intensity Maps and Scale Hei…: "the emission from a given species only directly traces the disk density structure and abundance gradients if optically thin"]
- limitation (authors): Continuum, CS and CN vertical profiles are marginally resolved. [Integrated Intensity Maps and Scale Hei…: "For the continuum, CS, and CN, which are marginally resolved"]
- limitation (authors): CO is contaminated by background clouds near ~1.8 and 6–7 km s^-1. [Integrated Intensity Maps and Scale Hei…: "CO is contaminated by background emission from extended molecular clouds at velocities around $\sim$1.8 km s$^{-1}$ and in the range 6-7 km s$^{-1}$ (cite: Guilloteau2016)"]
- not stated (card): Spectral-window layout, channel selections and continuum bandwidth.; Line-free channels used for the first-order uv continuum fit.; Visibility flagging, calibrator identities and calibration-table selections.; Phase self-calibration solution intervals, reference antenna, acceptance criteria and transfer to line data.; TCLEAN deconvolver, masks, stopping threshold, iteration limit and primary-beam…

### 2019MNRAS.483..806R: First ALMA maps of HCO, an important precursor of complex organic molecules, towards IRAS 16293-2422 (2019; astrochemistry, star_formation; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Where and how is the formyl radical HCO formed in IRAS 16293-2422 (envelope gas-phase vs hot corino grain surface), and what is its role as precursor of H2CO, CH3OH and glycolaldehyde?
- step 1: Fit continuum level and derive N(H2)
  - method: Modified blackbody fit by visual inspection varying beta and tau_d0 over four spws; T_c fixed to CH3OH Tex; N(H2) from tau_d0 [7 Fit of the continuum level: "We tried multiple combinations of both parameters (some examples are shown in Figures [11](#fig-continuoA) and [12](#fig-continuoB)) and chose by visual inspection the solutions"]
  - software: MADCUBA
  - parameter: nu0=94 GHz; Tc=178 K (A), 181 K (B) [7 Fit of the continuum level: "assumed that $T_{\rm c}$ is equal to the excitation temperature derived from CH$_3$OH, 178 K and 181 K for sources A and B respectively"]
  - assumption: gas-to-dust 100; mu=2.8; kappa0 extrapolated from 0.009 cm2/g at 1 mm [3.1 Continuum: "In the calculation we assumed a gas-to-dust mass ratio of 100"]
  - fitting: Visual inspection; errors from 5% calibration plus spectral rms [7 Fit of the continuum level: "We assumed a flux density calibration error of $\pm$5$\%$"]
  - validation: Comparison with previous N(H2) estimates [3.1 Continuum: "These hydrogen column densities are in good agreement with previous estimates"]
- step 2: Measure source sizes and identify lines
  - method: 2D Gaussian fits for sizes; SLIM line identification with JPL/CDMS; Gaussian line fits [3.2.1 Identification: "The identification of the lines was performed using the SLIM (Spectral Line Identification and Modeling) tool of MADCUBA"]
  - software: MADCUBA-SLIM
  - parameter: Molecular emission size assumed equal to continuum: 0.88 arcsec (A), 0.44 arcsec (B) [3.2.1 Identification: "This gives 0.88$\ensuremath{''}$ for source A and 0.44$\ensuremath{''}$ for source B."]
  - assumption: Line emission unresolved, same size as continuum [3.2.1 Identification: "we will assume hereafter that the molecular emission/absorption share the same size of the continuum"]
- step 3: Derive column densities, Tex and abundances via LTE
  - method: MADCUBA-AUTOFIT LTE fits of emission and absorption components with Levenberg-Marquardt; abundances relative to N(H2) [3.2.3 Derivation of physical parameters: "it provides the best non-linear least-squared fit using the Levenberg-Marquardt algorithm."]
  - software: MADCUBA-AUTOFIT
  - parameter: Free: N, Tex, v, FWHM; HCO Tex assumed 50-180 K; absorption 10 K; H2CO Tex 100 K [3.2.3 Derivation of physical parameters: "we then assumed a range of temperatures typical of hot corinos, 50$-$180 K"]
  - assumption: LTE [3.2.3 Derivation of physical parameters: "we assumed Local Thermodynamic Equilibrium (LTE) conditions"]
  - fitting: Errors from covariance matrix diagonal (inverse Hessian) and final chi2 [3.2.3 Derivation of physical parameters: "The errors of the parameters left free are derived from the diagonal elements of covariance matrix, the inverse of the Hessian Matrix, and the final $\chi^2$ of the fit."]
  - validation: Simulated HCO spectra with random noise and channel resampling show weaker lines dilute below 2-3 sigma [3.2.3 Derivation of physical parameters: "the other transitions can be spectrally diluted and fall below 2$-$3$\sigma$ levels"]
- step 4: Compare ALMA with IRAM 30m spectra
  - method: Integrate ALMA over 28 arcsec IRAM beam and compare; fit extended HCO with LTE at 10 K [3.2.2 Comparison with single-dish data: "ALMA integrated in an area matching the IRAM 30m beam ($\sim$28$^{\prime\prime}$)"]
  - software: MADCUBA-AUTOFIT
  - assumption: Emission fills the telescope beam; Tex 10 K [3.2.3 Derivation of physical parameters: "We assumed that the emission fills the telescope beam"]
  - fitting: LTE AUTOFIT [Derivation of physical parameters: "The MADCUBA-AUTOFIT tool compares the observed spectra with the LTE synthetic spectra of the different species calculated following the previous expressions, taking into account all transitions, and it provides the best non-linear least-squared fit using the Levenberg-Marquardt algorithm."]
- result: Dust beta, source A = 0.9 ± ±0.4 dimensionless [3.1 Continuum: "$\beta$=0.9$\pm$0.4 and $\tau_{d0}$=0.09$\substack{+0.01 \\ -0.01}$ for source A"]
- result: Dust beta, source B = 0.0 ± ±0.5 dimensionless [3.1 Continuum: "$\beta$=0.0$\pm$0.5 and $\tau_{d0}$=2.1$\substack{+0.6 \\ -0.4}$ for source B"]
- result: N(H2) source A = 4.4e24 ± not stated cm-2 [3.1 Continuum: "We obtained N(H2)=4.4$\times$10$^{24}$ cm$^{-2}$ and N(H2)=5.0$\times$10$^{25}$ cm$^{-2}$ for A and B, respectively."]
- result: N(H2) source B = 5.0e25 ± not stated cm-2 [3.1 Continuum: "We obtained N(H2)=4.4$\times$10$^{24}$ cm$^{-2}$ and N(H2)=5.0$\times$10$^{25}$ cm$^{-2}$ for A and B, respectively."]
- result: HCO abundance source A = (0.06-0.36)e-8 ± not stated relative to H2 [3.2.3 Derivation of physical parameters: "For source A, the HCO abundance is (0.06$-$0.36)$\times$10$^{-8}$, for the range of excitation temperature considered."]
- result: HCO abundance source B = (0.01-0.06)e-8 ± not stated relative to H2 [3.2.3 Derivation of physical parameters: "For source B, the HCO abundance is (0.01$-$0.06)$\times$10$^{-8}$"]
- result: CH3OH Tex source B = 181 ± ±9 K [Table 3: "| CH$_3$OH | 181$\pm$9 | 2.4$\pm$0.1 | 1.5$\pm$0.1 | 282$\pm$10"]
- result: CH2OHCHO Tex source B = 157 ± ±14 K [3.2.3 Derivation of physical parameters: "We obtained a temperature of 157$\pm$14 K for source B"]
- result: Extended envelope HCO abundance = (1.5-2.9)e-11 ± not stated relative to H2 [3.2.3 Derivation of physical parameters: "which translates into an abundance of (1.5$-$2.9)$\times$10$^{-11}$"]
- limitation (authors): CH3OH and H2CO column densities are lower limits because lines are optically thick [3.2.3: "the lines are not optically thin, and our estimates of the CH$_3$OH column densities should be considered as strict lower limits"]
- limitation (authors): HCO Tex could not be determined since all four lines share Eup of 4 K [3.2.3: "We were not able to derive the excitation temperature of HCO because the 4 detected lines of HCO share the same $E_{\rm up}$, 4 K."]
- not stated (card): CASA version and tclean/imaging parameters (weighting, robust, cell, image size, deconvolver, masks); Which channels were line-free for continuum subtraction; Whether archive calibration products or manual scripts were used; Chemical network updates and rate coefficients (details deferred to Vasyunin et al. in prep.); Details of the TIMASSS data reduction and matching to ALMA integration area; MA…

### 2017A&A...608A..38O: Properties of the molecular gas in the fast outflow in the Seyfert galaxy IC 5063 (2017; nearby_galaxies, agn_and_quasars; observational_alma_primary)
- question: What are the excitation, optical depth, mass, density, temperature and pressure of the molecular gas in the jet-driven fast outflow of IC 5063, from multiple CO and HCO+ transitions?
- step 3: Excitation temperature and outflow mass
  - method: LTE optically thin Tex from R32; sum flux of pixels with R32 > 1.0; convert with alpha_CO; Mdot = 3vM/R [5.2 Mass estimates: "adding the flux from all 3-D pixels in the CO(2-1) cube, and similarly in the CO(3-2) cube, for which $R_{32}$ $> 1.0$"]
  - parameter: R = 0.5 kpc, v = 300 km/s [5.2 Mass estimates: "Using $R \sim 0.5$ kpc and $v = 300$$\,$km$\,$s$^{-1}$"]
  - assumption: optically thin, LTE, alpha_CO = 0.25 at Tex 29 K, spherical outflow [5.2 Mass estimates: "This assumes that the outflow has a spherical geometry."]
  - validation: Compared flux integral with earlier papers' 10.0 and 12.3 Jy km/s [5.2 Mass estimates: "compares well with the values of 10.0 Jy $\,$km$\,$s$^{-1}$ and 12.3 Jy $\,$km$\,$s$^{-1}$"]
- step 4: Non-LTE RADEX modelling of Tkin, density, pressure
  - method: Single-slab RADEX grids in Tkin and nH2 compared to line ratios for four regions, for a range of CO column densities [5.3 Kinetic temperature, density and pr…: "we modelled the data using a single slab (1-D) model with constant kinetic temperature and density"]
  - software: RADEX
  - parameter: Tkin 10-200 K (log steps 0.043); nH2 1e2-1e7 cm-3 (log steps 0.167); N(12CO) 5e15-1e20 cm-2 (log step 0.5); line width 100 km/s; Tbg 2.73 K [5.3 Kinetic temperature, density and pr…: "We set the background temperature to 2.73 K and the line width to 100 $\,$km$\,$s$^{-1}$."]
  - assumption: 12CO/13CO = 100; HCO+/12CO = 1.3e-4 [5.3 Kinetic temperature, density and pr…: "For the ratio $^{12}$CO/$^{13}$CO we have assumed 100."]
  - fitting: Graphical comparison of tracks of line ratios in Tkin-n plane; no formal fit [5.3 Kinetic temperature, density and pr…: "The quality of models can be assessed by to what extent the different lines in the panels of Fig. [12](#fig:f_radex) intersect in a common region."]
  - validation: Varied 12CO/13CO between 50 and 200 and HCO+ abundance; similar results [5.3 Kinetic temperature, density and pr…: "varying this ratio between 50 and 200 gives similar results, within the errors."]
- result: Excitation temperature from mean R32 (LTE, thin) = 29 ± not stated K [5.1 Excitation temperatures: "indicates an excitation temperature of $T_{\rm ex}$ = 29 K"]
- result: Highest pixel R32 and corresponding Tex = R32 ~1.65; Tex = 54 K ± not stated K [5.1 Excitation temperatures: "Such values correspond to $T_{\rm ex}$ = 54 K assuming optically thin emission and LTE."]
- result: Outflow flux integrals (R32>1.0) = CO(2-1) 9.5; CO(3-2) 25.3 ± not stated Jy km/s [5.2 Mass estimates: "The flux integrals we find are 9.5 Jy $\,$km$\,$s$^{-1}$ for the CO(2-1) line and 25.3 Jy $\,$km$\,$s$^{-1}$ for the CO(3-2) line."]
- result: Molecular outflow mass = 1.3e6 (CO 2-1), 1.1e6 (CO 3-2) ± likely lower limit, factor of a few larger Msun [5.2 Mass estimates: "$1.3 \cdot 10^6$ $M_\odot$ based on the CO(2-1) flux integral and $1.1 \cdot 10^6$ $M_\odot$ from the CO(3-2) data"]
- result: Mass outflow rate = ~12 ± or somewhat higher Msun/yr [5.2 Mass estimates: "gives a gas mass outflow rate of the about $\sim$12 $M_\odot$ yr$^{-1}$ or somewhat higher"]
- result: Pressure in jet-affected regions = 10^6 - 10^7.5 ± not stated K cm^-3 [5.3 Kinetic temperature, density and pr…: "The resulting pressures are in the range $10^6$–$10^{7.5}$ K cm$^{-3}$."]
- limitation (authors): Separation of outflowing and regular gas is uncertain, so masses are likely lower limits [5.2 Mass estimates: "the main uncertainty in the mass estimate comes from the uncertainties in the separation of regular and anomalous gas"]
- limitation (authors): Single-component model not appropriate for mixed gas; Tkin-n degeneracy [5.3 Kinetic temperature, density and pr…: "there is a degeneracy between them in the models"]
- not stated (card): Self-calibration solution intervals and number of iterations; Number of CLEAN iterations and cleaning thresholds; Taper parameters for the low-resolution HCO+ and 13CO cubes; Exact definition of regions FO, O, L, D (polygons in position-velocity space) and how line ratios and errors were extracted; Rest frequencies used and continuum subtraction/line-free channels; Method of chi-square or matchin…

### 2025A&A...698A.261B: Complex organic molecules towards the central molecular zone of NGC 253 (2025; astrochemistry, nearby_galaxies, star_formation; archival_reuse)
- question: Where do five selected O- and N-bearing iCOMs (acetaldehyde, ethanol, formamide, methylamine, methanimine) emit in the NGC 253 central molecular zone, what are their rotational temperatures and column densities, and do differences between …
- step 2: Extract spectra and fit Gaussian lines
  - method: Extract spectra from beam-sized regions centred on GMC 7, GMC 6, pSSC 5, pSSC 2; shift to LSRK of each transition; fit Gaussians to get integrated intensity, FWHM, Vpeak; reject lines contaminated at 15% or more [Spectra and line parameters: "We then centred each spectrum at the local standard of rest velocity (LSRK) corresponding to the rest frequency of each transition used in this work before performing a Gaussian line fitting to the detected transitions."] [Spectra and line parameters: "Transitions which were contaminated by more than 15% by a transition from another species (i.e., contributing to 15% or more of the total integrated intensity of our desired transition)"]
  - software: GILDAS CLASS, WEEDS
  - parameter: Detection threshold 3 sigma on the intensity peak [Spectra and line parameters: "A threshold of 3$\sigma$ on the intensity peak was set to consider a line to be detected."]
  - parameter: Spectral rms from zeroth-order polynomial fit to line-free channels [Observations: "For each spectrum, we fitted a zeroth order polynomial to the line-free channels from the continuum-subtracted data cubes to retrieve the information about the spectral root-mean-square (rms)."]
  - fitting: Single or multiple Gaussian line fits in CLASS (details of priors not stated) [Observations: "We used these same packages to extract the spectra and perform Gaussian line fitting."]
  - validation: Line contamination checked with the ALCHEMI line identification and WEEDS (JPL and CDMS) [Spectra and line parameters: "performed an additional inspection using WEEDS (cite: maret_2011), an extension of CLASS, which uses both the Jet Propulsion Laboratory (JPL; (cite: pickett_1998)) and the Cologne Database for Molecular Spectroscopy (CDMS; e.g. (cite: muller_2005; endres2016)) databases"]
- step 3: Derive T_rot and N_tot with rotational diagrams
  - method: Rotational diagram per species and region assuming LTE, optically thin emission and Rayleigh-Jeans; one or two temperature components; blended transitions split 50/50 [Methodology: "This method assumes Local Thermodynamic Equilibrium (LTE) and optically thin emission."] [Methodology: "We also assume the Rayleigh-Jeans approximation holds."]
  - parameter: Beam-filling factor 0.5 for GMC scales (1.6'') and ~0.01 for pSSC scales (0.12'') [Rotation diagrams: "The beam filling factor, (see Sec. [4.1](#subsec:methodology)), is 0.5 for GMC scales and $\sim 0.01$ for pSSC scales."]
  - parameter: Each of two blended transitions assigned 50% of total integrated intensity [Methodology: "we thus assumed that each transition accounts for 50% of the total line integrated intensity."]
  - parameter: Species showing a single component evaluated for both emission sizes (1.6'' and 0.12'') [Results from the rotational diagrams: "In these cases, since we do not know the region of emission of these species, we calculated column densities for the two emission sizes, i.e. for the GMC scale ($\theta_s=1.6\ensuremath{''}$) and the pSSC scale ($\theta_s=0.12\ensuremath{''}$)."]
  - assumption: Low-Eu component arises from GMC scales (1.6''), high-Eu component from pSSC scales (0.12'', ~2 pc) [Methodology: "we assumed that the first component, traced by the lower upper-level energy transitions, arises from the GMCs whilst the second component, traced by the higher upper-level energy transitions, arises from the pSSCs."]
  - assumption: Ratio of 1 between symmetry states for one species (symbol missing in the text), since kinetic temperature exceeds the ~0.1 K level difference [Methodology: "we assumed a / ratio of 1 since the kinetic temperature is higher than the energy difference of $\sim 0.1$ K between the levels of the different symmetry states (cite: matthews1985)"]
  - fitting: Best fit by minimizing the reduced chi-square; 15% calibration error and spectral rms included in integrated-flux uncertainties [Methodology: "The best fit was calculated by minimizing the reduced chi-square"]
  - validation: Second component justified by shifts in FWHM/Vpeak with Eu and by emission becoming more compact with Eu [Methodology: "Our choice is supported by the fact that higher upper-energy level transitions showed often a different FWHM or $V_\mathrm{peak}$ compared to the lower upper-energy transitions"]
- step 5: Non-LTE LVG analysis of methanimine
  - method: Grid of ~10,000 LVG models per region varying column density, H2 density and kinetic temperature, source size free, uniform sphere geometry [Non-LTE analysis: "For each region we ran a grid of models ($\sim 10,000$) varying the column density, the gas density and temperature within $10^{13}-10^{18}$ , $10^4-10^8$ cm$^{-3}$, and $10-150$ K, respectively."] [Non-LTE analysis: "We left the source size as a free parameter. Then, for the geometry, we chose a uniform sphere (cite: osterbrock1974) as the most physically realistic choice."]
  - software: grelvg
  - parameter: Lines with Eu >= 27 K used; the two lowest-Eu lines (225.554, 172.267 GHz) and the 312.336 GHz line excluded [Non-LTE analysis: "We thus ran the LVG with the rest of the lines ($E_\mathrm{u}\geq 27$ K)."]
  - parameter: Linewidth fixed at 50 km/s (average across regions) [Non-LTE analysis: "the average linewidths of chosen is 50 km s$^{-1}$, which is the average linewidth across the regions"]
  - parameter: 15% calibration uncertainty in the integrated intensity [Non-LTE analysis: "We also included a 15% calibration uncertainty in the integrated intensity"]
  - assumption: Ortho-to-para ratio set to the statistical value of 3 [Non-LTE analysis: "The ortho-to-para ratio of was set to the statistical value of 3."]
  - fitting: Grid search with chi-square; 1 sigma confidence levels correspond to a 68% range [Non-LTE analysis: "The $1\sigma$ confidence levels constrained correspond to a 68% confidence range."]
  - validation: Initial fit with all lines gave chi2_r>5 (GMC 6, pSSC 5, pSSC 2) so low-Eu lines were dropped; population diagrams then used to test remaining non-LTE effects [Non-LTE analysis: "However, this resulted in a bad fit ($\chi^2_r>5$) for GMC 6, pSSC 5, and pSSC 2."]
- step 6: Correct rotational diagram for opacity and source size (population diagram)
  - method: Apply population diagram method using LVG optical depths and emission size to see whether opacity dominates the deviations [Non-LTE analysis: "We corrected the rotation diagram for the optical depth and source size for all the regions using the population diagram method (cite: goldsmith_1999), taking the output values of the LVG analysis for these parameters"]
  - validation: Corrected diagrams still deviate from a straight line, with T_rot below the LVG minimum gas temperature, so non-LTE effects are present [Non-LTE analysis: "Therefore, is also affected by non-LTE effects."]
- result: Beam-filling factor, GMC scale (1.6'') and pSSC scale (0.12'') = 0.5 (GMC); ~0.01 (pSSC) ± not stated dimensionless [Rotation diagrams: "The beam filling factor, (see Sec. [4.1](#subsec:methodology)), is 0.5 for GMC scales and $\sim 0.01$ for pSSC scales."]
- result: Rotational temperatures, cold and warm components = cold <=20 K; warm ~30-80 K; GMC 7 only cold; all T_rot below 100 K, majority 10-40 K ± not stated K [Results from the rotational diagrams: "On the other hand, the other regions have two components, a cold ($T_{\text{rot}}\leq 20$ K) and a warmer component ($T_{\text{rot}}\sim 30-80$ K)."]
- result: Typical FWHM of the cold gas component of the iCOMs = mostly 40-60 ± not stated km/s [Possible origins of the iCOMs in the CM…: "On average, we measured line widths mostly around $40-60$ km s$^{-1}$(see Table [3](#tab:av_GF)) for the cold gas component (component 1) of the iCOMs."]
- result: Methanimine LVG best-fit column density, GMC 7 / GMC 6 / pSSC 5 / pSSC 2 (x1e16) = 1.5 / 1.5 / 2 / 2 ± ranges 0.7-4.5 / 0.7-4 / 0.2-5 / 0.2-5 (68%) 1e16 (column density, unit symbol missing in converted text) [Non-LTE analysis: "$1.5$ | $0.7-4.5$ | $1.5$ | $0.7-4$ | $2$ | $0.2-5$ | $2$ | $0.2-5$"]
- result: Methanimine LVG best-fit n(H2), GMC 7 / GMC 6 / pSSC 5 / pSSC 2 (x1e5) = 0.6 / 8 / 1.7 / 3 ± ranges 0.5-1 / 3-60 / 1-10 / 0.7-20 1e5 cm^-3 [Non-LTE analysis: "$0.6$ | $0.5-1$ | $8$ | $3-60$ | 1.7 | $1-10$ | $3$ | $0.7-20$"]
- result: Methanimine LVG kinetic temperature, GMC 7 / GMC 6 / pSSC 5 / pSSC 2 = 110 / 45 / 150 / 33 ± >=45 / 25-130 / >=30 / 20-100 and >=120 (limited by 150 K rate-grid maximum) K [Non-LTE analysis: "110 | $\geq 45$ | 45 | $25-130$ | 150 | $\geq 30$ | 33 | $20-100$ ; $\geq 120$"]
- result: Methanimine LVG emission source size, GMC 7 / GMC 6 / pSSC 5 / pSSC 2 = 0.20 / 0.26 / 0.19 / 0.26 (best fits 0.19-0.26'', ~3-4 pc diameter) ± ranges 0.15-0.24 / 0.16-0.34 / 0.17-0.60 / 0.22-0.60 arcsec [Non-LTE analysis: "0.20 | $0.15-0.24$ | $0.26$ | $0.16-0.34$ | 0.19 | $0.17 - 0.60$ | $0.26$ | $0.22-0.60$"]
- result: Methanimine LVG reduced chi-square, GMC 7 / GMC 6 / pSSC 5 / pSSC 2 = 0.3 / 1.3 / 1.4 / 1.7 ± not stated dimensionless [Non-LTE analysis: "0.3 | 1.3 | 1.4 | 1.7"]
- limitation (authors): Spectral resolution (10 km/s) is insufficient to separate blended E/A states and limits identification of gas components [Spectra and line parameters: "the E- and A- states are often completely blended together, the spectral resolution of 10 km s$^{-1}$ being insufficient to disentangle the two states."]
- limitation (authors): Derived T_rot are not true gas temperatures because rotational diagrams deviate from LTE fits [Results from the rotational diagrams: "Due to the fact that we observe deviations from the fits in the RDs, it is clear that the derived $T_{\text{rot}}$ are not representative of the true gas temperature."]
- limitation (authors): Two-component treatment assumes the warm compact component contributes negligibly to the cold extended one [Rotational Diagrams: "As a caveat, this implies that the contribution from the warm compact component to the cold extended one is negligible."]
- not stated (card): Exact ALCHEMI cube versions/file names and how to obtain the processed cubes (public availability of the processed cubes is not stated).; Imaging parameters of the ALCHEMI cubes (weighting, deconvolver, 7m+12m combination, line-free channels for continuum subtraction) are deferred to the ALCHEMI summary paper.; Line-free channel ranges and velocity windows used for the per-spectrum rms and baseli…

### 2018MNRAS.480.4991G: ALMA imaging of the nascent planetary nebula IRAS 15103-5754 (2018; evolved_stars; observational_alma_primary)
- question: What are the morphology and kinematics of the circumstellar structure of the nascent PN IRAS 15103-5754, and what physical processes operate at the beginning of the PN phase?
- step 3: Torus geometry, kinematics and mass
  - method: Integrated intensity maps and PV diagrams along major/minor axes; C18O LTE mass; HCO+ parameters [3.2 Line emission: an expanding torus: "The estimated mass of the structure is 0.4-1.0 M ⊙ assuming local thermodynamic equilibrium (LTE) with a temperature of 60-180 K"]
  - parameter: Tex 60-180 K; 12CO/H2 = 3e-4; 16O/18O ~330 [3.2 Line emission: an expanding torus: "a [16] O/ [18] O ratio of ≃ 330"]
  - assumption: LTE, optically thin C18O [3.2 Line emission: an expanding torus: "Because the C [18] O(2-1) line is optically thinner, it can provide a better estimate of the mass of the torus."]
  - validation: Parameters consistent within errors among all lines [3.2 Line emission: an expanding torus: "the obtained geometric and kinematic parameters are consistent, within the errors, for all observed lines."]
- step 4: Model HCO+ emission from expanding torus
  - method: Radiative transfer model of an LTE expanding torus with power-law temperature (Appendix A), compared qualitatively with PV diagrams [3.2 Line emission: an expanding torus: "We have modelled the HCO [+] emission from an expanding torus, as described in Appendix A."]
  - parameter: Tmax 180 K, Tmin 60 K, turbulence >~2 km/s, HCO+ abundance ~1e-7 [3.2 Line emission: an expanding torus: "for a torus with a maximum (inner) temperature of 180 K, and a minimum (outer) temperature of 60 K."]
  - validation: Model PV diagrams visually compared to observed ones [3.2 Line emission: an expanding torus: "the resulting positionvelocity diagrams (Fig. 5) reasonably resemble the observed ones (Fig. 4)"]
- step 5: CO and HCO+ outflow masses
  - method: LTE optically thin CO(2-1) and (3-2) for high-velocity gas (>30 km/s); HCO+ outflow mass assuming abundance 1e-7 [3.3.1 A biconical outflow: "Assuming LTE and optically thin emission in both CO transitions for the high-velocity gas, we obtain a total mass of 0.03 − 0.10 M ⊙ for the outflow"]
  - assumption: Tex 60-180 K [3.3.2 A high-density outflow: "assuming excitation temperatures 60-180 K"]
  - validation: Could not obtain consistent Tex from CO(3-2)/CO(2-1), suggesting non-LTE [3.3.1 A biconical outflow: "We could not obtain consistent values of excitation temperatures from the ratio CO(3-2)/CO(2-1) at different velocities"]
- result: Torus mass (C18O, LTE) = 0.4-1.0 ± not stated Msun [3.2 Line emission: an expanding torus: "The estimated mass of the structure is 0.4-1.0 M ⊙"]
- result: Torus expansion velocity (deprojected) = 23 ± not stated km/s [3.2 Line emission: an expanding torus: "we obtain a deprojected expansion velocity of 23 km s [−][1]"]
- result: Torus mean diameter = 0.6 (2000 au) ± not stated arcsec [3.2 Line emission: an expanding torus: "We obtain a torus mean diameter of ≃ 0.6 arcsec (2000 au at 3.38 kpc)"]
- result: Torus dynamical age = 200 ± not stated yr [3.2 Line emission: an expanding torus: "we estimate a dynamical age of 200 yr for the expanding torus."]
- result: CO high-velocity outflow mass = 0.03-0.10 ± not stated Msun [3.3.1 A biconical outflow: "we obtain a total mass of 0.03 − 0.10 M ⊙ for the outflow"]
- result: HCO+ outflow mass = 0.02-0.04 ± not stated Msun [3.3.2 A high-density outflow: "We obtain an estimate of 0.02-0.04 M ⊙ for the HCO [+] outflow"]
- limitation (authors): Mass and mass-loss estimates are very uncertain; distance is a single uncertain literature estimate [3.1 Continuum emission: "our estimates of mass and mass-loss rate are very uncertain, as they depend on several parameters that are not well constrained"]
- limitation (authors): Torus model too simple for close reproduction; no detailed fit [3.2 Line emission: an expanding torus: "Our model of HCO [+] emission is only intended as an illustration of the general geometric characteristics of the torus"]
- not stated (card): cell size and image size; clean masks, threshold and number of iterations; line-free channel ranges for continuum; self-calibration solution intervals and number of iterations; flux scale uncertainty; uv-range/taper details beyond the 300 kilolambda cut; free parameter values (density, HCO+ profile exponents) of the torus model; fitting procedure used for the biconical model (done by eye?); metho…

### 2014ApJ...793....1Y: ALMA Observations of Infalling Flows toward the Keplerian Disk around the Class I Protostar L1489 IRS (2014; star_formation, protoplanetary_disks; observational_alma_primary)
- question: How does infalling envelope gas connect to the Keplerian disk around the Class I protostar L1489 IRS, and what are the disk and infall properties?
- step 2: Fit thin Keplerian disk to high-velocity C18O
  - method: chi2 fitting of axisymmetric geometrically-thin LTE disk model to convolved channel maps, staged free parameters [Analysis: "we have built axisymmetric disk models with the geometrically-thin approximation, generated model C$^{18}$O images, and performed $\chi^2$ fitting to the observed channel maps of the high-velocity ($|V| \gtrsim 2.5$ km s$^{-1}$) C$^{18}$O emission."]
  - parameter: Fit region central 2''x2'', velocities -6.1 to -2.5 and 2.5 to 7.1 km/s; Rd fixed 200 AU [Analysis: "We select the central 2$\ensuremath{''}$ $\times$ 2$\ensuremath{''}$ region and the velocity ranges of $-$6.1 to $-$2.5 and 2.5 to 7.1 km s$^{-1}$"]
  - assumption: d=140 pc, LTE, X(C18O)=3e-7, Gaussian line profile [Analysis: "$d$ is the distance of 140 pc"]
  - fitting: Grid chi2 minimization with staged parameter sets; uncertainty from delta reduced chi2 = 1 with others fixed [Analysis: "we adopt the parameter range that gives a reduced $\chi^2$ of 1+$\chi^2_{\rm min}$ as the fitting uncertainty"]
  - validation: Tested different initial p and q; residuals mostly <=3 sigma [Analysis: "The levels of of residuals are all $\lesssim$4$\sigma$ and mostly $\lesssim$3$\sigma$"]
- step 4: Flared disk and SO ring models
  - method: 3D flared disk (h0=25 AU, b=1.35) from best-fit; SO ring scaled C18O cube by SO/C18O ratio ~0.6 between 250-390 AU; compared via P-V diagrams [Analysis: "we simply scaled the model C$^{18}$O image cubes by the SO/C$^{18}$O intensity ratio ($\sim$0.6) in the radial range of the ring"]
  - software: RADEX
  - assumption: LTE, isotropic T=44(r/100AU)^-0.3 K [Analysis: "$T(r) = 44 \cdot (r/100\ {\rm AU})^{-0.3}$ K for simplicity"]
  - validation: Visual comparison of model and observed P-V diagrams [Analysis: "The linear velocity gradient seen in the SO emission as well as the intensity peaks at positional offsets of $\sim$$\pm$2$\ensuremath{''}$ and velocities of $\sim$$\pm$2 km s$^{-1}$ can be reproduced with our ring model."]
- step 6: Infall mass, rate and angular momentum
  - method: C18O flux at medium velocity gives mass (LTE, Tex 10-40 K); divide by infall time ~1e4 yr; compare specific angular momentum sqrt(GM Rc)/sin theta0 with sqrt(GM Rd) [Discussion: "the mass of the infalling flows is estimated to be 4–7 $\times$ 10$^{-3}$ $M_\ensuremath{\odot}$, assuming the LTE condition, excitation temperature of the C$^{18}$O emission of 10–40 K"]
  - assumption: X(C18O)=3e-7 [Discussion: "the C$^{18}$O abundance of 3 $\times$ 10$^{-7}$ (Frerking et al. 1982)"]
  - validation: Missing flux 20-50% noted; order-of-magnitude estimate [Discussion: "this estimate of the mass of the infalling flows should be regarded as just an order estimate"]
- result: Protostellar mass = 1.6 ± 0.5 Msun [Table 1: "Protostellar Mass ($M_*$) | 1.6$\pm$0.5 $M_\ensuremath{\odot}$"]
- result: Inclination = 66 ± 10 deg [Table 1: "Inclination ($i$) | 66$\pm$10$\ensuremath{^\circ}$"]
- result: Systemic velocity = 7.3 ± 0.1 km/s [Table 1: "Systemic Velocity ($V_{\rm sys}$) | 7.3$\pm$0.1 km s$^{-1}$"]
- result: Disk position angle = 68 ± 18 deg [Table 1: "Position Angle ($\psi$) | 68$\pm$18$\ensuremath{^\circ}$"]
- result: SO ring radii = 250 to 390 ± not stated AU [Analysis: "the inner and outer radii of the SO ring are roughly estimated to be $r$ $\sim$ 250 and 390 AU"]
- result: Infall mass = 4–7e-3 ± not stated Msun [Discussion: "the mass of the infalling flows is estimated to be 4–7 $\times$ 10$^{-3}$ $M_\ensuremath{\odot}$"]
- result: Mass infalling rate = 4–7e-7 ± not stated Msun/yr [Discussion: "the mass infalling rate onto the Keplerian disk in L1489 IRS is estimated to be $\sim$4–7 $\times$ 10$^{-7}$ $M_\ensuremath{\odot}$ yr$^{-1}$"]
- result: Infall specific angular momentum = 2.6e-3 ± not stated km/s pc [Discussion: "$\sim$ 2.6 $\times$ 10$^{-3}$ km s$^{-1}$ pc$^{-1}$ with $R_{\rm c}$ of 300 AU"]
- result: Keplerian disk specific angular momentum (Rd=700 AU) = 4.8e-3 ± not stated km/s pc [Discussion: "the specific angular momentum of the Keplerian disk is estimated to be 4.8 $\times$ 10$^{-3}$ km s$^{-1}$ pc$^{-1}$"]
- limitation (authors): Missing flux (~80% of C18O) hinders outer disk radius; p, q, T0, vturb poorly constrained [Analysis: "it is difficult to unambiguously constrain the outer radius of the Keplerian disk due to the effect of the missing flux ($\sim$80%; Hogerheijde et al. 1998)"]
- not stated (card): CLEAN threshold, mask, cell and image size, number of iterations; Line-free channels used for continuum and continuum subtraction; CASA version used; Exact channel width and velocity grid of imaged cubes; Whether the fit used a fixed beam-convolved model and the pixel/likelihood noise definition; Model grid details for infall flows (widths, n1 choice procedure); Whether primary beam correction ap…

### 2020MNRAS.496.5308S: ALMA observations of CS in NGC 1068: chemistry and excitation (2020; nearby_galaxies, agn_and_quasars, astrochemistry; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Disentangle the effects of the AGN and SB on the gas near the centre of NGC 1068.
- step 4: Calculate LTE CS column densities
  - method: Calculate N_u from integrated line intensity, Einstein A-coefficient, filling factor and optical-depth correction; convert each transition to total CS column density with the Boltzmann relation. [LTE analysis: "N_u = \frac{8\pi k\nu^2W}{hc^3A_{ul}}\left(\frac{\Delta\Omega_a}{\Delta\Omega_s}\right)C_\tau"] [LTE analysis: "N = \frac{N_uZ(T)}{g_ue^{\frac{-E_u}{kT}}}"]
  - parameter: Temperatures: 15, 50, 100, 150, 200, 250 K [Table 2: "| EKnot | 15 | 11 | 8.5 | 8.8 | 19 |"] [Table 2: "| | 50 | 31 | 17 | 3.4 | 3.4 | | | 100 | 59 | 29 | 4.1 | 3.5 |"]
  - assumption: Source fills beam; filling factor unity; all sub-region emission traces the same gas [LTE analysis: "We also initially assume that the source completely fills the beam such that the beam filling factor is unity and that all emission within a sub-region traces the same gas."]
  - assumption: Optically thin; optical-depth correction unity [LTE analysis: "We have assumed here that the gas is optically thin, setting the optical depth correction factor"] [LTE analysis: "to unity."]
  - validation: Compare column densities inferred from different transitions; disagreement indicates at least one failed assumption. [LTE analysis: "For a given temperature all of the transitions should theoretically give the same value for the column density in a sub-region; however, this is not the case here."] [LTE analysis: "This suggests that at least one of the assumptions made is incorrect."]
- step 5: Fit rotational diagrams and test opacity
  - method: Fit ln(N_u/g_u) versus upper-level energy; infer rotational temperature from negative inverse slope; iterate opacity corrections; also fit low-J and high-J pairs separately. [Rotation diagram: "A rotational diagram is a plot of the natural logarithm of column density over statistical weight of each transition against the energy in kelvin of the upper energy level"] [Rotation diagram: "The negative inverse slope of this line gives the rotational temperature"]
  - parameter: Column-density errors: band 3 10%; bands 4 and 7 15% [Rotation diagram: "The error bars were obtained by assuming an error of 10% on the transition column densities for transitions observed in band 3, and 15% for bands 4 and 7 observations."]
  - parameter: Cτ = τ/(1 - e^-τ); iterate until stable [Rotation diagram: "C_\tau = \frac{\tau}{1 - e^{-\tau}}"] [Rotation diagram: "This process is repeated iteratively until a stable value is found for $C_\tau$."]
  - assumption: Initially optically thin; rotational temperature equals kinetic temperature only for thermalised, optically thin levels [Rotation diagram: "Using the values of $N_u$ calculated under the optically thin assumption"] [Rotation diagram: "The rotational and kinetic temperature are only equivalent if all levels are thermalised and optically thin."]
  - fitting: Straight-line single-phase and separate low-J/high-J fits [Rotation diagram: "If the assumption of LTE is correct the data can be fit by a straight line."] [Rotation diagram: "line fits were also obtained for the CS(2-1) and CS(3-2) transitions only, and similarly for CS(6-5) and CS(7-6) only."]
  - validation: Cτ ≈ 1 immediately; diagrams unchanged; compare inferred columns with Boltzmann columns at the nearest kinetic temperature. [Rotation diagram: "We immediately find $C_\tau \approx 1$ for each sub-region, such that the values calculated from our new rotational diagrams remain unchanged, and so we cease iterating."] [Rotation diagram: "calculated for that sub-region at the kinetic temperature closest to the obtained rotational temperature."]
- step 6: Fit non-LTE excitation with RADEX
  - method: Grid H2 density, CS column density and kinetic temperature; compare predicted main-beam temperature to observed peak temperature using reduced χ². [RADEX: "By varying the H$_2$ density, CS column density and kinetic temperature in the model, a grid of $\sim 85~000$ points was produced"] [RADEX: "comparing the main beam temperature predicted ($T_{mb}$) to the peak temperature ($T_{peak}$) observed using the reduced $\chi^2$ test."]
  - software: RADEX
  - parameter: H2 density 10^3 - 10^7 cm^-3; CS column density 10^12 - 10^18 cm^-2; kinetic temperature 10-200 K [RADEX: "H$_2$ density: $10^3 - 10^7$ cm$^{-3}$"] [RADEX: "CS column density: $10^{12} - 10^{18}$ cm$^{-2}$"]
  - parameter: Line widths: CND 150 km s^-1; SB ring 50 km s^-1 [RADEX: "This was taken to be 150 km s$^{-1}$ within the CND and 50 km s$^{-1}$ within the SB ring, where the transitions appear narrower."]
  - parameter: Constraint contours: 3χ²_min; NSB-c 1.5χ²_min; AGN χ² = 3 [RADEX: "For each sub-region, the contour corresponding to a value of $3\chi_{min}^2$ is plotted"] [RADEX: "The NSB-c and AGN sub-regions are exceptions to this, with the contour instead placed at $1.5\chi_{min}^2$ and $3$"]
  - assumption: Uniform sphere escape probability [RADEX: "Testing found that the geometry used had negligible impact on the results, and so the uniform sphere geometry was arbitrarily chosen."]
  - assumption: LAMDA molecular data; H2 collisions; background 2.73 K [RADEX: "The molecular data files used in the simulation were obtained from the LAMDA database (cite: LAMDA), using H$_2$ as the collision partner."] [RADEX: "The background temperature in each sub-region was set equal to the cosmic background, 2.73 K"]
  - assumption: Impose 50 K CO rotational temperature as upper bound when constraining other parameters [RADEX: "Thus we choose to use the CO rotational temperature of 50 K derived by (cite: Viti) as an upper bound for the temperatures when constraining the other parameters."]
  - fitting: Reduced-χ² grid search; fixed-variable slices use the minimum-χ² value [RADEX: "using the reduced $\chi^2$ test."] [RADEX models: "In each case, the value of the fixed variable is chosen by taking the value that gives the lowest $\chi^2$ in each sub-region."]
  - validation: Test sphere, LVG and slab geometries; extend temperature grid to 1,000 K; upper temperatures remain unconstrained. [RADEX: "Three different options are available for the applied escape probability method, that of a uniform sphere, an expanding sphere (LVG) or a plane parallel slab."] [RADEX: "Testing found that the geometry used had negligible impact on the results"]
- result: Single-phase rotational temperatures across sub-regions = 13-20 ± not stated K [Conclusions: "predicts rotational temperatures of $13-20$ K for all of the sub-regions with the AGN providing the highest value."]
- result: Rotation-diagram E Knot CS column density = ~10^14 ± not stated cm^-2 [Rotation diagram: "Looking at the CS column density, it appears largest in the E Knot at $\sim10^{14}$ cm$^{-2}$ and lowest in NSB-c at $5\times 10^{12}$ cm$^{-2}$."]
- result: Rotation-diagram NSB-c CS column density = 5 × 10^12 ± not stated cm^-2 [Rotation diagram: "Looking at the CS column density, it appears largest in the E Knot at $\sim10^{14}$ cm$^{-2}$ and lowest in NSB-c at $5\times 10^{12}$ cm$^{-2}$."]
- result: High-J rotational temperatures where all four transitions detected = ~30-50 ± not stated K [Rotation diagram: "In sub-regions where all 4 transitions were detected the upper J fits give temperatures ($\sim$ 30-50 K) more in line with those observed for CO."]
- result: Low-J rotational temperatures = ~5-15 ± not stated K [Rotation diagram: "The lower J fits gives higher CS column densities than the upper J in all of the sub-regions and temperatures ($\sim$ 5-15 K)"]
- result: Opacity correction factor in each sub-region = ≈1 ± not stated dimensionless [Rotation diagram: "We immediately find $C_\tau \approx 1$ for each sub-region"]
- result: RADEX AGN log10(n_H [cm^-3]) = >6.5 ± not stated dimensionless [RADEX: "| AGN | $>6.5$ | $\sim14$ | 34 | 0.99 |"]
- result: RADEX NSB-c log10(n_H [cm^-3]) = <4 ± not stated dimensionless [RADEX: "| NSB-c | $<4$ | $15-16$ | 50 | 1.00 |"]
- limitation (authors): Only 3-4 transitions per sub-region limit modelling reliability. [Conclusions: "currently only 3-4 transitions are available for each which limits the reliability of our modelling efforts."]
- limitation (authors): Rotational diagrams cannot reliably constrain conditions or distinguish two phases from sub-thermal excitation. [Rotation diagram: "Due to the low number of transitions in the rotational diagrams, we are unable to use these results to reliably constrain the conditions in each of the sub-regions."]
- limitation (authors): RADEX temperatures have unconstrained upper bounds. [RADEX: "We found the upper bound of the temperature in each sub-region to be unconstrained within this temperature range."]
- not stated (card): Calibration scripts, pipeline/manual status, calibrator identities and any self-calibration procedure.; Imaging task, weighting/robust, deconvolution, image grid, clean mask and stopping threshold.; Continuum-subtraction procedure and channels, or an explicit statement that none was applied.; Exact spectral extraction aperture or pixel sampling for each listed position.; Gaussian-fitting implemen…

### 2015ApJ...798...85P: CO Gas Inside the Protoplanetary Disk Cavity in HD 142527: Disk Structure from ALMA (2015; protoplanetary_disks; observational_alma_primary)
- question: How much gas survives inside the dust-depleted cavity of the HD 142527 disk, and what are the gas cavity size, wall shape and inclination?
- step 3: Optical depth from isotopologue ratios
  - method: Isothermal slab line ratio in high-velocity channels (<1.8 and >5.4 km/s) [3.3 Optical Thickness: "In this velocity range, the isotopic line ratio $R$ has a value of 2.0$\pm$0.2, which yields optical depths of"]
  - assumption: Same excitation temperature, CMB neglected, 12CO/13CO=76, 12CO/C18O=500 [3.3 Optical Thickness: "We also assume that the two selected lines share the same excitation temperature"]
- step 4: Gas mass in cavity from optically thin 13CO and C18O
  - method: LTE column density from summed peak emission in high-velocity channels, converted to H2 mass [3.5: "We measured the integrated line intensity of $^{13}{\rm CO}$ by summing the peak emission from the selected high velocity channels"]
  - parameter: T=43 K, d=140 pc, mu=1.36 [3.5: "For the gas temperature we used $T=43$ K, as calculated from $^{12}{\rm CO}$ in the previous section."]
  - assumption: [H2]/[12CO]=1e4, [12CO]/[13CO]=76, LTE [3.5: "Assuming a fiducial CO interstellar abundance of $[{\rm H}_2]/[\mbox{$^{12}{\rm CO}$}]=10^4$ and $[\mbox{$^{12}{\rm CO}$}]/[\mbox{$^{13}{\rm CO}$}]=76$"]
  - validation: Cross-check with archival C18O J=3-2 [3.5: "consistent with the $J=2-1$ estimate (5-$\sigma$ error bars)"]
- step 5: Forward model gas disk with LIME
  - method: Parametric axisymmetric disk, LIME non-LTE radiative transfer, model visibilities sampled at observed uv, chi2 comparison; scan of M_star and inclination, then cavity radius [4.2 Modeling Results: "Our models were compared to the data in the visibility plane based on $\chi^2$ statistics."]
  - software: LIME, CASA
  - parameter: h=0.1 r, gamma=1, R_c=200 au, T=50 K (r/50au)^-0.3, b=50 m/s, inner truncation 0.3 au [4.1: "The velocity dispersion of the random motions of the gas (Doppler b-parameter) was fixed to 50 m s$^{-1}$."]
  - assumption: Keplerian rotation, ISM CO abundance, vertically isothermal, axisymmetric [4.1: "For simplicity, we assumed that the gas in the disk follows circular Keplerian rotation"]
  - fitting: Reduced chi2 grid in M_star and inclination; cavity radius by visual inspection [4.2 Modeling Results: "The error was obtained from visual inspection of the plotted models against the $^{13}{\rm CO}$ data."]
  - validation: Visual comparison of best model with 13CO and C18O maps; SED consistency check with MCFOST [4.2 Modeling Results: "Finally, the best fit models were contrasted with the data by visual inspection."]
- result: Inclination = 28 ± 0.5 deg [Table 2: "$i$ | deg | 28 | 0.5"]
- result: Gas cavity radius = 90 ± 5 au [4.2 Modeling Results: "The best fit for the cavity radius in the gas is 90$\pm5$ au."]
- result: Gas depletion factor delta_gap = 0.02 ± 0.01 dimensionless [4.2 Modeling Results: "We estimate an uncertainty of 0.01 based on the optical thickness of the lines."]
- result: Gas mass in cavity from 13CO = 1.7e-3 ± 0.6e-3 Msun [3.5: "$M_{\rm gas} = (1.7 \pm 0.6)\times 10^{-3}$ M$_\odot$"]
- result: Isotopic line ratio R = 2.0 ± 0.2 dimensionless [3.3: "the isotopic line ratio $R$ has a value of 2.0$\pm$0.2"]
- result: Optical depth 12CO = ~56 ± not stated dimensionless [3.3: "$\tau_{\scriptstyle \rm {12}CO} \sim 56$ and $\tau_{\scriptstyle \rm {13}CO} \sim 0.7$"]
- result: Total disk mass from model = 0.1 ± not stated Msun [4.2: "yields a total (dust plus gas) mass of the disk of 0.1 M$_{\odot}$"]
- limitation (authors): Gas mass is a crude estimate, dominated by assumed isotopic ratios and temperature [3.5: "The main sources of uncertainty are the assumed isotopic line ratios and the gas temperature."]
- not stated (card): Briggs robust value, cell size, image size, CLEAN masks and threshold; Line-free channels used for continuum subtraction; Whether data were self-calibrated; CASA version; LIME settings (number of grid points, photons, collision data) and model grid ranges; How the high-velocity channel peaks were selected and summed for 13CO; Solid angle Omega adopted for the cavity; Model grids for M_star and in…

### 2019ApJ...878..113H: Modeling the Spatial Distribution and Origin of CO Gas in Debris Disks (2019; debris_disks, astrochemistry; archival_reuse)
- question: Where is the CO gas located in three CO-rich debris disks, and is it primordial or of secondary (cometary) origin?
- step 2: Simple CO mass limits
  - method: Optically thin LTE mass, LTE critical-density mass and photodissociation shielding estimates [Estimate of optically thin LTE mass lim…: "A simple CO mass estimate can be made by assuming that the emission is in LTE and optically thin."]
  - assumption: disk thickness 10 au, radius ~200 au [Estimate of mass required for LTE: "Assuming a constant disk thickness of 10 au"]
- step 3: Thermochemical modelling of primordial and secondary gas and compare to ALMA
  - method: Thermochemical disk models with double power-law surface density, LIME non-LTE line cubes, simobserve and CASA imaging, fit by varying mass and slopes [Modeling gas of primordial origin: "The model images were processed through the CASA task simobserve to create synthetic visibilities"]
  - software: LIME, CASA
  - parameter: primordial mass grid 0.1-35 Earth masses in 5 steps, then 0.1 steps; p1,p2 varied manually in -5 to +5, fine step 0.05 [Modeling gas of primordial origin: "The surface density exponents p$_1$ and p$_2$ were varied manually between -5 and +5 using different step sizes, and then fine-tuned using steps of 0.05."]
  - assumption: secondary: CO:H2O 1:10 released, gas T = dust T, run to ~2 Myr; primordial: ISM abundances, CR rate 1e-16 s-1, X-ray 1e27 erg/s, 1% dust outside belt [Modeling gas of secondary origin: "we assume that at the start of the simulation CO and H$_2$O are released in a 1:10 ratio"]
  - fitting: manual grid fit to integrated spectra and channel maps [Modeling gas of primordial origin: "We initially fit the integrated flux by varying the total disk mass in the range 0.1 - 35M$_{\oplus}$ in steps of 5M$_{\oplus}$, to narrow down the mass range."]
  - validation: compared integrated spectra and channel maps with data [Modeling gas of primordial origin: "The integrated spectra for the best fit models are compared to the $^{12}$CO(2–1) data in Figure [7](#intspec-pa)."]
- result: CO masses, primordial models (HD 131835, HD 138813, HD 156623) = 5.0e-3, 4.2e-3, 3.8e-3 ± not stated Earth masses [Modeling gas of primordial origin: "total CO masses of $5.0\times 10^{-3}$,$4.2\times 10^{-3}$ and $3.8\times 10^{-3}$ M$_{\oplus}$"]
- result: CO masses, cometary models = 4.6e-3, 3.1e-3, 2.45e-3 ± not stated Earth masses [Modeling gas of secondary origin: "The best fit cometary gas masses are $4.6 \times 10^{-3}, 3.1\times 10^{-3}$ and $2.45\times 10^{-3}$ M$_{\oplus}$"]
- limitation (authors): Single CO transition cannot break mass-temperature degeneracy [Modeling gas of primordial origin: "this degeneracy cannot be broken with only one CO transition observed."]
- not stated (card): CASA version, calibration and imaging parameters (robust, weighting, masks) used for the data (inherited from Lieman-Sifry 2016); Stellar luminosity temperature/disk model details for synthetic LIME setup (grid, photons); Prior ranges and walker initialization for emcee; Thermochemical code availability and exact dust grain parameters; Observing dates, configurations and integration times

### 2024A&A...681A.117P: The CO-to-H<SUB>2</SUB> conversion factor of molecular outflows. Rovibrational CO emission in NGC 3256-S resolved by JWST/NIRSpec (2024; nearby_galaxies, agn_and_quasars; multi_facility_alma_minor)
- question: Can the CO-to-H2 conversion factor (alpha_CO) of the cold molecular outflow in NGC 3256-S be derived from the JWST/NIRSpec rovibrational CO v=1-0 band, with ALMA rotational CO used as ancillary data?
- step 4: Extract aperture spectra, subtract nuclear PSF, baseline and fit CO, 13CO, C18O, H2, HI and D-PAH
  - method: Scaled WebbPSF nuclear contribution subtracted; spline baseline subtracted; 13CO and C18O bands modelled from P-branch fluxes assuming optically thin emission and subtracted; then 12CO band fitted together with H2, HI and D_n-PAH Gaussians [3.3 Spectra from regions with CO v=1-0 …: "We calculated this contribution at each wavelength using point-spread functions (PSFs) created with WebbPSF (v1.1.1; (cite: Perrin2014))."] [3.3.1 Continuum baseline: "we defined a spline-interpolated baseline."]
  - software: WebbPSF
  - parameter: Spline pivot points at 4.395, 4.530, 4.586, 4.667, 4.845, 4.980 micron [3.3.1 Continuum baseline: "The pivot points (4.395, 4.530, 4.586, 4.667, 4.845, and 4.980"]
  - parameter: 13CO/C18O abundance ratio ~7.4 from 13CO(2-1)/C18O(2-1) (ALMA, r=1 arcsec aperture) [3.3.2 Optically thin 13CO and C18O v=1-…: "abundance ratio of $\sim$7.4"]
  - assumption: 13CO and C18O v=1-0 emission optically thin; same excitation temperatures for both [3.3.2 Optically thin 13CO and C18O v=1-…: "share the same excitation temperatures and have abundance ratio of"]
  - fitting: Single Gaussian per CO transition; two Gaussians for N1 and N2 where a broader second component appears in the residuals [3.3.5 12CO v=1-0 band: "Two regions (N1 and N2) show a second broader kinematic component in the residuals."]
  - validation: Predicted 13CO R-branch fluxes from P-branch agree with observed spectra in all regions [3.3.2 Optically thin 13CO and C18O v=1-…: "agree with the observed spectra for all the regions including region S3"]
- step 5: Fit P(J+2)/R(J) ratios with a single non-LTE component and derive alpha_CO
  - method: RADEX grid of v=0 populations, v=1 populations assumed proportional (N1,J = k N0,J), line fluxes from radiative transfer equations, fit to low-J ratios, then convert N(H2)/W(CO 1-0) to alpha_CO [4.1.3 Non-LTE models: "First, we used RADEX (cite: vanderTak2007) to determine the CO"] [5 CO-to-H2 conversion factor: "Then, the ratio $N({\rm H_2})$/$W$(CO(1–0)), known as $X_{\rm CO}$, is converted to $\alpha_{\rm CO}$"]
  - software: RADEX
  - parameter: Grid: 50 log steps per parameter, 125000 models [4.1.3 Non-LTE models: "50 log steps for each parameter (125 000 models in total)"]
  - assumption: [CO/H2] = 3.2e-4 [6 Discussion: "We assumed a standard CO abundance of 3.2$\times$10$^{-4}$ (e.g., (cite: Bolatto2013))"]
  - assumption: Mass multiplied by 1.36 for helium [5 CO-to-H2 conversion factor: "to account for helium and other elements (cite: Bolatto2013)"]
  - fitting: chi2 fit to the low-J ratios; the highest J included is where chi2 does not significantly increase; 4-6 ratios fitted per region [5.1 Single component model: "This single component model can fit between four and six ratios."]
  - validation: Compared model r21 and r31 with measured ALMA r21 [5.1 Single component model: "with a mean ratio of 0.88 $\pm$ 0.15, that is in good agreement with the models."]
- step 6: Fit two non-LTE components to all ratios
  - method: chi2 over all pairs of grid models with only the solid-angle ratio free; probability exp(-chi2/2) marginalized for each parameter; alpha_CO weighted by CO(1-0) intensity of each component [5.2 Two component model: "In total, we explored 56 million combinations of models for each region."] [5.2 Two component model: "To determine the likelihood function of the parameters, we assigned a probability to each model of"]
  - software: RADEX
  - parameter: Only nH2 = 1e3, 1e4, 1e5 cm^-3 considered [5.2 Two component model: "To limit the number of combinations, we only considered three number densities"]
  - fitting: Grid search chi2 with likelihood exp(-chi2/2) and marginalization; no sampler [5.2 Two component model: "To determine the likelihood function of the parameters, we assigned a probability to each model of"]
  - validation: With two components all observed ratios can be fitted [5.2 Two component model: "With two components, it is now possible to fit all the observed ratios."]
- step 7: Derive alpha_CO from the optically thin 13CO v=1-0 band
  - method: Use 13CO (optically thin) and 12CO (optically thick) R(0) transitions to get N(12CO, v=0, J=0)/dv, extrapolate to J=1 and other levels with the single-component excitation temperatures, convert FWHM to sigma_v with extra opacity factor, th… [5.3 Optically thin 13CO v=1-0 band: "we first divided $\Delta {\rm v}$ by $\sim$2.355 to account for the ratio between FWHM and $\sigma$."] [5.3 Optically thin 13CO v=1-0 band: "by an extra factor between 1.2 and 2.6 (valid for"]
  - parameter: Excitation temperature of low-J levels 15-30 K from the single-component fits [5.3 Optically thin 13CO v=1-0 band: "based on the excitation temperature of the low-$J$ levels, 15–30 K, of these regions"]
  - assumption: Same excitation temperature of v=1 and v=0 levels and same solid angle for 12CO and 13CO; 12CO/13CO abundance uncertain [5.3 Optically thin 13CO v=1-0 band: "the excitation temperature of the $v$=1 and $v$=0 levels is the same for $^{12}$CO and $^{13}$CO"]
  - validation: Compared with single and two component results (1.1-2.1 and 1.7-3.2 times lower) [5.3 Optically thin 13CO v=1-0 band: "The median $\alpha_{\rm CO}$ are 1.1–2.1 times lower than those derived using the single component non-LTE model"]
- result: alpha_CO, single non-LTE component, median (mean) over regions, scaled by 3.2e-4/[CO/H2] = 0.40 (mean 0.50) ± 0.15 (mean 0.30) Msun (K km/s pc^2)^-1 [5.1 Single component model: "with a median (mean) of 0.40 $\pm$ 0.15 (0.50 $\pm$ 0.30)"]
- result: alpha_CO, two non-LTE components, median over regions, scaled by 3.2e-4/[CO/H2] = 0.61 ± 0.17 Msun (K km/s pc^2)^-1 [5.2 Two component model: "with a median value of 0.61 $\pm$ 0.17."]
- result: alpha_CO from 13CO band, median range, scaled by [3.2e-4/[CO/H2]] x [12CO/13CO]/60 = 0.19-0.36 ± not stated Msun (K km/s pc^2)^-1 [5.3 Optically thin 13CO v=1-0 band: "with a median range of 0.19–0.36."]
- result: Mean Tkin of cold component (two-component fit) = 17 ± not stated K [5.2 Two component model: "with a mean $T_{\rm kin}$ of 17 K"]
- result: Mean Tkin of warmer component (two-component fit) = 30 ± not stated K [5.2 Two component model: "with a mean $T_{\rm kin}$ of 30 K"]
- result: Outflow gas temperature gradient from the P(2)/R(0) map = >40 K at the central 100 pc to <15 K at ~250 pc ± not stated K [5.1 Single component model: "the molecular gas cools down from $>$40 K at the central 100 pc to $<$15 K at $\sim$250 pc."]
- result: Model r21 and r31 (mean, single component) = r21 = 1.08, r31 = 0.97 ± 0.09 and 0.16 dimensionless [5.1 Single component model: "The mean ratios are 1.08 $\pm$ 0.09 and 0.97 $\pm$ 0.16, respectively."]
- result: 13CO/C18O abundance ratio = ~7.4 ± not stated dimensionless [3.3.2 Optically thin 13CO and C18O v=1-…: "abundance ratio of $\sim$7.4"]
- limitation (authors): The CO abundance in molecular outflows is not established and is a major uncertainty for alpha_CO [6 Discussion: "The CO abundance in molecular outflows has not been firmly established yet, so this is a major uncertainty for these results."]
- limitation (authors): 13CO-based alpha_CO is more uncertain because it depends on 12CO and 13CO abundances in the outflow [5.3 Optically thin 13CO v=1-0 band: "are more uncertain than those derived using the single and two component methods"]
- not stated (card): CASA tasks and parameters used for ALMA imaging (tclean deconvolver, weighting/robust, cell and image size, masks, uv-range, threshold) are not given; only 'following Pereira2021'; Whether ALMA archive products or a manual reduction script were used, and the CASA pipeline version, are not stated; Moment-map clipping and integration velocity range for the zeroth moment maps are in SanchezGarcia202…

### 2019A&A...623A..68T: Small-scale physical and chemical structure of diffuse and translucent molecular clouds along the line of sight to Sgr B2 (2019; milky_way_ism, astrochemistry; archival_reuse)
- question: What is the spatial and kinematic structure, physical nature and chemistry of diffuse and translucent molecular clouds seen in absorption along the line of sight to Sgr B2(N)?
- step 2: decompose c-C3H2 absorption into velocity components
  - method: Automatic fitting of Weeds synthetic spectra with Gaussian opacity components per pixel using MCWeeds and Powell minimisation; fitted N_tot, FWHM, v0 [3.3 Modelling: "we use the fitting routine MCWeeds (Giannetti et al. 2017), which combines the python package PyMC2 (Patil et al. 2010) and Weeds (Maret et al. 2011)."] [3.4 Minimisation method: "We use a modified version of this method ( fmin_powell from scipy 3 ) with MCWeeds."]
  - software: Weeds, MCWeeds, PyMC2, scipy, GILDAS CLASS
  - parameter: detect dips >4 sigma; four velocity ranges; velocity prior uniform ±3 km/s; FWHM max 30 km/s [Appendix B: "Search in the continuum-subtracted spectrum for absorption dips with a peak signal-to-noise ratio higher than 4"]
  - assumption: LTE, beam filling factor 1 [5.4 Cloud sizes: "This means that our assumption of a beam filling factor of 1 to fit the spectra is reasonable."]
  - fitting: Powell local minimisation, MCWeeds priors: uniform velocity, truncated normal FWHM, normal log N width 2 [Appendix B: "the probability distribution for the velocity is uniform over the chosen interval"]
  - validation: residual peaks above 5 sigma then 4 sigma searched for missing components [Appendix B: "The programme looks for peaks above a threshold of 5σ in the residuals."]
- step 6: determine cloud nature (H2, Av, density)
  - method: Fit HCO+/H13CO+ absorption towards K4 with Weeds; N(H2) from HCO+ abundance 3e-9; Av=N(H2)/9.4e20; test against Mopra emission with RADEX non-LTE grids [4.7 Nature of the detected line-of-sigh…: "We perform non-LTE radiative transfer calculations with RADEX (van der Tak et al. 2007) to estimate the densities and kinetic temperatures"]
  - software: Weeds, RADEX
  - parameter: Tkin 10–130 K, nH2 10–1e7 cm-3; Mopra Tmb = 1.7 × TA* [4.7 Nature of the detected line-of-sigh…: "10–130 K for the kinetic temperature and 10–10 [7] cm [−][3] for the H 2 density"]

### 2021MNRAS.500.5500B: A warm molecular ring in AG Car: composing the mass-loss puzzle (2021; evolved_stars, stellar_physics, astrochemistry, milky_way_ism; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Confirm and characterize the spatial distribution, kinematics, physical parameters, and origin of the molecular gas around the luminous blue variable star AG Car.
- step 5: Extract single-dish line intensities, line ratios, and excitation conditions via non-LTE RADEX modelling
  - method: Compute beam-averaged single-dish spectra at the stellar position, smooth to 1.3 km s^-1, fit Gaussians to components C1-C4 in CLASS, compute beam-dilution-corrected line ratios R_32 and R_12/13, and minimize reduced chi^2 over a RADEX non… [Physical parameters of the gas: "Then, we smoothed the resulting averaged spectra to a common velocity resolution of 1.3 km s$^{-1}$, and we attempted a gaussian fitting on components C1...C4 with CLASS, as shown in Fig. \[fig:spec\]."] [Physical parameters of the gas: "To model the excitation of the CO and $^{13}$CO lines, we employed the non-LTE radiative transfer code RADEX (cite: 2007A&A...468..627V)."]
  - software: CLASS, RADEX
  - parameter: Grid: T_k = 10 to 200 K, N(CO) = 10^13 to 10^19 cm^-2, n(H2) = 10^2 to 10^5 cm^-3; CMB = 2.73 K; representative FWHM = 6 km s^-1 [Physical parameters of the gas: "The grid covered $T_\mathrm{k}$ from 10 to 200 K, $N\mathrm{(CO)}$ from 10$^{13}$ to 10$^{19}$ cm$^{-2}$ and $n(\mathrm{H_2})$ from 10$^2$ to 10$^5$ cm$^{-3}$."] [Physical parameters of the gas: "We used H$_2$ as the only collision partner, with a cosmic background temperature of 2.73 K."]
  - assumption: Each component C_i has the same FWHM across all three transitions; homogeneous medium; beam-filling factors F <= 0.2 at 230.538 GHz and F <= 0.38 at 345.799 GHz for C4 (no beam dilution correction for C1-C3) [Physical parameters of the gas: "Therefore, we imposed the condition that every velocity component C$_i$ should have a same FWHM in each of the three lines, so that the derived line ratios were consistent."] [Physical parameters of the gas: "We adopt very conservative estimates of $F\leq$0.2 and $F\leq$0.38 at 230.538 and 345.799 GHz respectively, assuming an emitting area comparable to the ACA beam or smaller."]
  - fitting: Multi-Gaussian spectral line fitting in CLASS; reduced chi^2 minimization over RADEX grid [Physical parameters of the gas: "Then, we smoothed the resulting averaged spectra to a common velocity resolution of 1.3 km s$^{-1}$, and we attempted a gaussian fitting on components C1...C4 with CLASS, as shown in Fig. \[fig:spec\]."] [Physical parameters of the gas: "Once we constrained the parameter space, we followed a reduced $\chi^2$ minimisation technique to find the model that better reproduces the observed line intensities, such that"]
  - validation: Cross-checked best-fit RADEX parameters by predicting 13CO J=2-1 intensity to derive N(13CO) = 7.5x10^14 cm^-2 and an isotopic ratio of ~30, matching observed R_12/13. [Physical parameters of the gas: "As a cross check, we used the derived parameters to predict the $^{13}$CO $J=2\rightarrow1$ integrated intensity, for a range of $^{13}$CO column densities."] [Physical parameters of the gas: "The best match is obtained for $N(^{13}\mathrm{CO}) = 7.5\times10^{14}$ cm$^{-2}$, which yields an isotopic ratio of $\sim$ 30."]
- step 7: Model 3D ring kinematics and radiative transfer with LIME
  - method: Construct four 3D non-LTE radiative transfer models in LIME (rotation, expansion, expansion+rotation, expansion+turbulence), convolve synthetic CO J=2-1 cubes to the ACA beam, regrid, and qualitatively compare channel maps and integrated i… [A closer view to CO kinematics: "To do so, we used LIME (cite: 2010A&A...523A..25B), a 3D non-LTE code that solves radiative transfer by simulating ballistic photon propagation through unstructured Delaunay grids, calculating level populations and predicting the resulting spectra for a given transition, according to the LAMDA database collisional coe…"] [A closer view to CO kinematics: "For each of the models, LIME produced a synthetic CO $J=2\rightarrow1$ cube, which was later convolved to the ACA beam and re-gridded to allow for a direct comparison with the original data."]
  - software: LIME
  - parameter: Inner radius = 0.3 pc, half opening angle = 10 deg, inclination = 70 deg, PA = 135 deg; v_phi = 1 km s^-1, v_exp = 3.5 km s^-1, v_turb = 0.25 km s^-1 [A closer view to CO kinematics: "Regarding the geometrical parameters, we adopted an inner radius of 0.3 pc, a half opening angle of 10$^\circ$, an inclination of 70$^\circ$ and a position angle of 135$^\circ$ east of north."] [Table 4: "3 | Expansion + rotation | $v_\phi = 1$ km s$^{-1}$, $v_\mathrm{exp} = 3.5$ km s$^{-1}$"]
  - assumption: Axisymmetric ring at d = 6 kpc with r^-2 power-law density and temperature profiles anchored to RADEX outputs [A closer view to CO kinematics: "Due to the modest spatial and spectral resolution of our data, we made several assumptions to keep the model as simple as possible; we supposed an axisymmetric ring located at 6 kpc and described by power-law density and temperature profiles of the form $r^{-2}$."] [A closer view to CO kinematics: "Therefore, we adopted the density and temperature outputs of RADEX as reference values for the power-law profiles."]
  - fitting: Qualitative visual comparison of channel maps and integrated intensity maps (no formal fitting) [A closer view to CO kinematics: "To avoid overinterpreting our maps, instead of fitting the observed data, we qualitatively compared the overall morpho-kinematic features in the structure with those obtained with different kinematic models."]
  - validation: Checked that LIME synthetic integrated fluxes agree with observed fluxes within ~10%, validating the RADEX density and temperature estimates. [A closer view to CO kinematics: "Indeed, we found the integrated fluxes to be consistent within a $\sim$10$\%$, thus confirming that the average values for density and temperature obtained with RADEX are able to reproduce the observed intensities."]
- result: Line ratio R_32 (CO 3-2 / CO 2-1) in the ring (C4) = 0.45 ± 0.07 dimensionless [Physical parameters of the gas: "In the ring (component C4) we measure a $R_\mathrm{32} = 0.45\pm0.07$."]
- result: Line ratio R_12/13 (CO 2-1 / 13CO 2-1) in the ring (C4) = 25 ± 4 dimensionless [Physical parameters of the gas: "On the other hand, we measure a $R_\mathrm{12/13}$ of $25\pm4$ in C4."]
- result: Ring (C4) CO J=2-1 integrated intensity (W) = 4.79 ± 0.24 K km s^-1 [Table 2: "C4 | 33.7 | 5.7 | 0.79 | 4.79 (0.24) | 33.7 | 5.7 | 0.03 | 0.19 (0.03) | 33.7 | 5.7 | 0.67 | 4.09 (0.62)"]
- result: Ring (C4) 13CO J=2-1 integrated intensity (W) = 0.19 ± 0.03 K km s^-1 [Table 2: "C4 | 33.7 | 5.7 | 0.79 | 4.79 (0.24) | 33.7 | 5.7 | 0.03 | 0.19 (0.03) | 33.7 | 5.7 | 0.67 | 4.09 (0.62)"]
- result: Ring (C4) CO J=3-2 integrated intensity (W) = 4.09 ± 0.62 K km s^-1 [Table 2: "C4 | 33.7 | 5.7 | 0.79 | 4.79 (0.24) | 33.7 | 5.7 | 0.03 | 0.19 (0.03) | 33.7 | 5.7 | 0.67 | 4.09 (0.62)"]
- result: Best-fit kinetic temperature of the ring (T_k) = 50 ± not stated K [Physical parameters of the gas: "We find that the model that best fits the observations is that with $T_\mathrm{k}$ = 50 K, $N(\mathrm{CO})$ = 2.4$\times10^{16}$ cm$^{-2}$ and $n\mathrm{(H_2)}$ = 1.3$\times10^3$ cm$^{-3}$."]
- result: Best-fit CO column density of the ring N(CO) = 2.4e16 ± not stated cm^-2 [Physical parameters of the gas: "We find that the model that best fits the observations is that with $T_\mathrm{k}$ = 50 K, $N(\mathrm{CO})$ = 2.4$\times10^{16}$ cm$^{-2}$ and $n\mathrm{(H_2)}$ = 1.3$\times10^3$ cm$^{-3}$."]
- result: Best-fit H2 volume density of the ring n(H2) = 1.3e3 ± not stated cm^-3 [Physical parameters of the gas: "We find that the model that best fits the observations is that with $T_\mathrm{k}$ = 50 K, $N(\mathrm{CO})$ = 2.4$\times10^{16}$ cm$^{-2}$ and $n\mathrm{(H_2)}$ = 1.3$\times10^3$ cm$^{-3}$."]
- result: Best-fit 13CO column density of the ring N(13CO) = 7.5e14 ± not stated cm^-2 [Physical parameters of the gas: "The best match is obtained for $N(^{13}\mathrm{CO}) = 7.5\times10^{14}$ cm$^{-2}$, which yields an isotopic ratio of $\sim$ 30."]
- result: Optical depth of CO J=2-1 in the ring (tau_21 at 50 K) = 1.2 ± not stated dimensionless [Physical parameters of the gas: "The solution points to a moderately thick opacity in the emitting medium, with optical depths of $\tau_{21}=1.2$ and $\tau_{32}=0.8$ for this temperature range."]
- result: Optical depth of CO J=3-2 in the ring (tau_32 at 50 K) = 0.8 ± not stated dimensionless [Physical parameters of the gas: "The solution points to a moderately thick opacity in the emitting medium, with optical depths of $\tau_{21}=1.2$ and $\tau_{32}=0.8$ for this temperature range."]
- limitation (authors): Large angular distance (~4 deg) to the ALMA TP off position caused spectral ripples that were not removed in post-processing. [ALMA observations: "Such a large angular distance between the target and the off position caused ripples in the spectra, which we did not attempt to remove in post-processing, since their amplitude is negligible with respect to the brightness of the spectral lines from our target."]
- limitation (authors): Fainter arcs of the ring are not detected in 13CO J=2-1 due to limited sensitivity. [CO and 13CO emission: "In the $^{13}$CO $J=2\rightarrow1$ integrated intensity map (not shown), only the lobes of the structure are visible, with an S/N ratio slightly above 5 $\sigma$."]
- limitation (authors): ACA is likely missing a fraction of the extended 225 GHz radio continuum flux because its largest angular scale is ~1/3 that of the ATCA 750B configuration at 5.5 GHz. [Radio continuum emission: "We note, though, that the LAS (largest angular scale) of ACA at 225 GHz is about 1/3 of the LAS of ATCA 750B configuration at 5.5 GHz, so it is very likely that we are missing a fraction of the extended flux."]

- question: Obtain a more generalised understanding of the chemistry in C-rich AGB circumstellar envelopes by studying three carbon stars (IRAS 15194-5115, IRAS 15082-4808, IRAS 07454-7112) and observationally test the archetypal status of IRC +10 216.
- step 1: Continuum imaging and subtraction, spectral extraction, and line identification
  - method: Image continuum from line-free channels across 85-116 GHz using multi-frequency synthesis, subtract continuum from visibilities, extract spectra from 12.5'' radius circular apertures centred on continuum peaks (and molecule-specific apertu… [4.1 Line detection and identification: "Continuum maps were produced from the line-free channels across the survey bandwidth (85-116 GHz) using multifrequency synthesis (Rau & Cornwell 2011)."] [4.1. Line detection and identification: "The full line-labelled ALMA Band 3 spectra of the three sources, extracted by integrating the intensity in a circular aperture of 12.5'' radius centred on the respective continuum peaks, so as to recover emission from all molecules, are presented in App. A."]

  - parameter: Survey aperture radius = 12.5''; molecule-specific aperture radius = R_p + 2.5 * HWHM; detection threshold = 3 sigma [4.1.   - software: CDMS

  - fitting: Linear fit to ln(N_u / g_u) versus E_u / k_B. [Appendix E.1: The population diagram me…: "The rotational temperature and column density of each molecule are calculated from the slope and intercept, respectively, of a linear fit to such a plot."]
  - validation: Compared derived T_rot against literature values for IRC +10 216. [5. Discussion: "The rotation temperatures we estimate from our population diagrams match reasonably well with those estimated for IRC +10 216."]
- step 6: Calculate molecular fractional abundances and isotopic ratios
  - method: Convert N_tot from population diagrams to fractional abundance f_X = n(X)/n(H2) using Eq. E.6 and AARP radii; for species without population diagrams, compute f_X analytically from the highest-SNR single line using Eq. E.7; derive isotopic… [4.5. Abundance estimates: "For the species for which we could produce population diagrams (see Table 6 and App. E), we calculated fractional abundances from the corresponding column densities, using Eq. For all other species, the isotopic ratio is determined by calculating the corresponding ′ line intensity ratios."]

Column density and rotation temper…: "Based on these results, we use rotational temperatures of 40 K and 20 K for centrally-peaked and shell species, respectively, in the abundance estimates based on single lines in Sect. E.3."]
  - assumption: Isotropic, constant mass-loss rate CSE expanding at terminal velocity with r^-2 density distribution; constant fractional abundance between R_i and R_e and zero outside; optically thin lines for single-line estimates; identical excitation … [Appendix E: Column densities and abunda…: "The results in this section are based on a simple model for the CSE, assuming an isotropic, constant mass-loss rate and the gas expanding at its terminal velocity."] [Appendix E.3: Abundance calculation fro…: "In such cases, we obtain a rough estimate of the fractional abundance, using the highest signal-to-noise line available, by assuming it to be constant in the radial range R i to R e and zero outside it."]
