### 2014A&A...570A..28V: Molecular line emission in NGC 1068 imaged with ALMA. II. The chemistry of the dense molecular gas (2014; nearby_galaxies, agn_and_quasars, astrochemistry; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Are the different line intensity ratios within the CND, and between the CND and the SB ring, due to excitation effects (density, temperature) or to a different chemistry?
- step 2: LTE column densities per transition and region
  - method: Boltzmann relation between upper-level and total column density for a grid of kinetic temperatures, assuming optically thin emission [3.1 LTE analysis: "We estimate the column densities of each species in Local Thermodynamic Equilibrium (LTE)."] [3.1 LTE analysis: "If optically thin, and assuming a filling factor of unity"]
  - parameter: Tk 50 to 250 K reported (calculated 10 to 300 K); 150 K adopted for the ALMA ratio comparison [3.1 LTE analysis: "(from 50 to 250 K, although the LTE calculations were performed from 10 to 300 K)"] [3.1 LTE analysis: "Therefore taking an intermediate kinetic temperature of 150 K"]
  - assumption: All transitions optically thin, so column densities are lower limits [3.1 LTE analysis: "Note that we have made the implicit assumption that all our transitions are optically thin."]
  - validation: Comparison of column densities from different transitions of the same species shows inconsistencies [3.1 LTE analysis: "then the same total column density at a specific temperature should be obtainable from each transition. This is clearly not the case"]
- step 3: CO rotation diagram per CND region
  - method: Plot ln(Nu/gu) versus Eu/k for four CO transitions and fit a line to get rotational temperature and CO column density [3.1.1 CO rotation diagram: "The rotation diagram is a plot of the natural logarithm of $N_u/g_u$ versus $E_u/k$"]
  - parameter: Level column density errors 10% (first two), 15% (J=3-2), 30% (highest J) [3.1.1 CO rotation diagram: "the error on the level column densities are 10% for the first two transitions, 15% for the $J$=3–2 and 30% for the highest $J$"]
  - assumption: LTE, optically thin lines, Rayleigh-Jeans regime [3.1.1 CO rotation diagram: "making the assumptions that LTE conditions hold, that the lines are optically thin, and that we are mostly in the Rayleigh-Jeans regime"]
  - fitting: Single-component line fit; two-component fit also tried for AGN and CND-S [3.1.1 CO rotation diagram: "the AGN and CND-S regions may be better represented by a two component fit (not shown)"]
  - validation: Rotational temperatures treated as lower limits to kinetic temperature [3.1.1 CO rotation diagram: "The rotational temperatures derived from the rotation diagrams ought to be considered as lower limits of the excitation and kinetic temperatures (cite: Gold99)."]
- step 5: RADEX grid with multi-species ratios
  - method: Vary density and temperature for seven fixed sets of CO, HCO+, HCN and CS column densities per line width and fit all independent ratios [3.2.2 A RADEX grid computed with multi-…: "We have varied the gas density and temperature as in our first RADEX grid in Section 3.2.1 for several molecular column density ratios"]
  - software: RADEX
  - parameter: over 10000 models [3.2.2 A RADEX grid computed with multi-…: "(resulting in a grid of over 10000 models)"]
  - parameter: H12CN/H13CN = 18 and H12CO+/H13CO+ = 24 for all regions [3.2.2 A RADEX grid computed with multi-…: "ratios of, respectively, 18 and 24 (cite: Papa99; User04) for each region within the CND"]
  - assumption: Column-density sets guided by LTE column densities; sets are not exhaustive [3.2.2 A RADEX grid computed with multi-…: "they were guided by the range of column densities derived in LTE"]
  - fitting: Log of reduced chi2 minimised over the grid [3.2.2 A RADEX grid computed with multi-…: "for this RADEX grid we shall report results from the log of the reduced $\chi^2$ fitting"]
  - validation: Density lower limit of 10^4 cm^-3 from the CO analysis used to discard sets [3.2.2 A RADEX grid computed with multi-…: "we therefore use this lower limit as a further constraint and we are left with Sets 1 and 7"]
- result: CO rotational temperature, E Knot / W Knot / AGN / CND-N / CND-S = 58 / 41 / 50 / 52 / 37 ± 5-7 K K [Conclusions: "rotational temperatures: 58 K, 41 K, 50 K, 52 K, and 37 K for the E Knot, W Knot, AGN, CND-N and CND-S, respectively, with typical errors of 5-7 K"]
- result: Two-component CO rotational temperatures, AGN and CND-S = AGN 25 and 67; CND-S 15 and 54 ± not stated K [3.1.1 CO rotation diagram: "which gives 25 K and 67 K for the AGN region, and 15 K and 54 K for the CND-S region"]
- result: CO column density from rotation diagram, AGN / W Knot / E Knot = 3e16 / 5e16 / 7e16 ± not stated cm^-2 [3.1.1 CO rotation diagram: "the AGN, the W knot and the E Knot CO column densities are respectively 3$\times$10$^{16}$, 5$\times$10$^{16}$ and 7$\times$10$^{16}$ cm$^{-2}$"]
- result: LTE N(CO)/N(HCN) at 150 K from CO(3-2) and HCN(4-3), E Knot to W Knot = ~2700 (E Knot) to >5000 (W Knot) ± not stated dimensionless [3.1 LTE analysis: "varies from $\sim$ 2700 in the E Knot to $>$ 5000 in the W Knot"]
- result: LTE N(CO)/N(HCO+) at 150 K from CO(3-2) and HCO+(4-3) = E Knot ~11,700; AGN ~13,700; W Knot ~23,300 ± not stated dimensionless [3.1 LTE analysis: "is lowest in the E Knot ($\sim$ 11,700) and AGN ($\sim$ 13,700) and highest in the W Knot ($\sim$ 23,300)"]
- result: LTE N(CO)/N(CS) from CO(6-5) and CS(7-6) = AGN and CND-S ~5500-6000; E Knot 33,000 ± not stated dimensionless [3.1 LTE analysis: "lowest for the AGN and the CND-S ($\sim$ 5500–6000) and highest for the E Knot (33,000)"]
- result: SB ring N(CO)/N(HCN) and N(CO)/N(HCO+) = ~50,000 and ~90,000 ± not stated dimensionless [3.1 LTE analysis: "much higher in the SB ring ($\sim$ 50,000 and $\sim$ 90,000 respectively)"]
- limitation (authors): RADEX assumes all transitions sample the same gas, and several ratios use degraded or low-resolution data [3.2 RADEX analysis of the CND: "Of course such modelling is limited by the assumption that all the molecular transitions sample the same gas."]
- limitation (authors): Chemical modelling is preliminary and compares time-dependent models only at one time step [4 Chemical modelling: "This attempt at chemical modelling was preliminary"]
- limitation (authors): Infrared pumping not considered [Conclusions: "this analysis did not take into consideration infrared pumping"]

### 2020ApJ...905..122H: Physical Conditions of Gas Components in Debris Disks of 49 Ceti and HD 21997 (2020; debris_disks; archival_reuse)
- question: Constrain the gas kinetic temperature and CO column density, and test CO excitation with H2 or electron collisions.
- step 5: Fit LTE excitation and opacity
  - method: Use peak-intensity ratios to assess opacity; fit rotational temperature, CO column density and beam filling factor with optical-depth effects. [LTE analysis: "the rotational temperature, the column density of $^{12}$CO, and the beam filling factor are derived by using the following equation, where we take the effect of the optical depth into account:"] [LTE analysis: "evaluated by using the least-squares analysis on the observed intensities of CO and its isotopic species."]
  - parameter: Fit blue-shifted and red-shifted components separately [LTE analysis: "We treat the data for the blue-shifted and red-shifted components separately."]
  - assumption: LTE; isotope ratios 13CO/12CO=1/77 and C18O/12CO=1/560 [LTE analysis: "$T_{\rm{rot}}$ is the rotational temperature which is equivalent with the excitation temperature in the LTE condition"] [LTE analysis: "We employ the isotope ratios of 1/77 and 1/560 for $^{13}$CO and C$^{18}$O, respectively (cite: wil94)."]
  - fitting: Least-squares fit of T_rot, N and f_d; maximum parameter correlation 0.99 between T_rot and f_d, reflected in quoted errors [LTE analysis: "evaluated by using the least-squares analysis on the observed intensities of CO and its isotopic species."] [LTE analysis: "The highest correlation among the three parameters in the fit is 0.99"]
  - validation: Compare calculated peak intensities and residuals with measurement uncertainties [LTE analysis: "The observed peak intensities are well reproduced within the measurement uncertainties for both sources."]
- step 7: Fit non-LTE excitation with H2 collisions
  - method: Use the authors' LVG code to balance collisional and radiative processes; fix H2 density and fit CO column density, kinetic temperature and filling factor separately for each velocity component. [The case of H$_{2}$ collision: "In this analysis, the rotational level populations are determined by the balance between the collision and radiation processes, from which the line intensities are calculated."] [The case of H$_{2}$ collision: "Hence, we fix the H$_{2}$ density and determine the remaining three parameters."]
  - software: authors' non-LTE code
  - parameter: Fixed H2 density range: 3×10^3 to 1×10^7 cm^-3 [The case of H$_{2}$ collision: "The range of the H$_{2}$ density assumed in the fit is from 3$\times$10$^{3}$ to 1$\times$10$^{7}$ cm$^{-3}$."]
  - assumption: LVG model; H2 collision rates from LAMDA [The case of H$_{2}$ collision: "First, we conduct the non-LTE analysis based on the LVG (large velocity gradient) model (cite: gol74; van07), assuming the collision with H$_{2}$."] [Non-LTE analysis: "The collisional cross sections are taken from The Leiden Atomic and Molecular Database (LAMDA) (cite: sch05)."]
  - assumption: Use H2 rates as a proxy for CO/C/C+/O collisions; interpret density as an effective sum of collision partners. [The case of H$_{2}$ collision: "we cannot distinguish the collision with H$_{2}$ and that of CO/C/C$^{+}$/O in our calculation and just employ the collisional rate coefficients for the H$_{2}$ collision."] [The case of H$_{2}$ collision: "it should approximately be regarded as $n$(H$_{2}$)+$n$(CO)+$n$(C)+$n$(C$^{+}$)+$n$(O)."]
  - assumption: Ignore CO-H collisions [The case of H$_{2}$ collision: "The rate for the CO-H collision is much lower than that of the CO-H$_{2}$ collision (cite: yan13; wal15a), and thus we have ignored the CO-H collision in this study."]
  - fitting: Least squares; simultaneous four-parameter fitting failed to constrain H2 density, so density is fixed. [The case of H$_{2}$ collision: "We have initially tried to optimize these four parameters by using the least-squares fit to reproduce the $^{12}$CO, $^{13}$CO and C$^{18}$O data."] [The case of H$_{2}$ collision: "However, all the four parameters cannot be determined simultaneously in the fit. Specifically, the H$_{2}$ density is not well constrained."]
  - validation: Code tested against published results and RADEX; inspect calculated intensities and residuals. [Non-LTE analysis: "We employ the non-LTE code prepared by ourselves in the analysis, which is tested by using the results reported by (cite: gol74) and also those derived from the RADEX code."] [The case of H$_{2}$ collision: "The fitting is successful, as shown by the residuals."]
- step 8: Test electron excitation
  - method: Repeat non-LTE LVG analysis with electron collisions, fixing electron density and determining the other three parameters. [The case of electron collision: "Next, we conduct the non-LTE LVG analysis assuming the collision with an electron, considering that the gas has the secondary origin."] [The case of electron collision: "we fix the electron density and determine the remaining three parameters."]
  - software: authors' non-LTE code
  - parameter: Electron densities: 30, 1.0×10^2, 1.0×10^3 and 1.0×10^4 cm^-3 [The case of electron collision: "The analysis is conducted for several electron densities from 30 to 10$^{4}$ cm$^{-3}$ for 49 Ceti and HD 21997"] [Table 8: "1.0$\times$10$^{2}$"]
  - assumption: Electron collisional rate coefficients calculated using (cite: dic77) [The case of electron collision: "Here, we employ the collisional rate coefficients calculated by using the method described by (cite: dic77)."]
  - assumption: Electrons must exceed 0.01 of CO, C, C+ and O to dominate collisions; maximum electron density around 10^4 cm^-3. [The case of electron collision: "the electron abundance relative to CO, C, C$^{+}$, and O needs to be higher than 0.01 in order for an electron to become a major collision partner."] [The case of electron collision: "the maximum electron density would be around 10$^{4}$ cm$^{-3}$"]
  - fitting: Fix electron density because the four parameters cannot be determined simultaneously. [The case of electron collision: "As in the H$_{2}$ collision case, we cannot determine the four parameters simultaneously in the analysis, and hence, we fix the electron density and determine the remaining three parameters."]
  - validation: Compare column densities, temperatures and filling factors with the H2-collision solutions. [The case of electron collision: "These values resemble those found in the H$_{2}$ collision case, except for the electron density of 30 cm$^{-3}$ where the optical depth of the CO lines is very high due to insufficient excitation."] [The case of electron collision: "For HD 21997, the ranges of the column density and the gas kinetic temperature are also similar to those of the H$_{2}$ collision case."]
- result: 49 Ceti peak 13CO(2–1)/12CO(2–1) intensity ratio = 0.46 ± ±0.03 dimensionless [LTE analysis: "the peak intensity ratio of $^{13}$CO(2–1)/$^{12}$CO(2–1) is calculated to be 0.46$\pm$0.03 and 0.48$\pm$0.04 for 49 Ceti and HD 21997, respectively."]
- result: HD 21997 peak 13CO(2–1)/12CO(2–1) intensity ratio = 0.48 ± ±0.04 dimensionless [LTE analysis: "the peak intensity ratio of $^{13}$CO(2–1)/$^{12}$CO(2–1) is calculated to be 0.46$\pm$0.03 and 0.48$\pm$0.04 for 49 Ceti and HD 21997, respectively."]
- result: 49 Ceti LTE 12CO(2–1) optical depth = 38–46 ± not stated dimensionless [LTE analysis: "For 49 Ceti, the optical depths of the $^{12}$CO(2–1) and $^{12}$CO(3–2) emissions are evaluated to be 38–46 and 18–24, respectively"]
- result: 49 Ceti LTE 12CO(3–2) optical depth = 18–24 ± not stated dimensionless [LTE analysis: "For 49 Ceti, the optical depths of the $^{12}$CO(2–1) and $^{12}$CO(3–2) emissions are evaluated to be 38–46 and 18–24, respectively"]
- result: HD 21997 LTE 12CO(2–1) optical depth = 66 – 76 ± not stated dimensionless [LTE analysis: "For HD 21997, the optical depths of the $^{12}$CO(2–1) and $^{12}$CO(3–2) emissions are 66 – 76 and 26 – 36, respectively"]
- result: HD 21997 LTE 12CO(3–2) optical depth = 26 – 36 ± not stated dimensionless [LTE analysis: "For HD 21997, the optical depths of the $^{12}$CO(2–1) and $^{12}$CO(3–2) emissions are 66 – 76 and 26 – 36, respectively"]
- result: 49 Ceti LTE blue-component rotational temperature = 9 ± 1 (1σ) K [Table 4: "2.2 (1.1) $\times$10$^{17}$ | 9 (1) | 0.06 (0.01)"]
- result: 49 Ceti LTE red-component rotational temperature = 8 ± 2 (1σ) K [Table 4: "1.6 (0.5) $\times$10$^{17}$ | 8 (2) | 0.08 (0.04)"]
- result: HD 21997 LTE rotational temperature, both velocity components = 8 ± 3 (1σ) K [Table 5: "1.9 (0.9) $\times$10$^{17}$ | 8 (3) | 0.08 (0.06)"]
- result: 49 Ceti non-LTE gas kinetic temperature, H2-collision case = 8–11 ± not stated K [Abstract: "The CO column density and the gas temperature are evaluated to be (1.8–5.9)$\times$10$^{17}$ cm$^{-2}$ and 8–11 K for 49 Ceti"]
- result: HD 21997 non-LTE gas kinetic temperature, H2-collision case = 8–12 ± not stated K [Abstract: "and (2.6–15)$\times$10$^{17}$ cm$^{-2}$ and 8–12 K for HD 21997, respectively, where the H$_{2}$ collision is assumed for the rotational excitation of CO."]
- result: 49 Ceti non-LTE CO column density, H2-collision case = (1.8–5.9)×10^17 ± not stated cm^-2 [Abstract: "The CO column density and the gas temperature are evaluated to be (1.8–5.9)$\times$10$^{17}$ cm$^{-2}$ and 8–11 K for 49 Ceti"]
- result: HD 21997 non-LTE CO column density, H2-collision case = (2.6–15)×10^17 ± not stated cm^-2 [Abstract: "and (2.6–15)$\times$10$^{17}$ cm$^{-2}$ and 8–12 K for HD 21997, respectively, where the H$_{2}$ collision is assumed for the rotational excitation of CO."]
- result: 49 Ceti blue-component CO column density, electron-collision case = 2×10^17 to 1.2×10^18 ± not stated cm^-2 [The case of electron collision: "For 49 Ceti, the column density of CO ranges from 2$\times$10$^{17}$ to 1.2$\times$10$^{18}$ cm$^{-2}$ and from 1.8$\times$10$^{17}$ to 9.8$\times$10$^{17}$ cm$^{-2}$ for the blue-shifted and red-shifted components, respectively."]
- result: 49 Ceti red-component CO column density, electron-collision case = 1.8×10^17 to 9.8×10^17 ± not stated cm^-2 [The case of electron collision: "For 49 Ceti, the column density of CO ranges from 2$\times$10$^{17}$ to 1.2$\times$10$^{18}$ cm$^{-2}$ and from 1.8$\times$10$^{17}$ to 9.8$\times$10$^{17}$ cm$^{-2}$ for the blue-shifted and red-shifted components, respectively."]
- result: HD 21997 red-component CO column density at electron density 3.0×10^1 cm^-3 = 4.0×10^18 ± 5.9×10^18 (1σ) cm^-2 [Table 9: "3.0$\times$10$^{1}$ | 4.0 (5.9) $\times$10$^{18}$ | 14 (13)"]
- result: HD 21997 red-component kinetic temperature at electron density 3.0×10^1 cm^-3 = 14 ± 13 (1σ) K [Table 9: "3.0$\times$10$^{1}$ | 4.0 (5.9) $\times$10$^{18}$ | 14 (13)"]
- limitation (authors): H2 density is not independently constrained by the fits. [The case of H$_{2}$ collision: "Specifically, the H$_{2}$ density is not well constrained."]
- limitation (authors): High optical depths can violate the simple LVG approximation; more rigorous radiative transfer including dust opacity is deferred. [The case of H$_{2}$ collision: "the simple approximation by the LVG model (cite: gol74) employed here does not perfectly describe the radiation transfer."]
- limitation (authors): State-to-state collision coefficients for CO, C and C+ are unavailable, preventing explicit treatment of these collisions. [The case of H$_{2}$ collision: "the state-to-state collisional rate coefficients for the CO, C, and C$^{+}$ are not available, and therefore the contribution of their collisions are not explicitly considered."]

### 2024ApJ...969..102N: Physical and Chemical Properties of Galactic Molecular Gas toward QSO J1851+0035 (2024; milky_way_ism, astrochemistry; archival_reuse)
- question: Physical state (column density, excitation temperature, velocity substructure) and chemical composition of Galactic molecular gas along the line of sight to QSO J1851+0035, derived from molecular absorption lines
- step 4: Derive column densities and abundances from integrated opacity
  - method: Column density from integral of tau, assuming optically thin lines and LTE; Tex set to T_CMB for most species; abundances relative to N(H2) = 2e21 cm^-2 for Components 7 and 16 [3.1.2 Marginal and non-detection: "by assuming optically thin lines and the LTE (local thermodynamic equilibrium) conditions"] [3.6 Chemical compositions: "the column density was obtained by assuming LTE for optically thin lines and that the excitation temperature is the CMB temperature."]
  - parameter: 1 sigma upper limits for undetected species; narrower velocity ranges used for CN, HCN and HNC to limit hyperfine blending [3.6.1 CN-bearing molecules: "narrower velocity ranges were set to minimize the blending by the other hyperfine components of CN and HCN"]
  - assumption: Optically thin LTE; Tex = T_CMB for most species [3.6 Chemical compositions: "the column density was obtained by assuming LTE for optically thin lines and that the excitation temperature is the CMB temperature."]
- step 5: Derive CO and 13CO excitation temperatures and check emission-based Tex
  - method: Tex from the ratio of integrated tau(2-1) to tau(1-0) via an analytic LTE solution without the Rayleigh-Jeans approximation; compared with CO brightness temperatures from NRO 45 m; NRO 45 m HCO+, HCN, HNC, C2H emission searched to constrai… [4.1 Excitation temperature through mult…: "we finally get the analytical solution of"] [4.2 Validity of CO excitation temperatu…: "To check the validity of this method toward general Galactic disk clouds, the CO excitation temperatures obtained through the excitation analysis of the absorption lines were compared with the CO brightness temperatures."]
  - parameter: E_J=1/kB = 5.53 K for CO and 5.29 K for 13CO; nu(2-1) ~ 2 nu(1-0); S(2,1) ~ 2 S(1,0) [4.1 Excitation temperature through mult…: "Here, $E_{J=0}/k_{\rm B}$ = 0 K"]
  - assumption: LTE with constant Tex for all transitions [4.1 Excitation temperature through mult…: "remains constant for all transitions"]
  - assumption: Rayleigh-Jeans approximation not valid at these low Tex [4.1 Excitation temperature through mult…: "However, the excitation temperature is quite small for the current absorption study and the assumption for the Rayleigh-Jeans approximation"]
  - validation: Absorption Tex compared with CO brightness temperature: absorption values tend to be higher, explained by opacity [4.2 Validity of CO excitation temperatu…: "The opacity explains this discrepancy because the excitation analysis of the CO absorption lines can only be done when the lines are not saturated"]
- step 6: Estimate total H2 column density along the sightline
  - method: Six estimates compared: Planck A_V with gas-to-extinction ratio, CO J=1-0 integrated intensity with X_CO, optically thin LTE 13CO emission, CO absorption, HCO+ absorption, c-C3H2 absorption [4.4 Total amount of molecular gas along…: "We estimated the total molecular gas column density toward this line of sight using several different means."]
  - parameter: X(13CO) = 2e-6 and standard Galactic CO-to-H2 factor [3.2.2 Characteristics of the parent clo…: "as a canonical value for Galactic disk clouds (cite: 2008ApJ...679..481P)"]
  - assumption: Universal HCO+ and c-C3H2 abundances relative to H2 taken from the literature [4.4 Total amount of molecular gas along…: "can be converted to $N$(H$_2$) by assuming the universal HCO$^+$ abundance"]
  - validation: Estimates agree within about (0.6-2)e22 cm^-2 except A_V and CO-absorption based values [4.4 Total amount of molecular gas along…: "All these estimations of total molecular column density are in reasonable agreement with"]
- step 8: Infer density and CO abundance of subcomponents with non-LTE models
  - method: ndradex/RADEX grids of Tex and tau versus n(H2) and X(CO)/(dv/dr) at fixed Tk; solution at the crossing of constant-Tex and constant-tau lines; only subcomponents with tau(1-0) <~ 1 [4.2 Physical conditions and CO abundanc…: "Here we deduce the physical conditions and CO abundance of some of the substructures identified by absorption measurements with the aid of"]
  - software: ndradex, RADEX
  - parameter: Tk = 50 K [4.2 Physical conditions and CO abundanc…: "Instead, we assume $T_{\rm k}$ = 50 K suggested from the large HCN/HNC ratio toward these velocity components throughout this subsection."]
  - assumption: Canonical X(CO) not assumed; velocity gradient dv/dr degenerate with X(CO), reported as X(CO)/(dv/dr) [4.2 Physical conditions and CO abundanc…: "We thus do not assume the canonical $X$(CO) value in the present analysis."]
- result: CO excitation temperature, Component 7 = 6.75 ± 0.11 K [Conclusions: "The CO excitation temperature was just 6.75$\pm$0.11 K"]
- result: CO excitation temperature, Subcomponent 18-1 = 6.52 ± 2.72 K [4.2.1 Properties of molecular gas at th…: "= 6.52$\pm$2.72 K"]
- result: Excitation temperature of dense-gas tracers (HCO+, HCN) = ~2.9 ± not stated K [Conclusions: "toward this line of sight is very low ($\sim$2.9 K)"]
- result: Total N(H2) range from agreeing estimates = (0.6-2)e22 ± not stated cm^-2 [4.4 Total amount of molecular gas along…: "All these estimations of total molecular column density are in reasonable agreement with $N$(H$_2$) $\sim$ (0.6–2) $\times 10^{22}$ cm$^{-2}$"]
- result: N(H2) from CO absorption column density = 1.8e21 ± not stated cm^-2 [4.4 Total amount of molecular gas along…: "The extracted value of $N$(H$_2$) = $1.8 \times$ $10^{21}$ cm$^{-2}$ is about one order of magnitude smaller than the others."]

- result: Total N(c-C3H2) = 1.5e13 ± not stated cm^-2 [4.4 Total amount of molecular gas along…: "$N$($c$-C$_3$H$_2$) = $1.5 \times 10^{13}$ cm$^{-2}$ yields"]
- result: N(13CO), Component 7 = 3461 ± 47 1e12 cm^-2 [Table 4: "3461$\pm$47"]

- limitation (authors): Archival calibrator data have poorer angular resolution, frequency resolution and sensitivity than the dedicated CO data [2.2 Absorption data from ALMA calibrati…: "the angular resolution, frequency resolution, and sensitivity were not as good as those of the CO data."]

- limitation (authors): Narrower line components may exist but SNR prevents identification [4.5 Multiple-Gaussian line fit of CO ab…: "the limited signal-to-noise ratio prevented us from further identification."]

### 2025ApJ...989...83B: CO Emission and Absorption-line Survey of the M87 Nucleus Using Archival ALMA Imaging (2025; agn_and_quasars, nearby_galaxies; archival_reuse)
- question: Is there molecular gas in the inner kpc of M87 (CO emission from the circumnuclear disk, ionized-gas filaments and dusty clouds, or CO absorption against the nucleus), as probed by archival ALMA imaging?
- step 8: Derive excitation temperature and column density (LTE)
  - method: Solve the opacity-ratio equation for T_ex(2-1), then N_CO from CO(1-0) opacity and Q_rot; error bars from Monte Carlo draws of tau_CO; N_H2 from CO/H2 abundance ratios [Temperature and Column Densities: "To determine a total column density $N_\mathrm{CO}$, we primarily considered CO(1$-$0) absorption and an excitation temperature for the lowest-$J$ transitions:"] [Temperature and Column Densities: "After $10^5$ iterations, we used the 15.9 to 84.1 (0.14 to 99.87) percentile levels of the $T^{21}_\mathrm{ex}$ distribution"]
  - software: Splatalogue
  - parameter: 10^5 MC iterations with tau_CO drawn from normal distributions (std = 1-sigma uncertainty); 1-sigma and 3-sigma percentile ranges [Temperature and Column Densities: "We recovered more reliable error bars in a series of trials by drawing individual $\tau_\mathrm{CO}$ randomly from normal distributions centered on their measured value with standard deviations equal to their 1$\sigma$ uncertainties in Table"]
  - assumption: Single-zone LTE with adjacent transitions under the same conditions; beam filling factor ignored; CMB negligible; T_K = T_ex [Temperature and Column Densities: "This assumes a single-zone system in local thermodynamic equilibrium (LTE) where adjacent transitions (e.g., $J = 0\rightarrow1$ and $1\rightarrow2$) originate under the same conditions."] [Temperature and Column Densities: "We have ignored beam filling factors and also considered the impact of the CMB background to be negligible"]
  - assumption: Diffuse-gas CO/H2 abundance ratios (~2e-6 to ~8e-7) preferred over the standard 3.2e-4 for N_H2 [Column Densities: "CO/H$_2$ abundances of $\sim$2$\times$10$^{-6}$ (cite: liszt19) to $\sim$8$\times$10$^{-7}$ (cite: pineda10)"]
  - fitting: Direct solution of the equations with Monte Carlo error propagation (no priors) [Temperature and Column Densities: "After calculating weighted-average opacities at each iteration, we used the newly-fit $T_\mathrm{ex}$ to find a new $Q_\mathrm{rot}$ and $N_\mathrm{CO}$."]
  - validation: CO(3-2)/CO(2-1) opacity ratio is not consistent with LTE expectations, motivating non-LTE modelling [Temperature and Column Densities: "is not consistent with LTE expectations."]
- step 9: Non-LTE radiative transfer modelling of the absorber
  - method: RADEX uniform-sphere LVG models fitted to the weighted-average peak opacities of CO(1-0), CO(2-1) and CO(3-2) on a grid of n_H2 and T_bg for T_K = 10 K and 30 K [Temperatures and Densities: "we employed the one-dimensional non-LTE radiative transfer code `radex` (cite: ferland98; van07) to reproduce the observed peak opacities"] [Temperatures and Densities: "and present $\chi^2$ contours over a grid of $n_\mathrm{H_2}$ and $T_\mathrm{bg}$ points"]
  - software: radex
  - parameter: N_CO = 1e15 cm^-2 fixed from LTE; CO FWHM fixed to 10 km/s [Temperatures and Densities: "we adopted $N_\mathrm{CO} = 10^{15}$ cm$^{-2}$ from LTE measurements and fixed the CO line FWHM to 10 km s$^{-1}$"]
  - parameter: Grid n_H2 = 1e2-1e5 cm^-3 and T_bg = 30-300 K; T_K = 10 K and 30 K tested [Temperatures and Densities: "During `radex` modeling, we explored $n_\mathrm{H_2} = 10^2-10^5$ cm$^{-3}$ and background temperature $T_\mathrm{bg} = 30-300$ K."] [Temperatures and Densities: "we assumed a cold gas distribution and tested $T_K = 10$ K and 30 K in turn"]
  - assumption: Uniform, isothermal, homogeneous sphere with large-velocity-gradient escape probability; 12CO traces the whole cloud because tau << 1 [Temperatures and Densities: "We assumed the gas is organized in a uniform sphere, although different geometries and escape probabilities give similar results for low-$J$ CO transitions."]
  - fitting: Chi-square grid search over n_H2 and T_bg (no sampler or priors stated) [Temperatures and Densities: "and present $\chi^2$ contours over a grid of $n_\mathrm{H_2}$ and $T_\mathrm{bg}$ points"]
  - validation: Different geometries and escape probabilities give similar results; 1-sigma contours do not close, so only lower limits n_H2 > 1100 cm^-3 and T_bg > 45 K [Temperatures and Densities: "While the 1$\sigma$ contours do not close for reasonable $n_\mathrm{H_2}$ and $T_\mathrm{bg}$, they provide $n_\mathrm{H_2} > 1100$ cm$^{-3}$ and $T_\mathrm{bg} > 45$ K lower limits."]
- result: Excitation temperature T_ex(2-1) (median) = 7.77 ± +1.34/-1.94 (1-sigma); +14.88/-2.90 (3-sigma) K [Temperature and Column Densities: "we used the 15.9 to 84.1 (0.14 to 99.87) percentile levels of the $T^{21}_\mathrm{ex}$ distribution to find a median $T^{21}_\mathrm{ex} = 7.77^{+1.34}_{-1.94}\left(^{+14.88}_{-2.90}\right)$ K"]
- result: CO column density N_CO = 1.16e15 ± +0.21/-0.28 (1-sigma); +3.55/-0.47 (3-sigma), in 1e15 cm^-2 [Temperature and Column Densities: "$N_\mathrm{CO}/10^{15}\,\mathrm{cm}\ensuremath{^{-2}}= 1.16^{+0.21}_{-0.28}\left(^{+3.55}_{-0.47}\right)$ with 1$\sigma$ (3$\sigma$) error bars"]
- result: Excitation temperature T_ex(3-2) (median) = 28.6 ± +26.7/-14.9 K [Temperature and Column Densities: "we find higher median $T^{32}_\mathrm{ex} = 28.6^{+26.7}_{-14.9}$ K with $N_\mathrm{CO} \sim 10^{16}$ cm$^{-2}$"]
- result: H2 column density range N_H2 (in 1e20) = 0.92 to 2.3 ± +0.68/-0.36 and +1.7/-0.9 (1-sigma) cm^-2 [Conclusion: "$N_\mathrm{H_2}/10^{20}\,\mathrm{cm}\ensuremath{^{-2}}= 0.92^{+0.68}_{-0.36} - 2.3^{+1.7}_{-0.9}$ (1$\sigma$ uncertainties)"]
- result: RADEX best-fit n_H2 and T_bg for T_K = 10 K = n_H2 ~ 5100 cm^-3; T_bg ~ 62 K ± 1-sigma contours do not close; lower limits n_H2 > 1100 cm^-3 and T_bg > 45 K cm^-3 and K [Temperatures and Densities: "best-fitting $n_\mathrm{H_2} \sim 5100$ cm$^{-3}$ and $T_\mathrm{bg} \sim 62$ K"]
- limitation (authors): Bandpass calibration effects on tau_0 and tau_CO are not quantified [CO Absorption-line Properties: "However, we do not attempt to quantify here the bandpass calibration effects on"]
- limitation (authors): CO(3-2) and CO(2-1) opacities are less reliable than CO(1-0), so LTE reconciliation is not attempted [Temperature and Column Densities: "Unfortunately, neither $\tau_\mathrm{CO(3-2)}$ or $\overline{\tau}_\mathrm{CO(2-1)}$ values are as reliable as is $\overline{\tau}_\mathrm{CO(1-0)}$"]
- limitation (authors): Xa1 maximum recoverable scale cannot entirely rule out diffuse emission over larger regions [Channel Maps: "cannot entirely rule out diffuse emission spread out over a couple of the larger regions with $\sim$5$''$ extent"]
- not stated (card): CASA and pipeline versions used for each data set (only 'the appropriate pipeline version' is given); Briggs robust value for MFS maps, cell size, image size, niter/threshold, deconvolver and masks for tclean MFS and cube imaging; Which flux calibrator and which spws had setjy rescaling, and the exact rescale factors per spw; Self-calibration details: refant, solint sequence, number of loops, whi…

### 2026A&A...705A.201B: The ALMA survey to Resolve exoKuiper belt Substructures (ARKS): VII. Optically thick gas with broad CO gaussian local line profiles in the HD 121617 disc (2026; debris_disks, astrochemistry; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Are the 12CO and 13CO lines in the HD 121617 debris disc optically thick, and what do local line profiles imply for CO mass, temperature and mean molecular weight
- step 6: Optically thin RADMC-3D 12CO model compared qualitatively with data
  - method: Gaussian CO surface density (r_peak 73 au, sigma 21 au, fixed from radial profile), Gaussian vertical profile in hydrostatic equilibrium, Keplerian velocity; mu, Tpeak and MCO adjusted by hand against FWHM and peak-intensity azimuthal prof… [5.1.2 Data comparison: "Since we did not formally fit the cube, these parameters were adjusted to achieve the best qualitative agreement with key features of our dataset."] [5.1.1 Model assumptions: "We assumed a Gaussian vertical profile with a vertically constant temperature, as expected from hydrostatic equilibrium."]
  - software: RADMC-3D, emcee
  - parameter: Model cube 678 x 678 pixels of 0.0225 arcsec with 1182 channels of 13 m/s; spectral Gaussian FWHM = 2 x channel width; beam 0.12 x 0.11 arcsec [5.1.1 Model assumptions: "678 $\times$ 678 $0.0225\ensuremath{''}$ pixels with 1182 13 m s$^{-1}$ channels"]
  - parameter: Azimuthal sections of 15 deg for data; Gaussian fits with 2000 steps and 100 burn-in [5.1.2 Data comparison: "The fitting procedure consists of 2000 steps with a burn-in period of 100 steps due to faster convergence."]
  - assumption: LTE level populations; Keplerian rotation with 1.89 Msun [5.1.1 Model assumptions: "For simplicity, we assumed local thermodynamic equilibrium (LTE) to calculate the CO level populations."]
  - fitting: Qualitative adjustment of three parameters; Gaussian FWHM and peak intensity fitted per azimuth with MCMC [5.1.2 Data comparison: "We varied three parameters in our"]
  - validation: Model rejected as it needs unrealistically high temperature, cannot make the X-shape, and overestimates 13CO linewidths [5.1.2 Data comparison: "it requires unrealistically high temperatures, cannot account for the X-shape"]
- step 7: Fit optically thick RADMC-3D model to the 13CO cube
  - method: MCMC fit of 13CO cube with free R, delta R, T at 73 au, temperature power-law index beta, mu, CO mass, inclination, PA and stellar mass [5.2.1 13CO RADMC-3D model: "$\mu$, the mean molecular weight"] [5.2.1 13CO RADMC-3D model: "was carried out using MCMC, with an affine-invariant ensemble sampler implemented in the emcee package"]
  - software: RADMC-3D, emcee
  - parameter: Per-pixel/channel noise = cube RMS times sqrt(pixels per beam and channels per effective bandwidth) [5.2.1 13CO RADMC-3D model: "the uncertainty per pixel and channel assumed to be equal to the cube’s RMS multiplied by the square root of the number of pixels per beam and the number of channels per effective bandwidth"]
  - assumption: LTE so kinetic temperature equals excitation temperature; Gaussian radial CO distribution; distance 117.9 pc [5.2.1 13CO RADMC-3D model: "assuming LTE allows us to break this degeneracy by assuming that the kinetic temperature is the same as the excitation temperature (38 K)"]
  - fitting: emcee affine-invariant sampler; priors and chain length not stated [5.2.1 13CO RADMC-3D model: "was carried out using MCMC, with an affine-invariant ensemble sampler implemented in the emcee package"]
  - validation: Residuals of moment maps and channel maps compared with data [5.2.1 13CO RADMC-3D model: "is a very good fit to the data"]
- result: Optically thin RADMC-3D model: peak optical depth, Tpeak, mu, CO mass = ~0.11; 380 K; 28; 1e-3 Mearth ± not stated dimensionless; K; dimensionless; Mearth [5.1.2 Data comparison: "We find that an optically thin model with a peak optical depth of $\sim$0.11, $T_{\rm peak} = 380$ K, $\mu = 28$ (CO dominated), and $M_{\rm CO} = 10^{-3}$ M$_{\oplus}$ reproduces many key features of the data."]
- result: Peak radius R of 13CO Gaussian surface density = 74.4 ± +0.2/-0.2 au [Table 4: "$R$ (au) | $74.4_{-0.2}^{+0.2}$"]
- result: FWHM of 13CO Gaussian surface density = 17.3 ± +0.9/-0.9 au [Table 4: "$\Delta R$ (au) | $17.3_{-0.9}^{+0.9}$"]
- result: Temperature at 73 au (13CO model) = 38.1 ± +1.3/-1.2 K [Table 4: "$T_{73\ \mathrm{au}}$ (K) | $38.1_{-1.2}^{+1.3}$"]
- result: Temperature power-law index beta = -0.14 ± +0.07/-0.07 dimensionless [Table 4: "$\beta$ | $-0.14_{-0.07}^{+0.07}$"]
- result: Mean molecular weight mu = 12.6 ± +1.3/-1.1 dimensionless [abstract: "We constrain the mean molecular weight to $12.6_{-1.1}^{+1.3}$, dependent on model assumptions."]
- result: log 13CO mass = -2.7 ± +0.2/-0.1 log10(Mearth) [Table 4: "$\log M_{^{13}\mathrm{CO}}$ (M$_\oplus$) | $-2.7_{-0.1}^{+0.2}$"]
- result: Inclination from 13CO cube fit = 44.4 ± +0.2/-0.2 deg [Table 4: "$i$ ($^\circ$) | $44.4_{-0.2}^{+0.2}$"]
- result: Stellar mass from 13CO cube fit = 1.915 ± +0.009/-0.009 Msun [Table 4: "$M_\star$ (M$_\odot$) | $1.915_{-0.009}^{+0.009}$"]
- result: 13CO mass (best-fit RADMC-3D) = 2e-3 ± not stated Mearth [5.2.1 13CO RADMC-3D model: "a $^{13}$CO mass of $2 \times 10^{-3}$ M$_{\oplus}$"]
- result: 13CO peak optical depth of best-fit model = 26 ± not stated dimensionless [5.2.1 13CO RADMC-3D model: "The best-fit model is optically thick, with a peak optical depth of 26"]
- limitation (authors): Keplerian fit uncertainties likely underestimated [Table 2: "We note that these uncertainties are likely underestimated."]
- limitation (authors): Mu and 13CO mass depend on the LTE assumption and radial and vertical model choices [5.2.1 13CO RADMC-3D model: "we caution that its derivation is critically dependent on our LTE assumption"]
- limitation (authors): Different total CO masses or abundance ratios may fit equally well given high optical depth [5.2.2 12CO RADMC-3D model: "We, however, caution that different total CO masses/abundance ratios may also fit the data, given the high optical depth of both isotopologues."]

### 2022A&A...667A.131B: Understanding if molecular ratios can be used as diagnostics of AGN and starburst activity: The case of NGC 1068 (2022; agn_and_quasars, nearby_galaxies, astrochemistry; archival_reuse)
- question: Whether molecular line ratios between HCN, HCO+, and CS are useful tracers of AGN-dominated versus starburst-dominated gas in NGC 1068 across spatial scales, and what physical or chemical differences drive the observed ratio variations.
- step 3: Calculate LTE column density bounds for HCN, HCO+, and CS
  - method: Calculate upper-state column density N_u and total molecular column density N independently for each transition using LTE equations and CDMS partition functions to establish minimum and maximum column density limits for non-LTE grids [LTE analysis: "Using the observed line intensities from the archival data, we were able to estimate the column densities by assuming LTE and by using the same groupings of observations as discussed earlier, devolved to the lowest resolution of that group."] [LTE analysis: "To compute the partition function, we made use of molecular data from the Cologne Database for Molecular Spectroscopy (CDMS) (cite: CDMS)."]
  - parameter: T = 50 K to 400 K [LTE analysis: "We used temperatures in the range 50 K to 400 K and computed the column density from each transition independently"]
  - assumption: Local Thermodynamic Equilibrium (LTE) [LTE analysis: "Using the observed line intensities from the archival data, we were able to estimate the column densities by assuming LTE"]
  - assumption: Beam filling factor Delta Omega_a / Delta Omega_s = 1 (source completely fills the beam) [LTE analysis: "Since we are investigating the regions themselves, we made the assumption that the source completely fills the beam, such that $\frac{\Delta\Omega_a}{\Delta\Omega_s}=1$."]
  - assumption: Optically thin limit tau = 0 [LTE analysis: "We set $\tau = 0$ for our lines, following the example of (cite: Krips_2008)."]
- step 4: Perform non-LTE radiative transfer modelling and MCMC parameter estimation
  - method: Run a grid of LVG non-LTE radiative transfer models using RADEX via SpectralRadex with LAMDA collisional data, compute modelled line ratios, and sample the chi^2 distribution using MCMC to infer posterior distributions of gas density, kine… [Non-LTE analysis: "We used `RADEX` for this task via the `SpectralRadex` python package[^1]."] [Non-LTE analysis: "We then fit these modelled ratios to their observed values by MCMC sampling the $\chi^2$ distribution of the parameters."]
  - software: RADEX, SpectralRadex
  - parameter: n_H2 = 10^3 to 10^7 cm^-3 (50 discrete values); T_K = 50 to 400 K (50 discrete values); N_Mol = N_min to N_max (50 discrete values); Delta v = 50 to 150 km s^-1 (3 discrete values) [Table 4: "$n_{H_2}$ (cm$^{-3}$) | $10^3$ | $10^7$ | 50"] [Table 4: "$T_K$ (K) | 50 | 400 | 50"]
  - assumption: Large Velocity Gradient (LVG) escape probability approximation with uniform spherical geometry for sub-regions [Non-LTE analysis: "`RADEX` is a non-LTE radiative transfer code developed by (cite: Van_der_Tak_2007), which uses the escape probability method (cite: 1960_Sobolev). We assumed the sub-regions could be approximated as uniform spheres"]
  - fitting: MCMC sampling of chi^2 = sum_{i=1}^4 (R_o - R_m)/sigma^2 with uniform priors, 12 walkers, 100000 steps per walker, for regions/resolutions with at least 2 observed ratios [Non-LTE analysis: "Assuming a uniform prior, sampling this distribution gives the posterior probability distribution of the parameters."] [Non-LTE analysis: "Our MCMC sampler uses 12 walkers, each performing 100000 steps, to sample possible parameter combinations. We did this for all combinations of regions and resolutions with at least two of the investigated ratios."]
  - validation: Evaluated posterior 16th and 84th percentile confidence intervals across 40 pc and 100 pc resolution datasets [Non-LTE analysis: "The resulting 16th and 84th percentile limits of the three ratio distributions are shown in Figure [11](#fig:grid_Abundance_ratios)."]
- result: LTE column density range for CS (N_min to N_max) = 3.34E+12 to 2.44E+16 ± not stated cm^-2 [Table 3: "CS | 3.34E+12 | 2.44E+16"]
- result: LTE column density range for HCO+ (N_min to N_max) = 1.73E+11 to 3.43E+16 ± not stated cm^-2 [Table 3: "HCO+ | 1.73E+11 | 3.43E+16"]
- result: LTE column density range for HCN (N_min to N_max) = 2.62E+13 to 7.38E+17 ± not stated cm^-2 [Table 3: "HCN | 2.62E+13 | 7.38E+17"]
- result: Best-fit Log(n_H) at E Knot (~40 pc) = 5.31 ± +0.25/-0.31 log(cm^-3) [Table 5: "40.0 | E Knot | $5.31_{0.31}^{0.25}$ | $125.52_{49.54}^{209.49}$ | $96.99_{32.42}^{33.98}$ | $16.96_{0.45}^{0.73}$ | $15.50_{0.25}^{0.23}$ | $15.73_{0.3}^{0.3}$"]
- result: Best-fit Log(n_H) at W Knot (~40 pc) = 5.12 ± +0.35/-0.23 log(cm^-3) [Table 5: "40.0 | W Knot | $5.12_{0.23}^{0.35}$ | $172.63_{94.41}^{165.44}$ | $95.87_{31.85}^{34.69}$ | $16.88_{0.4}^{0.72}$ | $15.40_{0.27}^{0.22}$ | $15.59_{0.31}^{0.3}$"]
- result: Best-fit Log(n_H) at NSB-a (~40 pc) = 5.02 ± +0.23/-0.17 log(cm^-3) [Table 5: "40.0 | NSB-a | $5.02_{0.17}^{0.23}$ | $260.80_{125.93}^{97.43}$ | $83.62_{24.1}^{38.03}$ | $16.23_{0.31}^{0.19}$ | $15.90_{0.29}^{0.22}$ | $16.13_{0.31}^{0.18}$"]
- result: Best-fit Log(N_HCN) at AGN (~40 pc) = 16.84 ± +0.52/-1.21 log(cm^-2) [Table 5: "40.0 | AGN | $4.40_{0.83}^{0.57}$ | $240.19_{148.66}^{117.72}$ | $94.33_{31.79}^{36.44}$ | $16.84_{1.21}^{0.52}$ | $15.85_{1.06}^{0.41}$ | $15.74_{0.95}^{0.39}$"]
- limitation (authors): Not all sub-regions have been observed at all spatial resolutions for all molecules; NSB-b lacks consistent resolution observations and NSB-c has only a single high-resolution ratio (with HCN(4-3) and HCO+(4-3) below 3 sigma), excluding NS… [Comparison between observations: "We note that not all sub-regions listed in Table [1](#tab:Regions), and shown in Figures [1](#fig:HCO$^+$) and [2](#fig:HCN), have been observed at all resolutions for all molecules."]
- limitation (authors): Gas kinetic temperature, gas density (except in E Knot, W Knot, and NSB-a at 40 pc), and linewidth are poorly constrained by the MCMC RADEX fitting across resolutions. [Non-LTE analysis: "Neither the CND nor the SB ring regions can be reasonably constrained in the temperature or density planes, at least with regards to showing distinguishing qualities within these parameters."]
- limitation (authors): Follow-up studies of other nearby galaxies are required to confirm whether HCN(4-3)/HCO+(4-3) and HCN(4-3)/CS(2-1) hold as reliable diagnostics beyond NGC 1068. [Summary and conclusion: "whereas HCN(4-3)/HCO+(4-3) HCN(4-3)/CS(2-1) may show promise at certain resolutions but follow up studies, especially of other nearby galaxies, is required in order to confirm our findings."]
- not stated (card): CASA version and specific imaging parameters (cell size, image size, cleaning threshold, continuum subtraction method, velocity integration ranges for moment 0 maps) for the re-analysed Band 3 HCN(1-0) and HCO+(1-0) data.; Exact aperture size or pixel extraction method used to extract sub-region intensities from the moment 0 maps.; Which specific MCMC Python package (e.g., emcee) was used and how…

### 2014Sci...345.1584B: Detection of a branched alkyl molecule in the interstellar medium: iso-propyl cyanide (2014; astrochemistry, star_formation; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Can a branched alkyl molecule (iso-propyl cyanide) be detected in the ISM, and how does its abundance compare with its straight-chain isomer and with chemical model predictions?
- step 2: Identify lines via LTE modeling
  - method: LTE model of all molecules with 5 parameters (column density, temperature, source size, velocity offset, linewidth) per spectral window [Line identification and spectral modeli…: "with five parameters: column density, temperature, source size, velocity offset, and linewidth."]
  - software: Weeds, CLASS
  - assumption: LTE [Line identification and spectral modeli…: "The emission of each molecule across the full 3 mm atmospheric window is modeled assuming local thermodynamic equilibrium"]
  - validation: Only transitions not severely blended with other species are used [Line identification and spectral modeli…: "Only the transitions that are not severely blended with transitions of other species are shown in these figures."]
- step 4: Derive rotation temperature
  - method: Population diagram linear fit after subtracting contaminating-molecule model [Line identification and spectral modeli…: "Finally, we constructed population diagrams(cite: Goldsmith99) for *i*-PrCN and *n*-PrCN using the transitions that are not too severely blended with emission from other identified species."]
  - assumption: Optically thin transitions [Line identification and spectral modeli…: "provided the transitions are optically thin (which is verified to be true a posteriori)"]
  - fitting: linear fit to population diagram [Materials and Methods, Line identificat…: "a linear fit to these data points can give a rough estimate of the rotation temperature characterizing the emission of the molecules, provided the transitions are optically thin"]
  - validation: Optical thinness verified a posteriori [Line identification and spectral modeli…: "provided the transitions are optically thin (which is verified to be true a posteriori)"]
- step 5: Derive column densities and abundance ratio
  - method: LTE model with size 1.0'', Trot 153 K, linewidth 5 km/s, with correction for vibrationally excited states [main text: "With the size and temperature derived above and a linewidth measurement of 5 km s$^{-1}$, we obtain a good fit to all transitions"]
  - software: Weeds
  - parameter: Vibrational partition function factors at 150 K: 1.64 (i-PrCN), 2.09 (g-n-PrCN), 2.36 (a-n-PrCN) [Laboratory spectroscopy: "increase the partition function values at 150 K by factors of 1.64, 2.09, and 2.36 for *i*-PrCN, *g-n*-PrCN, and *a-n*-PrCN, respectively."]
- result: Rotation temperature (n-PrCN) = 153 ± 12 (SEM) K [Line identification and spectral modeli…: "We derive $T_{\rm ex} = 154 \pm 99$ K for *i*-PrCN and $153 \pm 12$ K (SEM) for *n*-PrCN."]
- result: i-PrCN column density = 7.2e16 ± 1.4e16 (SEM) cm^-2 [main text: "we derive column densities of $7.2 \pm 1.4 \times 10^{16}$ cm$^{-2}$ and $1.8 \pm 0.4 \times 10^{17}$ cm$^{-2}$ (SEM), respectively"]
- result: n-PrCN column density = 1.8e17 ± 0.4e17 (SEM) cm^-2 [main text: "we derive column densities of $7.2 \pm 1.4 \times 10^{16}$ cm$^{-2}$ and $1.8 \pm 0.4 \times 10^{17}$ cm$^{-2}$ (SEM), respectively"]
- result: [i-PrCN]/[n-PrCN] = 0.40 ± 0.06 (SEM) dimensionless [main text: "which yields an abundance ratio \[*i*-PrCN\]/\[*n*-PrCN\] of $0.40 \pm 0.06$ (SEM)"]
- limitation (authors): Preliminary LTE model not fully optimized for ALMA; abundance uncertainties exclude free-free contamination and dust property uncertainties [Line identification and spectral modeli…: "This model has not been fully optimized for ALMA yet but it is already sufficiently reliable to allow for detecting new molecules in the ALMA spectra."]
- not stated (card): Exact line-free channel ranges used for continuum subtraction; CLEAN parameters (weighting/robust, cell size, image size, masks, threshold); Which spectral line and reference antenna/solint used for self-calibration; Full list and frequencies of i-PrCN and n-PrCN transitions used in population diagrams; Weeds LTE parameters for all other molecules in the preliminary model; Details of free-free co…

### 2021ApJ...921...97J: Molecular Line Observations in Two Dusty Star-forming Galaxies at z = 6.9 (2021; high_z_galaxies, star_formation, astrochemistry; observational_alma_primary)
- question: What are the physical properties of the molecular interstellar medium, gas mass, CO-to-H2 conversion factor, gas depletion timescales, and heating mechanisms in the two dusty star-forming galaxies of SPT0311-58 at z = 6.9?
- step 6: Perform non-LTE LVG radiative transfer modeling of dust, CO, and [CI] simultaneously
  - method: Simultaneously fit dust continuum, CO SLED, and [CI](2-1) flux densities using non-LTE LVG radiative transfer models (1-component, 2-component, and Turbulence models, where Turbulence samples 50 densities from a log-normal PDF) optimized v… [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "To model the dust emission, observed CO, and \[CI\] flux densities in SPT0311-58 W and E, we use two non-local thermodynamic equilibrium (non-LTE) large velocity gradient (LVG) radiative transfer modeling methods: *N-component* and *Turbulence*."] [N-component and Turbulence Models: "For each value of $\rm < n_{H_{2}}>$, we sample 50 gas densities within $\rm 10 - 10^{10} \ cm^{-3}$. The final dust and gas SED, which describes the observations, is the sum of all the 50 SEDs."]
  - parameter: 10 input parameters (1-component), 20 input parameters (2-component), 12 input parameters (Turbulence); 30 independent runs evaluating ~10^7 models total [N-component and Turbulence Models: "This model will henceforth be referred to as *1-component* and *2-component*, which has 10 and 20 input parameters, respectively (see Table \[ncomp_parameters\] in the Appendix). The *Turbulence* model realistically models the ISM with 12 input parameters (see Table \[turb_parameters\])."] [N-component and Turbulence Models: "We run the code 30 times to avoid artificially narrow PDFs for the solutions and hence have 30 final solutions."]
  - parameter: Parameter priors/ranges in Table 3: log(n_H2) in [1, 7], T_K in [21, 600] K, T_K/T_dust in [0.5, 6.0], beta_TK in [-0.5, -0.05], beta_Tdust in [1.5, 2.0], R_eff in [0.1, 5000] pc, kappa_vir in [1, 3], dv_turb in [5, 200] km/s, GDMR in [90,… [Table 3: "| log(n$_{\rm H_{2}}$) | cm$^{-3}$ | 1 $-$ 7 | 5.3 $\pm$ 1.3 | 5.1 $\pm$ 0.9 | 4.7 $\pm$ 2.4 | 4.4 $\pm$ 2.2 |"] [Table 3: "| GDMR | \- | 90 $-$ 1100 | 124 $\pm$ 62 | \- | 138 $\pm$ 95 | \- |"]
  - assumption: Dust opacity kappa_nu / m^2 kg^-1 = 0.04 * (nu / 250 GHz)^beta_Td; log-normal density PDF in supersonically isothermal turbulent gas; T_K and [CI] abundance coupled to density as power laws in Turbulence model; Helium factor 1.36 [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "In the radiative transfer models, we adopt $\rm \kappa_{\nu}/m^{2}\ kg^{-1}\ = 0.04 \times (\nu/250\ GHz)^{\beta_{T_{d}}}$."] [N-component and Turbulence Models: "$\rm dp(n^{\prime}, dv_{turb})$ is the log-normal probability distribution function (PDF) in supersonically isothermal turbulent gas as given in (cite: krumholz05), which depends on the normalized gas density ($\rm n^{\prime}$) and the turbulence line width ($\rm dv_{turb}$)."]
  - fitting: Monte Carlo Bees Algorithm optimizing chi^2 across 30 runs (~10^7 models); best-fit parameters and errors are chi^2-weighted mean and standard deviation [N-component and Turbulence Models: "The models are optimized to fit the observed flux densities of dust and gas using the Monte Carlo Bees Algorithm (cite: pham09)."] [Model Outputs: "The best-fit value for each parameter is taken as the mean of outputs from all the 10$^{7}$ models weighted by $\chi^{2}$, and the error on the parameters is the $\chi^{2}$ weighted standard deviation."]
  - validation: Compared 1-component, 2-component, and Turbulence models, and tested sub-solar vs solar metallicity constraints and sensitivity to CO(13-12) upper limits [N-component and Turbulence Models: "We also include these upper limits in the *1-component* and *Turbulence* models, although the results do not change significantly when the limits are not included."] [Gas Mass and SFR: "The total gas mass from the *2-component* models is consistent with the *Turbulence* models within uncertainties."]
- result: Mean gas density log(n_H2) from Turbulence LVG model (SPT0311-58 W) = 5.3 ± 1.3 log(cm^-3) [Model Outputs: "The best-fit mean gas densities are log(n$_{\rm H_{2}}$/cm$^{-3}$ ) = (5.3 $\pm$ 1.3) in W and (4.7 $\pm$ 2.4) in E from the *Turbulence* model."]
- result: Mean gas density log(n_H2) from Turbulence LVG model (SPT0311-58 E) = 4.7 ± 2.4 log(cm^-3) [Model Outputs: "The best-fit mean gas densities are log(n$_{\rm H_{2}}$/cm$^{-3}$ ) = (5.3 $\pm$ 1.3) in W and (4.7 $\pm$ 2.4) in E from the *Turbulence* model."]
- result: Gas kinetic temperature T_K from Turbulence LVG model (SPT0311-58 W) = 116 ± 42 K [Model Outputs: "The kinetic temperature from the *Turbulence* model is (116 $\pm$ 42) K in W and (166 $\pm$ 65) K in E."]
- result: Gas kinetic temperature T_K from Turbulence LVG model (SPT0311-58 E) = 166 ± 65 K [Model Outputs: "The kinetic temperature from the *Turbulence* model is (116 $\pm$ 42) K in W and (166 $\pm$ 65) K in E."]
- result: Intrinsic gas mass M_gas from Turbulence LVG model (SPT0311-58 W, sub-solar prior) = 5.4e11 ± 3.4e11 M_sun [Abstract: "We model the dust, CO, and \[CI\] using non-local thermodynamic equilibrium radiative transfer models and estimate the intrinsic gas mass to be (5.4 $\pm$ 3.4)$\times\rm 10^{11} \ \rm M_{\odot}$ in West and (3.1 $\pm$ 2.7)$\times\rm 10^{10} \ \rm M_{\odot}$ in East."]
- result: Intrinsic gas mass M_gas from Turbulence LVG model (SPT0311-58 E, sub-solar prior) = 3.1e10 ± 2.7e10 M_sun [Abstract: "We model the dust, CO, and \[CI\] using non-local thermodynamic equilibrium radiative transfer models and estimate the intrinsic gas mass to be (5.4 $\pm$ 3.4)$\times\rm 10^{11} \ \rm M_{\odot}$ in West and (3.1 $\pm$ 2.7)$\times\rm 10^{10} \ \rm M_{\odot}$ in East."]
- result: Intrinsic SFR from Turbulence LVG model (SPT0311-58 W) = 5046 ± 944 M_sun yr^-1 [Table 3: "| SFR | M$_{\odot}$ yr$^{-1}$ | | 5046 $\pm$ 944 | 5043 $\pm$ 949 | 701 $\pm$ 151 | 708 $\pm$ 151 |"]
- result: Intrinsic SFR from Turbulence LVG model (SPT0311-58 E) = 701 ± 151 M_sun yr^-1 [Table 3: "| SFR | M$_{\odot}$ yr$^{-1}$ | | 5046 $\pm$ 944 | 5043 $\pm$ 949 | 701 $\pm$ 151 | 708 $\pm$ 151 |"]
- limitation (authors): CO(3-2) from ATCA is spatially unresolved (5-6'') between W and E and had to be distributed by scaling to CO(6-5). [Ancillary Data: "Since $\rm CO(3-2)$ is spatially unresolved ($\sim$5 $-$ 6$''$) into W and E, we distribute the flux density of $\rm CO(3-2)$ by scaling to $\rm CO(6-5)$ in the two galaxies."]
- limitation (authors): Signal-to-noise is insufficient for lensing reconstruction in E and for p-H2O(2_1,1-2_0,2) in W, requiring adoption of fixed magnification factors from Marrone et al. (2018). - limitation (authors): [CI](2-1) and p-H2O(2_1,1-2_0,2) are not significantly detected in E, and the lack of [CI](1-0) observations prevents direct measurement of the [CI] excitation temperature and confirmation of the [CI] abundance-density relation. [Model Outputs: "However, it has to be noted that, due to the lack of \[CI\]$\rm (1-0)$ observations, we cannot reliably confirm the relationship between \[CI\] abundance and gas density."]

### 2023A&A...671A..69G: The Sun at millimeter wavelengths. IV. Magnetohydrodynamic waves in small-scale bright features (2023; sun; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Identify and study magnetohydrodynamic (MHD) waves and their energy flux in bright small-scale magnetic features in the solar chromosphere using ALMA Band 3 and Band 6 observations alongside Bifrost simulations.
- step 1: Synthesize and degrade simulation continuum brightness temperature maps to match ALMA observations
  - method: Solve radiative transfer with ART on Bifrost simulation snapshots to get Band 3 and Band 6 brightness temperatures, subsample frames to 2 s cadence with scan gaps matching ALMA, convolve with the median ALMA synthetic elliptical beam, and … [Simulation: "We used the Advanced Radiative Transfer code (cite: 2021_art) which solves the radiative transfer equation under the assumption of Local Thermodynamics Equilibrium (LTE)."] [Simulation: "For this, we have arbitrary chosen the snapshot corresponding to 700 s as starting point and so on removed every other frame, constructing a new time series wit the frames 700, 702, 704 and etc, until eventually we matched both ALMA observations time stamps."]
  - software: Bifrost, ART
  - parameter: Band 3 simulation snapshots: [700-1288, 1428-2022, 2172-2764]; Band 6 simulation snapshots: [700-1182, 1294-1770, 1913-2389, 2499-2981, 3120-3354] [Simulation: "In the case of Band 3, the 3 scans are composed of the snapshots [700-1288, 1428-2022, 2172-2764]. For the case of Band 6, the 5 scans correspond to the snapshots [700-1182, 1294-1770, 1913-2389, 2499-2981, 3120-3354]."]
  - parameter: Degraded pixel sizes: 0.34 arcsec (Band 3) and 0.14 arcsec (Band 6) [Simulation: "Subsequently, the pixel size of the simulation was changed from 0.066 arcsec to the respective 0.34 arcsec in Band 3 and 0.14 arcsec in Band 6."]
  - assumption: LTE radiative transfer using non-equilibrium electron densities from Bifrost; perfect spatial Fourier space sampling approximated by Gaussian beam convolution [Simulation: "We used the Advanced Radiative Transfer code (cite: 2021_art) which solves the radiative transfer equation under the assumption of Local Thermodynamics Equilibrium (LTE). In this work, ART uses the non-equilibrium electron densities provided by the Bifrost simulations"] [Simulation: "Please note that this approach assumes a perfect sampling of the spatial Fourier space while, in reality, ALMA's Fourier space coverage is sparse."]
  - validation: Tested a different starting snapshot for time series matching and confirmed it did not change final results [Simulation: "As a side note, we also did a test with a different starting snapshot and it did not change the final results"]
- limitation (authors): Degrading the simulation by Gaussian beam convolution assumes perfect spatial Fourier space sampling rather than ALMA's true sparse uv coverage. [Simulation: "Please note that this approach assumes a perfect sampling of the spatial Fourier space while, in reality, ALMA's Fourier space coverage is sparse."]
- limitation (authors): The feature tracking algorithm has a ~60% detection success rate when features overlap and systematically underestimates feature sizes by ~20%, which can affect radial velocity and energy flux estimates by a factor of sqrt(2). [Discussion and Conclusions: "20% for most common sizes in Band 3 and 6. We note that it does not affect the derived oscillation periods, or the measured temperatures and horizontal velocities."]
- limitation (authors): ALMA Band 3 and Band 6 observations were not simultaneous and lack direct wave propagation measurements between the two bands. [Discussion and Conclusions: "the fact that the ALMA data for Band 3 and Band 6 used here were not obtained simultaneous prevents drawing any strong conclusion regarding the differences in the found oscillations period distributions and its implications for any related decrease in the energy flux."]
- not stated (card): Hyperparameters (eps, min_samples, distance metric, and feature vector representation of light curves) used in the scikit-learn DBSCAN clustering to classify oscillating light curves; Mother wavelet function (e.g., Morlet) and wavenumber parameter used in the continuous wavelet transform (CWT); Value of the adiabatic index gamma and mean molecular weight assumed when computing the sound speed c_s…

- question: Do CO(1-0) and [CI](1-0) trace molecular gas consistently in unlensed SMGs at z=2-5, what are the cross-calibrated conversion factors (alpha_CO, X_[CI], alpha_850), and how does the warmer CMB affect the tracers

  - method: Combine M(H2)[CI] = 9.51e-5/(X_[CI] Q10) L'[CI] with M(H2)CO = alpha_CO L'CO to constrain X_[CI] x alpha_CO = 2.07e-4 L'[CI]/L'CO; test trends with z and L_IR [[C I](1–0) Line Emission as a Gas Mass …: "we can constrain the product of the two main unknowns for each tracer"]
  - software: LINMIX
  - parameter: T_ex = 30 K, Q10 = 0.46 (LTE) [[C I](1–0) Line Emission as a Gas Mass …: "= 30 K (cite: weiss2003; walter2011; harrington2021), which results in"]
  - parameter: alpha_CO = 1 M_sun (K km/s pc^2)^-1 for SMGs [[C I](1–0) Line Emission as a Gas Mass …: "is the CO-to-H$_2$ conversion factor, which we take to be 1 M$_\odot$"]
  - assumption: LTE with T_ex = 30 K gives about 4% higher mass than non-LTE [[C I](1–0) Line Emission as a Gas Mass …: "only implies a 4% higher mass compared more complex non–LTE approaches"]
  - fitting: LINMIX Bayesian linear regression including errors and upper limits; priors not stated [[C I](1–0) Line Emission as a Gas Mass …: "We look for any trends in the sample with LINMIX (cite: kelly2007), a Bayesian linear regression fitting model which can fit data with errors along with the upper limits."]
  - validation: slopes consistent with zero; z<3.5 and z>3.5 bins give consistent means [[C I](1–0) Line Emission as a Gas Mass …: "This is consistent with no dependence on either parameter, and we can thus compute a constant conversion factor."]
- step 9: Model CMB effect on CO and [CI] emission
  - method: RADEX non-LTE models of CO(1-0) and [CI](1-0) intensities and their ratio versus T_CMB for grids of density and kinetic temperature [CMB Effect on CO and [C I] Emission at …: "We use RADEX (cite: radex2007) for the models, a non-LTE radiative transfer code which uses the escape probability method"]
  - software: RADEX, LAMBDA database
  - parameter: ortho-to-para ratio 3:1; log(N/dv) = 18 for CO and C [CMB Effect on CO and [C I] Emission at …: "We fix the H$_2$ ortho–to–para ratio to 3:1 and set log($N_\mathrm{CO}$/$d$v) = log($N_\mathrm{C}$/$d$v) = 18."]
  - assumption: lower bound on T_kin from PDR CO(1-0) excitation temperature, 18-20 K [CMB Effect on CO and [C I] Emission at …: "this is 18–20 K, though the real"]
  - validation: effect on the line ratio compared with the scatter of the observed ratio [CMB Effect on CO and [C I] Emission at …: "Given the current scatter of the CO(1–0) and"]
- result: Slope of X_[CI] x alpha_CO vs redshift and vs L_IR = 0.02 and 0.01 ± 0.02 and 0.05 dimensionless [[C I](1–0) Line Emission as a Gas Mass …: "Our fits return a slope of 0.02$\pm$0.02 and 0.01$\pm$0.05 for the dependence on redshift and"]
- result: Scatter of X_[CI] x alpha_CO = 0.12 ± not stated dex [[C I](1–0) Line Emission as a Gas Mass …: "The full sample has a scatter of 0.12 dex in both cases."]
- result: Median X_[CI] x alpha_CO (mean 5.4 +/- 2.6) = 5.1e-5 ± 1.8e-5 M_sun (K km/s pc^2)^-1 [[C I](1–0) Line Emission as a Gas Mass …: "(5.1$\pm$1.8)$\times$10$^{-5}$ M$_\odot$ (K km s$^{-1}$ pc$^2$)$^{-1}$ and a mean (5.4$\pm$2.6)"]
- result: Mean X_[CI] x alpha_CO at z<3.5 versus z>3.5 = 5.6e-5 versus 5.1e-5 ± 2.0e-5 and 1.8e-5 M_sun (K km/s pc^2)^-1 [CMB Effect on CO and [C I] Emission at …: "we find consistent mean factors for both bins (5.6$\pm$2.0$\times$10$^{-5}$ M$_\odot$ (K km s$^{-1}$ pc$^2$)$^{-1}$ versus 5.1$\pm$1.8$\times$10$^{-5}$"]
- result: Median X_[CI] if alpha_CO = 3.6 = 1.4e-5 ± 0.5e-5 dimensionless [Discussion: "we derive a median $X_\mathrm{[CI]}=(1.4\pm0.5)\times10^{-5}$"]
- result: Mean X_[CI] if alpha_CO = 1 = 5.1e-5 ± 1.8e-5 dimensionless [Discussion: "we obtain a mean carbon abundance of $X_\mathrm{[CI]}=(5.1\pm1.8)\times10^{-5}$"]
- result: Gas masses M_H2,[CI] for X_[CI] ~ 5e-5 = 5-21e10 ± not stated M_sun [Discussion: "we derive gas masses $M_\mathrm{H_2,[CI]}$ in the range 5–21$\times10^{10} M_\odot$"]
- result: Maximum decrease of the L'[CI]/L'CO ratio from the warmer CMB at cold T_kin = ~30% ± not stated percent [CMB Effect on CO and [C I] Emission at …: "For the colder temperatures, the ratio can decrease by up to $\sim$30%."]
- result: Maximum change in derived molecular gas masses from the CMB = up to 70% ± not stated percent [abstract: "which can change the derived molecular gas masses by up to 70% for the coldest kinetic gas temperatures expected"]
- limitation (authors): Source companions are unresolved in AS2COS0001.1 and AS2COS0008.1, so [CI] fluxes are likely overestimated [Footnote 1: "the \[C I\] line flux densities are likely overestimated"]
- limitation (authors): Conversion factor constraints are limited to very active sources and need samples across masses and metallicities [Conclusions: "We caveat that current constraints on the conversion factors at high redshift are limited to typically very active sources."]

### 2020MNRAS.499.2493A: η Carinae: high angular resolution continuum, H30α and He30α ALMA images (2020; stellar_physics, evolved_stars; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Resolve the 230 GHz continuum and H30alpha/He30alpha recombination-line emission around eta Carinae at 50 mas to map the extended NW ionized region, its compact components, and the recent mass-loss history
- step 3: Extract spectra and identify compact line components L1-L16
  - method: Integrated spectra over the continuum region and a 0.09 arcsec radius compact region; iso-velocity channel maps, moment maps and peak maps; component spectra integrated over 66 mas radius [3.2 The H30alpha spectrum: "integrated over an area of $0.09\ensuremath{''}$ radius"] [3.3 The strong narrow H30alpha line: "We identified sixteen of these individual components, labeled L1 to L16; their spectra, integrated over a region of 66 mas radius"]
  - parameter: Channel width 1.262 km/s; moment-0 integrated between -25 and -116 km/s [3.3 The strong narrow H30alpha line: "Contour maps of the flux density, integrated over the line profile between $-25$ and $-116$ km s$^{-1}$ (zero momentum)"]
  - assumption: LTE used for the emission measures in Table 1 [3.3 The strong narrow H30alpha line: "assuming LTE conditions"]
  - validation: Table 1 estimated errors given (e.g. S(cont) = 0.04 Jy/beam) [Table 1: "Estimated errors: S(cont) = 0.04 Jy/beam"]
- step 6: NLTE model of compact ionized sources L1 and L14
  - method: Homogeneous ionized spheres; solve NLTE transfer for Te, ne and D matching continuum flux and maximum line intensity at several diameters [4.3 The physical parameters of the NW c…: "we assume that they are homogeneous ionized spheres, with diameter $D$ smaller than the HPBW"] [4.3 The physical parameters of the NW c…: "We modeled two components, L1 and L14, because each one of them seems to arise from a single velocity region."]
  - parameter: Te grid 1.0, 1.1, 1.2 x 1e4 K, ne solved to match continuum flux [4.3 The physical parameters of the NW c…: "three values for $T_e:$ (1.0, 1.1 and 1.2)$\times 10^4$ K"]
  - assumption: Sources are homogeneous ionized spheres with diameter below the HPBW [4.3 The physical parameters of the NW c…: "we assume that they are homogeneous ionized spheres, with diameter $D$ smaller than the HPBW"]
  - fitting: Grid in Te and diameter with interpolation to match model and observed line flux (no sampler or priors) [4.3 The physical parameters of the NW c…: "We then obtained, by interpolation, the values of $T_e$ and $n_e$ for which the model and observed line flux density coincide."]
  - validation: ne compatible with earlier ALMA Cycle 0 modelling; turnover frequency close to observing frequency [4.3 The physical parameters of the NW c…: "The values of $n_e$ are compatible with what was found in"]
- result: Narrow H30alpha line central velocity (integrated spectrum) = -57.8 ± not stated km/s [3.2 The H30alpha spectrum: "The central velocity of the narrow line is $-57.8$ km s$^{-1}$, its maximum flux density $60.4 \pm 0.1$ Jy and HPW 45 km s$^{-1}$."]
- result: Narrow H30alpha line maximum flux density (integrated spectrum) = 60.4 ± 0.1 Jy [3.2 The H30alpha spectrum: "The central velocity of the narrow line is $-57.8$ km s$^{-1}$, its maximum flux density $60.4 \pm 0.1$ Jy and HPW 45 km s$^{-1}$."]
- result: He30alpha maximum flux density = 3.9 ± 0.1 Jy [3.2 The H30alpha spectrum: "A weak line with $3.9 \pm 0.1$ Jy maximum flux density and $-176$ km s$^{-1}$ central velocity is identified with the He30"]
- result: Number of compact H30alpha components = 16 ± not stated dimensionless [3.3 The strong narrow H30alpha line: "We identified sixteen of these individual components, labeled L1 to L16"]
- result: Maximum negative high-velocity H30alpha flux density at -350 km/s = 0.43 ± 0.09 Jy/beam [3.5 High velocity H30alpha emission: "are $0.43 \pm 0.09$ Jy beam$^{-1}$ at $-350$ km s$^{-1}$ and $0.13 \pm 0.02$ Jy beam$^{-1}$ at 115 km s$^{-1}$, respectively"]
- result: Maximum positive high-velocity H30alpha flux density at 115 km/s = 0.13 ± 0.02 Jy/beam [3.5 High velocity H30alpha emission: "are $0.43 \pm 0.09$ Jy beam$^{-1}$ at $-350$ km s$^{-1}$ and $0.13 \pm 0.02$ Jy beam$^{-1}$ at 115 km s$^{-1}$, respectively"]
- result: Mass of model sources L1 and L14 = L1: 0.9 to 3.0e-5; L14: 1.8 to 4.0e-5 ± not stated Msun [4.3 The physical parameters of the NW c…: "vary between 0.9 and 3.0$\times 10^{-5}$ M$_\odot$ for L1 and 1.8 and 4.0$\times 10^{-5}$ M$_\odot$ for L14"]
- result: Total mass of the extended NW region = 1e-4 to 1e-3 ± not stated Msun [4.3 The physical parameters of the NW c…: "we obtain a total mass for the extended region of $10^{-4} - 10^{-3}$ M$_\odot$"]
- limitation (authors): Lower flux than in 2012 may be due to emission outside the maximum recoverable scale [3.1 Continuum emission: "the lower flux density obtained in the present observations can be due to the existence of emission outside the maximum recovery scale of the ALMA configuration."]
- limitation (authors): Unclear whether the narrow line in the compact-source spectrum belongs to the compact source [3.2 The H30alpha spectrum: "It is not clear if the narrow line belongs to the compact source or if it only represents the contribution of the superimposed extended NW region."]
- limitation (authors): Blob proper motions depend on identification of Weigelt blobs with ALMA sources [4.5 The Weigelt blobs: "Of course, this result depends on the accuracy of the identification of the Weigelt blobs with the compact sources detected by ALMA"]
