### 2015RAA....15.2213A: <SUP>12</SUP>CO emission from the Red Rectangle (2015; evolved_stars; archival_reuse)
- question: What are the morphology, temperature, density and kinematics of the CO gas envelope of the Red Rectangle, from archival ALMA CO(3-2) and CO(6-5) data?
- step 2: Reconstruct effective density
  - method: Iteratively solve F(y,z)=∫ρ(ξ,z)dx assuming rotational symmetry, 35 ξ bins x 50 z bins of 0.1'' [GAS EFFECTIVE DENSITY: "The integral equation is solved by iteration, using as input the fluxes, integrated over Doppler velocities, measured in each of the 1526 pixels that have been retained."]
  - parameter: uncertainties: absolute 0.16 (3-2) and 2.2 (6-5) Jy arcsec^-2 plus 20% relative; ten iterations [GAS EFFECTIVE DENSITY: "Ten iterations are amply sufficient to achieve convergence."]
  - assumption: Rotational symmetry, negligible absorption, LTE [MAIN FEATURES: "The assumption of rotational invariance about the star axis implies in addition that absorption is negligible."]
  - fitting: chi2 minimization [GAS EFFECTIVE DENSITY: "The iterative process is stopped when the value of $\chi^2$ used to measure the quality of the agreement"]
  - validation: Compared observed and reintegrated flux densities in 8 R and 8 angle bins [GAS EFFECTIVE DENSITY: "we compare in Figure 5 the observed flux densities with those obtained by integration of the effective densities along the line of sight"]
- step 3: Derive temperature from CO ratio
  - method: k_BT=(E6-E3)/ln(C/R_T) with (E6-E3)/kB=82.5 K, C=15.6 [TEMPERATURE AND DENSITY DISTRIBUTIONS: "In the present case, $(E_6-E_3)/k_B$=82.5 K and $C$=15.6."]
  - assumption: LTE, optically thin [TEMPERATURE AND DENSITY DISTRIBUTIONS: "In a regime of thermal equilibrium, and in the absence of significant absorption"]
  - validation: Compared with Men'shchikov et al. 2002 dust temperature and CO density [TEMPERATURE AND DENSITY DISTRIBUTIONS: "It is in good agreement with a model (Men’shchikov et al. 2002)"]
- result: Average temperature = 63 ± not stated K [MAIN FEATURES: "their ratio, 4.3, corresponds to an average temperature of 63 K"]
- limitation (authors): Imperfect deconvolution/calibration and crude symmetry and optically thin assumptions [DATA: "most of the flux fluctuations observed here arise from imperfect deconvolution and/or calibration"]
- not stated (card): Calibration and imaging software/CASA version and parameters of the archive-delivered cubes; Exact regridding/rotation interpolation method; Initial guesses and optimizer for chi2 minimization; Rest frequencies used; Observing dates and times on source

### 2026ApJ...999L..32R: CH<SUB>3</SUB>OH and HCN in Interstellar Comet 3I/ATLAS Mapped with the ALMA Atacama Compact Array: Distinct Outgassing Behaviors and a Remarkably High CH<SUB>… (2026; solar_system, astrochemistry; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Measure molecular production rates, kinematics, parent scale lengths, and coma radial temperature profiles of CH3OH and HCN in interstellar comet 3I/ATLAS pre-perihelion, and place it into context with solar system comets.
- step 2: Construct non-LTE radiative transfer models and sample visibilities in the Fourier domain
  - method: Generate 1D and 3D non-LTE radiative transfer model cubes using SUBLIME with Haser radial density profiles, time-average observed visibilities, Fourier-transform model cubes onto the observed uv coverage with vis_sample, and optimize param… [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "We modeled molecular line emission using the SUBLIME radiative transfer code for cometary atmospheres (cite: Cordiner2022)."] [Fourier Domain Modeling: "We then used the `vis_sample` program (cite: Loomis2018) to take the Fourier transform of our radiative transfer model cubes using the same $uv$ coverage as our (time averaged) ALMA observations"]
  - software: SUBLIME, vis-sample, lmfit
  - parameter: CH3OH-H2O average collisional cross-section = 5 x 10^-14 cm^-2 [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "using an average collisional cross-section with H$_2$O of $5\times10^{-14}$ cm$^{-2}$."]
  - parameter: Q(OH) = (5.7 +/- 0.6) x 10^28 s^-1 at r_H = 1.43 au; branching ratio = 85.5% [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "we scaled the $Q$(OH)=$(5.7\pm0.6)\times10^{28}$ s$^{-1}$ measured by (cite: Crovisier2025) at *r*~*H*~ = 1.43 au to the *r*~*H*~ of the ACA observations assuming insolation-driven (*r*~*H*~$^{-2}$) dependence and an branching ratio of 85.5% (cite: Huebner2015)."]
  - parameter: 3D cone geometry: phi = phi_STO, psi = psi_sun, gamma = 90 deg [Determination of CH$_3$OH Outgassing Ge…: "We assumed hemispheric asymmetry along the projected Sun-comet vector ($\phi=\phi_\mathrm{STO}$, $\psi=\psi_{\ensuremath{\odot}}$, $\gamma=90\ensuremath{^\circ}$)."]
  - assumption: Insolation-driven r_H^-2 scaling of Q(H2O) from Q(OH); thermalization approximation for CH3OH-H2O collisions; Haser density distribution; constant expansion velocity in each region [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "There are no published state-to-state CH$_3$OH-H$_2$O collisional rates, so we calculated them using the thermalization approximation (cite: Crovisier1987; Biver1999; Bockelee-Morvan2012)"] [Determination of CH$_3$OH Outgassing Ge…: "We determined the CH$_3$OH distribution in $R_1, R_2$ using a Haser formalism (cite: Haser1957)"]
  - fitting: Levenberg-Marquardt least-squares minimization in lmfit on real and imaginary visibilities normalized by per-baseline noise; uncertainties from diagonal elements of the covariance matrix [Fourier Domain Modeling: "We used the `lmfit` application of the Levenberg-Marquardt minimization technique and retrieved uncertainties on our optimized parameters from the diagonal elements of the covariance matrix, minimizing the residual for the real and imaginary parts of the observed and modeled interferometric visibilities."] [Fourier Domain Modeling: "We normalized the residuals by the measured per-baseline noise at each iteration of the fitting process."]
  - validation: Compared 1D and 3D models via F-test on residual sum of squares (F = 1.45 vs critical value 2.99 at 95% confidence) [Determination of CH$_3$OH Production Me…: "We performed an F-test calculating the residual sum of squares (RSS) between each model and the data and determining the F value as $F = \frac{RSS_{\mathrm{1D}}-RSS_{\mathrm{3D}}}{RSS_{\mathrm{3D}}}\frac{n-p_{\mathrm{3D}}}{p_{\mathrm{3D}}-p_{\mathrm{1D}}}$"] [Determination of CH$_3$OH Production Me…: "For $\alpha=0.05$ (95% confidence), the critical value for an F-distribution with $(p_{\mathrm{3D}}-p_{\mathrm{1D}},n-p_{\mathrm{3D}})$ degrees of freedom is 2.99, whereas our F-value is 1.45."]
- limitation (authors): Low signal-to-noise on the longest ACA baselines prevented definitively ruling out CH3OH as purely a parent species. [Abstract: "However low signal-to-noise on long baselines, which sample emission on small spatial scales closest to the nucleus, prevented definitively ruling out CH$_3$OH as purely a parent species."]
- limitation (authors): Low spectral resolution (0.87 km s^-1) on Aug 28, Sept 18 (low-res), Sept 22, and Oct 1 was comparable to the line FWHM, preventing direct retrieval of kinematics or L_p on those dates. [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "For the low spectral resolution observations, the line full width at half maximum (FWHM) was comparable to the instrumental resolution; therefore, we could not retrieve robust kinematics directly from fits to the lines."]
- limitation (authors): Absence of contemporaneous Q(H2O) measurements required scaling Q(OH) measured at r_H = 1.43 au assuming r_H^-2 insolation-driven dependence, and CH3OH/HCN and CH3OH/H2O ratios relied on interpolating/extrapolating non-simultaneous CH3OH m… [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "In the absence of contemporaneously measured $Q$(H$_2$O) values, we scaled the $Q$(OH)=$(5.7\pm0.6)\times10^{28}$ s$^{-1}$ measured by (cite: Crovisier2025) at *r*~*H*~ = 1.43 au to the *r*~*H*~ of the ACA observations assuming insolation-driven (*r*~*H*~$^{-2}$) dependence"]
- not stated (card): Duration/interval used when time-averaging the observed visibilities prior to Fourier-domain fitting; Continuum subtraction procedure (or line-free channel definitions) prior to spectral line imaging and visibility fitting; Image pixel cell size and image dimensions used during CASA deconvolution; Bandpass and phase calibrator source names for each epoch; Grid of L_p values sampled when construct…

### 2017ApJ...850..170O: High Dense Gas Fraction in Intensely Star-forming Dusty Galaxies (2017; high_z_galaxies; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Do lensed high-z dusty starbursts (SDP.9, SDP.11) have dense gas excitation, line ratios and L_IR-L_dense relations like local (U)LIRGs, and is their dense gas fraction higher?
- step 4: Non-LTE modelling of CO SLED and CO+HCN SLEDs
  - method: Grid of Myradex LVG models, likelihood marginalised over parameters [4.2: "We marginalize the parameters by integrating the likelihood distribution over all other parameters."]
  - software: Myradex
  - parameter: n_H2 = 1e2-1e8 cm^-3; Tkin = 10^0.5-10^3 K; dv/dr = 1-1e3 km/s/pc [4.2: "number density of molecular hydrogen $n_{\rm H_2} = 10^2$ – $10^8$ cm$^{-3}$, kinetic temperature $T_{\rm kin} = 10^{0.5} - 10^3 \, {\rm K}$"]
  - assumption: CO abundance 1e-4, T_CMB=7.004 K, HCN abundance 2e-8, single component [4.2: "We adopt a CO abundance relative to H$_2$ of $10^{-4}$."] [4.3: "We keep the HCN abundance (relative to ${\rm H_2}$) to be 2$\times 10^{-8}$"]
  - fitting: Likelihood grid, marginalisation [4.2: "we calculate the likelihood distributions for all parameter combinations following (cite: Ward2003ApJ...587..171W)"]
  - validation: Consistency of Tkin and column density between CO-only and CO+HCN fits [4.3: "We see that the $T_{\rm kin}$ and column density are consistent with the CO-based results."]
- result: CO SLED best-fit n(H2) = 3.2e5 ± not stated cm^-3 [4.2: "$n(\rm H_2) = 3.2 \times 10^{5} \, {\rm cm^{-3}}$"]
- result: CO SLED M_H2 SDP.9 = 1.3e11 ± not stated Msun [4.2: "$M_{\rm H_2} = 1.3 \times 10^{11} \, M_\odot$"]
- limitation (authors): Differential magnification is a main uncertainty [3 Lens modelling: "The possible differential magnification between different transitions represents one of the main sources of uncertainty in the analysis presented in this work."]
- limitation (authors): Scatter and lack of homogeneous high-z samples prevent redshift trends [abstract: "prevents us from distinguishing differential trends with redshift"]
- not stated (card): ALMA CASA version and calibrators; Line-free channels and moment-map velocity ranges; Image size and cell size; uvmcmcfit priors and run settings; Myradex grid sampling and priors; Uncertainty derivation for line fluxes; Conversion of L' used for CO(1-0) and HCN luminosities, incl. L_IR demagnification details

### 2021A&A...648A..38G: CO<SUP>+</SUP> as a probe of the origin of CO in diffuse interstellar clouds (2021; milky_way_ism, astrochemistry; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Is thermal cosmic-ray and UV-driven chemistry producing CO in diffuse interstellar molecular gas through the intermediate formation of CO+?
- step 2: Integrate opacity and derive column densities / limits
  - method: Integrate line profiles over fixed velocity intervals; 3-sigma upper limits for non-detections; convert integrated opacity to column density [Table 2: "Upper limits are 3$\sigma$."] [3.1 The sight-lines toward J1717-3342 a…: "Therefore, the CO$^+$ column densities have been derived assuming an excitation temperature of 2.73 K."]
  - parameter: Integration velocity intervals -14..10 km/s (J1717-3342) and -20..20 km/s (J1744-3116) [Table 2: "J1717-3342 | 352.7333 | 2.3911 | -14..10"] [Table 2: "J1744-3116 | 357.8635 | -0.9968 | -20..20"]
  - parameter: N(HCO+) = 1.12e12 cm^-2 x integral tau dv [2.4 Other data discussed here: "using N(HCO$^+$) = $1.12\times 10^{12}~{\rm cm}^{-2}\int \tau ~{\rm dv}$ as before"]
  - assumption: CO+ excitation set by the CMB at 2.73 K [2.1 Spectroscopy: "assuming that the energy level population is determined by the cosmic microwave background at 2.73 K"]
  - assumption: 13CO and C18O excitation temperature 5 K [3.1 The sight-lines toward J1717-3342 a…: "we assume an excitation temperature of 5 K (Table 1)"]
- result: CO+ J=5/2-3/2 integrated opacity limit, J1717-3342 = <0.027 ± 3 sigma upper limit km/s [Table 2: "CO$^+$ J=5/2-3/2 | 0.780 | 0.0027 | $<$0.027"]
- result: CO+ J=3/2-1/2 integrated opacity limit, J1717-3342 = <0.033 ± 3 sigma upper limit km/s [Table 2: "CO$^+$ J=3/2-1/2 | 0.780 | 0.0034 | $<$0.033"]
- result: CO+ J=5/2-3/2 integrated opacity limit, J1744-3116 = <0.087 ± 3 sigma upper limit km/s [Table 2: "CO$^+$ J=5/2-3/2 | 0.234 | 0.0084 | $<$0.087"]
- result: CO+ J=3/2-1/2 integrated opacity limit, J1744-3116 = <0.111 ± 3 sigma upper limit km/s [Table 2: "CO$^+$ J=3/2-1/2 | 0.251 | 0.0107 | $<$0.111"]
- result: N(HCO+) toward J1717-3342 = 7.49 ± 0.03 10^12 cm^-2 [Table 3: "J1717-3342 | $7.49\pm 0.03$ | $< 0.30$ | $<$ 0.040 | $< 1.2 \times 10^{-10}$"]
- result: N(CO+) toward J1717-3342 = <0.30 ± 3 sigma upper limit 10^12 cm^-2 [Table 3: "J1717-3342 | $7.49\pm 0.03$ | $< 0.30$ | $<$ 0.040 | $< 1.2 \times 10^{-10}$"]
- result: N(HCO+) toward J1744-3116 (printed J1744-3416 in Table 3) = 23.35 ± 0.06 10^12 cm^-2 [Table 3: "J1744-3416 | $23.35 \pm0.06$ | $< 0.98$ | $<$0.042 | $<1.3 \times 10^{-10}$"]
- result: N(CO+) toward J1744-3116 = <0.98 ± 3 sigma upper limit 10^12 cm^-2 [Table 3: "J1744-3416 | $23.35 \pm0.06$ | $< 0.98$ | $<$0.042 | $<1.3 \times 10^{-10}$"]
- result: Fractional rms flux density, J1717-3342 = 0.22% ± not stated percent of continuum flux density [3.2 CO+ as a source of HCO+: "achieved fractional rms flux density levels $\Delta{\rm S}_\nu/{\rm S}_\nu$ of 0.22% and 0.83% for J1717-3342 and J1744-3116 respectively"]
- result: Fractional rms flux density, J1744-3116 = 0.83% ± not stated percent of continuum flux density [3.2 CO+ as a source of HCO+: "achieved fractional rms flux density levels $\Delta{\rm S}_\nu/{\rm S}_\nu$ of 0.22% and 0.83% for J1717-3342 and J1744-3116 respectively"]
- result: 13CO(2-1) integrated opacity and N(13CO), J1717-3342 = 0.260 km/s; 6.3e14 cm^-2 ± 0.004 km/s; 0.4e14 cm^-2 km/s; cm^-2 [Table 4: "0.818 | 0.0024 | 0.260(0.004) | 6.3(0.4)"]
- result: C18O(2-1) integrated opacity and N(C18O), J1717-3342 = <0.055 km/s; <1.3e14 cm^-2 ± 3 sigma upper limit km/s; cm^-2 [Table 4: "0.822 | 0.0123 | $<$ 0.055 | $<1.3$"]
- result: 13CO(2-1) integrated opacity and N(13CO), J1744-3116 = 2.975 km/s; 72.0e14 cm^-2 ± 0.023 km/s; 0.6e14 cm^-2 km/s; cm^-2 [Table 4: "0.244 | 0.0073 | 2.975(0.023) | 72.0(0.6)"]
- result: C18O(2-1) integrated opacity and N(C18O), J1744-3116 = 0.102 km/s; 2.5e14 cm^-2 ± 0.018 km/s; 0.4e14 cm^-2 km/s; cm^-2 [Table 4: "0.244 | 0.0084 | 0.102(0.018) | 2.5(0.4)"]
- result: N(13CO)/N(C18O) toward J1744-3116 = 29 ± 5 dimensionless [3.1 The sight-lines toward J1717-3342 a…: "N($\mathrm{^{13}CO}$)/N($\mathrm{C^{18}O}$) = 29$\pm5$ toward J1744-3116"]
- limitation (authors): The sample of sight lines used for n(H)/G0 includes CO and HCO+ detections, which may bias densities high [5 Conditions for the growth of CO: "which may bias the derived $n({\rm H}/G_0$ to somewhat high values as a higher density implies a faster CO production rate"]
- limitation (authors): Aspects of the C+ + H2O reaction (rate, branching ratio) may not be fully understood, so the HOC+ comparison is uncertain [4 The thermal chemistry of CO+, HOC+ an…: "or perhaps some aspect of the reaction of C$^+$ and $\mathrm{{\rm H}_2}$O is not fully understood"]
- not stated (card): CASA / pipeline version and which calibration (manual or pipeline) produced the products used; Flux and phase calibrators, observing dates, array configuration, baselines and time on source; How the continuum level was set and spectra normalised to obtain optical depth (continuum-subtraction, line-free channels); How the channel rms (sigma l/c) was measured and how the 3-sigma integrated opacity …

### 2019ApJ...880..157D: ALMA and HST Kiloparsec-scale Imaging of a Quasar-galaxy Merger at Z ≈ 6.2 (2019; agn_and_quasars, high_z_galaxies; observational_alma_primary)
- question: What are the morphology, kinematics and ISM/star-formation properties of the z=6.23 quasar PJ308-21 and its extended companion components, and are they a merger or an outflow?
- step 3: Derive gas and dust masses, IR luminosities
  - method: [CII] optically thin LTE C+ mass, converted with solar C/H; alternative alpha_[CII]=30; modified blackbody for IR and dust mass [3.2 Gas and dust masses: "we infer $M_{\rm C+}$ = $(13.9,\,5.1,\,2.7)\times10^6$ M$_\odot$ for Q, E, and W, respectively."]
  - parameter: Tex=100 K; Tdust=35-45 K; beta=1.6 [3.2 Gas and dust masses: "where the range refers to dust temperatures spanning between 35 and 45 K"]
  - assumption: Optically thin [CII], LTE, C/H=2.95e-4, gas-to-dust 100 for comparison, Tdust=35 K adopted [3.2 Gas and dust masses: "in the following we assume $T_{\rm dust}$=35 K everywhere in the system"]
  - validation: Compared the [CII]-based and gas-to-dust gas masses [3.2 Gas and dust masses: "a typical gas–to–dust ratio of 100 (cite: berta16) would yield a significantly lower gas mass"]
- result: 1.1 mm continuum flux Q/E/W = 1.01 / 0.19 / 0.13 ± not stated mJy [3.2 Gas and dust masses: "$F_{\rm 1.1mm}$=1.01, 0.19, and 0.13 mJy for Q, E, and W respectively"]
- result: gas mass from [CII] (alpha=30) Q/E/W = 13.9 / 5.1 / 2.7 e10 ± not stated Msun [3.2 Gas and dust masses: "$M_{\rm gas}=(13.9, 5.1, 2.7)\times 10^{10}$ M$_\odot$"]
- limitation (authors): Gas masses are lower limits/order-of-magnitude; PSF color systematics; Q UV is a lower limit [3.2 Gas and dust masses: "These are lower limits in that the estimated gas mass would increase"]
- not stated (card): Cell size and image size of the cube; Line-free channel ranges for continuum; Aperture definitions for Q, E, W flux extraction; CASA version; Cleaning threshold/masking in tclean; Whether archive products or own calibration were used; HST aperture shapes and UV luminosity-to-SFR details; Toy tidal model code and parameter grid; the Q circular velocity and host mass values

### 2026ApJ..1000..141K: First Determination of the Cosmic Microwave Background Radiation Temperature at z = 0.68 Using Molecular Absorption Lines (2026; cosmology, agn_and_quasars, astrochemistry; archival_reuse)
- question: What is the cosmic microwave background (CMB) radiation temperature at z = 0.68 derived from rotational molecular absorption lines toward the lensed quasar B0218+357, and is it consistent with the standard Big Bang cosmological model?
- step 4: Derive excitation temperatures T_ex from J=2-1 and J=3-2 optical depths
  - method: Resample HCN, HCO+, HNC spectra to ΔV_bar = 0.909 km/s and fit Boltzmann optical depth relation (Eq. 3) per velocity bin where tau < 1, then take weighted mean over +5 km/s component; for H13CN and H13CO+, fit single Gaussian to optical de… [HCN, HCO+, and HNC: "All spectra of these molecules were resampled to match the velocity spacing of the *J*=3–2 data, which have the lowest spectral resolution ($\Delta V_\mathrm{bar}=0.909\,\mathrm{km\,s^{-1}}$)."] [HCN, HCO+, and HNC: "$T_\mathrm{ex}$ and $N$ were determined by performing a weighted least-squares fit of Equation (\[eq:tobesolved\]) to the integrated optical depth calculated in each velocity bin."]
  - software: SciPy, RADEX
  - parameter: ΔV_bar = 0.909 km/s; tau < 1 cutoff; initial T_ex = 4.59 K; initial N = 7.8e13, 4.5e13, 2.6e13, 2.9e12, 2.0e12 cm^-2 for HCN, HCO+, HNC, H13CN, H13CO+ [HCN, HCO+, and HNC: "The initial value of $T_\mathrm{ex}$ was set to the standard Big Bang model prediction, $T_\mathrm{CMB}(z=0.68)=4.59\,\mathrm{K}$."] [HCN, HCO+, and HNC: "To ensure that the derived excitation temperature reliably reflects the CMB temperature, the analysis was restricted to the velocity ranges where the optical depth is less than unity."]
  - assumption: Rotational levels follow a Boltzmann distribution characterized by a single excitation temperature T_ex [Fundamentals: "Under the assumption that the molecular rotational transitions are characterized by a single excitation temperature ($T_{\rm ex}$) but are not necessarily thermalized (cite: WiklindCombes_1995), $N_{J+1}$ and $N_J$ follow a Boltzmann distribution determined by the $T_{\rm ex}$."]
  - fitting: Weighted least-squares fit of Eq. 3 with uncertainties from 68% confidence interval of Monte Carlo distribution [HCN, HCO+, and HNC: "$T_\mathrm{ex}$ and $N$ were determined by performing a weighted least-squares fit of Equation (\[eq:tobesolved\]) to the integrated optical depth calculated in each velocity bin."] [HCN, HCO+, and HNC: "The uncertainty in $T_\mathrm{ex}$ corresponds to the 68% confidence interval of the Monte Carlo-derived distribution."]
  - validation: RADEX non-LTE calculations (at T_kin = 55 K, n(H2) = 5e3 cm^-3) performed to assess collisional excitation, leading to exclusion of HCO+ [HCN, HCO+, and HNC: "To quantitatively assess the impact of collisional excitation, we performed RADEX (cite: vanderTak_2007) calculations adopting the physical conditions of the $z\!=\!0.68$ absorbing cloud ($T_\mathrm{kin}\!=\!55 \,\mathrm{K}$ and $n(\mathrm{H_2})\!=\!5\times10^3\, \mathrm{cm^{-3}}$) reported by (cite: Henkel_2005)."]
- step 5: Determine T_CMB(z=0.68) and constrain cosmological deviation parameter beta
  - method: Compute weighted mean of HCN and HNC excitation temperatures assuming T_CMB = T_ex, and fit Lima et al. (2000) scaling relation T_CMB(z) = T_0(1+z)^(1-beta) to combined literature compilation [HCN, HCO+, and HNC: "For this reason, we regard the HCN and HNC results as the most reliable tracers of the CMB temperature in the $z\!=\!0.68$ absorber toward the A image of B0218+357. We then applied the weighted-mean procedure described by Equation (\[meanTex\]) to the excitation temperatures of these two species."] [Redshift Dependence of T_CMB: "We fitted these data using the phenomenological model proposed by (cite: Lima_2000), $$\begin{equation} \label{eq:btcmb} T_\mathrm{CMB}(z)=T_0\,(1+z)^{1-\beta}, \end{equation}$$ and obtained ${\beta=(3.9^{+7.4}_{-8.2})\times10^{-3}}$."]
  - software: SciPy, Matplotlib
  - parameter: T_0 = 2.72548 +/- 0.00057 K [Introduction: "The cosmic microwave background (CMB; (cite: Penzias_1965; Dicke_1965)) is a nearly uniform, isotropic blackbody radiation field with a current temperature of $2.72548\pm0.00057\,\mathrm{K}$ (cite: Fixsen_2009)."]
  - assumption: Radiative equilibrium with the CMB (T_CMB = T_ex), negligible local dust emission and negligible collisional excitation for HCN and HNC in optically thin regime [T_CMB Calculations: "Under these conditions, the rotational level populations of the highly polar molecules detected in this absorber, namely HCN, HCO$^+$, HNC, and their isotopologues, are in radiative equilibrium with the CMB."]
  - fitting: Weighted mean for T_CMB(z=0.68); fit of T_CMB(z) = T_0(1+z)^(1-beta) across ~80 literature points plus new measurement [Redshift Dependence of T_CMB: "We fitted these data using the phenomenological model proposed by (cite: Lima_2000), $$\begin{equation} \label{eq:btcmb} T_\mathrm{CMB}(z)=T_0\,(1+z)^{1-\beta}, \end{equation}$$ and obtained ${\beta=(3.9^{+7.4}_{-8.2})\times10^{-3}}$."]
  - validation: Compared derived T_CMB with standard Big Bang prediction (4.59 K) and SZ measurements at z ~ 0.68 [HCN, HCO+, and HNC: "This value is in good agreement, within the uncertainties, with the prediction of the standard Big Bang model ($4.59~\mathrm{K}$)."]
- result: CMB temperature at z = 0.68 = 4.50 ± ±0.17 K [Abstract: "From a weighted mean of the excitation temperatures obtained from HCN and HNC, we determined the CMB temperature at $z\!=\!0.68$ to be ${4.50\pm0.17\,\mathrm{K}}$."]
- result: Weighted mean excitation temperature of HCN (+5 km/s component) = 4.42 ± +0.20/-0.20 K [Table 1: "| HCN | $-3.36$–$+12.9$ | 6 | $4.42^{+0.20}_{-0.20}$ |"]
- result: Weighted mean excitation temperature of HCO+ (+5 km/s component) = 5.62 ± +0.26/-0.25 K [Table 1: "| HCO$^{+}$ | $-2.69$–$+12.6$ | 8 | $5.62^{+0.26}_{-0.25}$ |"]
- result: Weighted mean excitation temperature of HNC (+5 km/s component) = 4.71 ± +0.35/-0.32 K [Table 1: "| HNC | $-0.88$–$+10.8$ | 9 | $4.71^{+0.35}_{-0.32}$ |"]
- result: Excitation temperature of H13CN (+5 km/s component) = 4.93 ± +0.61/-0.49 K [Table 1: "| H$^{13}$CN | $-5.00$–$+15.0$ | ... | $4.93^{+0.61}_{-0.49}$ |"]
- result: Excitation temperature of H13CO+ (+5 km/s component) = 4.69 ± +0.51/-0.40 K [Table 1: "| H$^{13}$CO$^{+}$ | $-5.00$–$+15.0$ | ... | $4.69^{+0.51}_{-0.40}$ |"]
- result: CMB temperature redshift-evolution deviation parameter beta = 3.9e-3 ± +7.4e-3/-8.2e-3 dimensionless [Redshift Dependence of T_CMB: "We fitted these data using the phenomenological model proposed by (cite: Lima_2000), $$\begin{equation} \label{eq:btcmb} T_\mathrm{CMB}(z)=T_0\,(1+z)^{1-\beta}, \end{equation}$$ and obtained ${\beta=(3.9^{+7.4}_{-8.2})\times10^{-3}}$."]
- limitation (authors): The Band 3 and Band 4 observations were separated by ~9 months (October 2016 to July 2017) without simultaneous millimeter monitoring, though a few percent variability would shift T_CMB by < 0.1 K. [Time Variability: "The Band 3 and Band 4 observations used in this study are separated by approximately nine months (from October 2016 to July 2017)"]
- limitation (authors): The -6 km/s component cannot constrain f_c due to lack of saturation, leaving its T_ex uncertain. [HCN, HCO+, and HNC: "In contrast, the $-6\,\mathrm{km\,s^{-1}}$ component shows no evidence of saturation, preventing a meaningful constraint on $f_\mathrm{c}$ in that velocity range; consequently, the associated $T_\mathrm{ex}$ remains uncertain."]
- limitation (authors): H13CN and H13CO+ transitions have low S/N (~3), limiting their statistical significance. [Summary and Future Prospects: "The excitation temperatures derived from H$^{13}$CN and H$^{13}$CO$^{+}$ are also consistent with the prediction of the standard Big Bang model, although their statistical significance is limited by the lower S/N of the data."]
- not stated (card): Exact CASA version and UVMULTIFIT version used for data reduction and visibility fitting; Rest frequencies, Einstein A coefficients, level energies, and partition function values/tables adopted for HCN, HCO+, HNC, H13CN, and H13CO+; Exact velocity ranges defining line-free continuum channels used in UVMULTIFIT to determine peak positions and normalize the absorption spectra; Explicit mathematical…

### 2017ApJ...848..101V: A <SUP>13</SUP>CO Detection in a Brightest Cluster Galaxy (2017; galaxy_clusters_and_sz, milky_way_ism, nearby_galaxies; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Can 13CO(3-2) emission in a BCG give an LTE molecular gas mass and a CO-to-H2 conversion factor to test the Galactic value?
- step 4: Line ratios and excitation temperature
  - method: r31 and R32 from peak temperatures; Tex and filling factor from Rayleigh-Jeans equation assuming optically thick 12CO and LTE [CO (3-2)/(1-0): "The ratio of CO (3-2)/(1-0) brightness temperatures for the $^{13}$CO-bright component, $0.71\pm0.14$, implies an excitation temperature of $T_{\rm ex}\approx 15\rm\; K$."]
  - assumption: optically thick 12CO, same filling factor for 1-0 and 3-2, LTE [CO (3-2)/(1-0): "We also assume that the CO(1-0) and CO(3-2) emission originate from the same regions in the molecular clouds, so the area filling factors are the same."]
- step 5: LTE 13CO column density, mass and alpha_CO
  - method: 13CO column from optical depth and Tex; N(H2) via abundances; mass = 1.36 m_H2 N A_source; alpha_CO = M/L'_10 with L'_10 = L'_32/r31 [CO-to-H$_2$ Conversion Factor: "The total molecular gas mass is then computed from $M_{\rm mol} = 1.36 m_{\rm H_2} N_{\rm H_2} A_{\rm source}$"]
  - parameter: Tex=15 K, filling factor 0.1, 13W32=22.8 K km/s, A_reg=11.2 kpc^2 [CO-to-H$_2$ Conversion Factor: "For $T_{\rm ex}=15\rm\; K$, $\Phi_A=0.1$, and $^{13}W_{32}=22.8\hbox{$\rm\; K\rm\; km\rm\; s^{-1}\,$}$"]
  - assumption: [12CO]/[13CO]=50, [12CO]/[H2]=1e-4, LTE, 13CO optically thin with finite-opacity correction [CO-to-H$_2$ Conversion Factor: "Assuming the abundance ratios $[^{12}{\rm CO}]/[^{13}{\rm CO}] = 50$ and $[^{12}{\rm CO}]/[{\rm H}_2] = 10^{-4}$"]
  - validation: Optical depth cross-checked with 12CO/13CO ratio method (0.36); repeated for full region [CO-to-H$_2$ Conversion Factor: "This method yields an optical depth of $0.36$"]
- result: 13CO-traced molecular gas mass (LTE) = 2.1e9 ± not stated Msun [CO-to-H$_2$ Conversion Factor: "This gives a total molecular gas mass of $2.1\ensuremath{\times 10^{9}}\hbox{$\rm\thinspace M_{\odot}$}$."]
- result: alpha_CO (13CO-bright component) = 2.26 ± not stated Msun (K km/s pc^2)^-1 [CO-to-H$_2$ Conversion Factor: "giving a CO-to-H$_2$ conversion factor of $\alpha_{\rm CO} = 2.26\hbox{$\rm\thinspace M_{\odot}$}(\hbox{$\rm\; K\rm\; km\rm\; s^{-1}\rm\; pc^{2}\,$})^{-1}$"]
- result: X_CO = 1.04e20 ± not stated cm^-2 (K km/s)^-1 [Discussion: "$X_{\rm CO}=1.04\ensuremath{\times 10^{20}}\hbox{$\rm\; cm^{-2}\,$}(\hbox{$\rm\; K\rm\; km\rm\; s^{-1}\,$})^{-1}$"]
- result: r31 (13CO-bright component) = 0.71 ± 0.15 dimensionless [CO (3-2)/(1-0): "The resulting $r_{31}$ line ratio is $0.71\pm0.15$ for the $^{13}$CO-bright velocity component"]
- result: R32 (13CO-bright, 13CO-tracing region) = 3.32 ± 0.66 dimensionless [Table 1: "$3.32\pm0.66$"]
- result: 13CO column density = 1.74e17 ± not stated cm^-2 [CO-to-H$_2$ Conversion Factor: "the total $^{13}$CO column density is $1.74\ensuremath{\times 10^{17}}\hbox{$\rm\; cm^{-2}\,$}$"]
- result: Total molecular gas mass with Galactic factor or alpha scaling = 1.1e10 ± not stated Msun [CO-to-H$_2$ Conversion Factor: "the total CO(1-0) luminosity gives a total molecular gas mass of $1.1\ensuremath{\times 10^{10}}\hbox{$\rm\thinspace M_{\odot}$}$"]
- limitation (authors): Mass traced is only a fraction of total; LTE and abundance assumptions are simplistic [CO-to-H$_2$ Conversion Factor: "As a result, this LTE analysis is only sensitive to a fraction of the total molecular gas mass."]
- not stated (card): Number of tclean/clean iterations, threshold and masks; Line-free channels used for uvcontsub and continuum fit order; Flux calibrator and flux scale uncertainty; Pipeline version; Image size and cell size; Exact extraction polygon for the region tracing 13CO; Details of the Monte Carlo and Gaussian fitting method (fitter, errors); Whether the 2011/2012 archival projects were used and how

### 2020NatAs...4..861C: Unusually high CO abundance of the first active interstellar comet (2020; solar_system, astrochemistry; observational_alma_primary)
- question: Parent volatile composition of 2I/Borisov and implications for its natal environment.
- step 5: Construct molecular excitation models
  - method: Modified cometary LIME: three-dimensional NLTE radiative transfer with molecular and electron collisions and solar pumping; use LTE for CO. [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "using a modified version of the LIne Modeling Engine (LIME)(cite: bri10), adapted for cometary comae as described by Cordiner et al.(cite: cor19)."] [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "This three-dimensional, non-local-thermodynamic-equilibrium (NLTE) radiative transfer model incorporates a calculation of the rotational energy level populations resulting from collisions with gas-phase molecules and electrons, as well as pumping by solar radiation."]
  - software: LIME
  - parameter: Scale radiative pumping and photodissociation rates to r_H = 2.01 au. [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "Radiative pumping and photodissociation rates(cite: hue15) were appropriately scaled for the comet’s heliocentric distance ($r_H=2.01$ au)."]
  - assumption: HCN-H2O and HCN-CO collisions represented by H2 collisional rates. [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "Collisional rates for HCN with H$_2$O and CO were taken from the H$_2$ collisional rates in the Leiden Atomic and Molecular Database (cite: sch05)."]
  - assumption: Nominal electron density scaling x_ne = 1.0; kinetic temperature 50 K. [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "Our nominal model assumed a standard electron density scaling factor of $x_{n_e}=1.0$ and kinetic temperature $T_{kin}=$ 50 K to obtain the results given in Table 1"]
  - validation: Scaling H2 collision rates by 0.1-10 changed HCN production rates by only 2%. [Radiative Transfer Modeling: "The impact of this assumption was tested by scaling the H$_2$ collision rates by a factor of 0.1-10, but the resulting HCN production rates changed by only 2%."]
- limitation (authors): Detailed coma morphology largely hidden by noise. [Unusually High CO Abundance of the Firs…: "Our HCN and CO detections are robust, but the detailed coma morphology in Figure 1 is largely hidden by noise"]
- limitation (authors): HCN signal-to-noise cannot distinguish a jet from isotropic outgassing. [Unusually High CO Abundance of the Firs…: "However, the signal-to-noise is too low to conclusively distinguish this scenario from isotropic outgassing."]
- limitation (authors): Interferometric observations filter emission on angular scales ≳10''. [Unusually High CO Abundance of the Firs…: "because they include flux from angular scales $\gtrsim10''$, which was filtered out by the interferometer due to a lack of very short baselines in the telescope array."]
- not stated (card): The paper's program identifier is printed as 2019.01008.T; an unambiguous archive identifier is needed.; Spectral-window bandwidths, channel selections, and the construction of the combined continuum image.; Image cell size, image dimensions, exact spectral regridding, and primary-beam correction treatment.; Exact line-free channels, polynomial orders, and interpolation interval for the autocorre…

### 2015ApJ...798..124R: ALMA Observations of the Debris Disk around the Young Solar Analog HD 107146 (2015; debris_disks; observational_alma_primary)
- question: What is the radial distribution of mm-sized dust in the HD 107146 debris disk, and is there a gap or dip in the dust surface density?
- step 6: CO upper limit and planet mass from gap width
  - method: LTE CO column/mass upper limit from non-detection; chaotic/crossing zone relation for planet mass using gap width [7 The non-detection of CO: "we derived a $3\sigma$ upper limit for the mass of gaseous CO of $M_{\rm{CO}} < 1.9 \times 10^{-6}~M_{\oplus}$"]
  - parameter: Tex = 50 K, FWHM 1.9 km/s, inclination ~20 deg [7 The non-detection of CO: "uniform excitation temperature of 50 K throughout the disk"]
  - assumption: LTE [7 The non-detection of CO: "after assuming LTE"]
- result: CO mass 3σ upper limit (Tex=50 K) = 1.9e-6 ± not stated Earth masses [7 The non-detection of CO: "$M_{\rm{CO}} < 1.9 \times 10^{-6}~M_{\oplus}$"]
- result: Planet mass from gap, crossing zone (coefficient 2.1) = 6.1 ± +2.1/-3.1 Earth masses [9.1 A gap in the disk?: "We obtain $6.1^{+2.1}_{-3.1}~M_{\oplus}$ and $4.1^{+1.4}_{-2.1}~M_{\oplus}$"]
- limitation (authors): Data cannot distinguish between gap and double power-law forms; dust opacity degenerate with surface density [5.3 Double power-law: "The ALMA data presented in this paper do not allow us to distinguish between these possible different functional forms"]
- not stated (card): Number of emcee walkers and iterations (only 'several hundreds') and burn-in; Priors and bounds on model parameters; Clean threshold, mask, cell and image size of the CLEAN images; Whether CASA clean or other tools were used for the visibility extraction and weight rescaling in detail; Which spectral windows/channels were used for continuum (line-free channels excluded CO); Exact dust opacity tab…
